# Jeebsland project notes (the full history)

Moved out of CLAUDE.md on 2026-11-15 so every session reads less. CLAUDE.md keeps the standing rules and the current state; this file keeps the detail of every system and batch. Search it (Grep) for the system you are working on before changing it.


Unreal Engine 5.8 C++ project (Windows). Game code lives in `Source/Jeebsland/`. Most of the world (forest, village, castle, pond, etc.) is built procedurally in C++ actors rather than placed assets.

- Default game mode: `KnightGameMode` (set in `Config/DefaultEngine.ini`); the world is built by `KnightGameState`. Map: `/Game/Field`.
- The UE template code and content (Variant_*, Characters, ThirdPerson, LevelPrototyping, template game mode / controller) were deleted 2026-10-12. `AJeebslandCharacter` stays: it is the knight's base class (camera boom and camera). StateTree plugins are off.
- `Game Updates.txt` is the owner's private notes. Don't read or act on it; requests come through chat.

## Art direction (owner, 2026-11-05; applies to every design choice)

This is permanent and goes into every design from now on.

- **World, weapons and items:** a Breath of the Wild meets Overwatch look. Simple, but smooth and detailed. Focus on good colours and textures, not realism, but still pretty and natural. Think of how the forests of Fortnite look, the medieval towns in Overwatch and the landscape of Breath of the Wild.
- **Items** (weapons, tools, potions and so on): a mash-up of Breath of the Wild, Overwatch, Fortnite, Skyrim and RuneScape, slightly cartoonish, an HD RuneScape feel. Good detail, smooth, not too small. In practice (owner, same day, after seeing both ends and sending reference pictures): "not quite realistic but a tiny bit cartoonish". Sleek, natural proportions only a little bolder than real life, crisp bevels, hand-painted textures, rich clean colours, polished wood and dark steel, no rust or dirt. Too realistic was the first thin, weathered set; too cartoonish was fat, toy-like, banana-shaped forms. Wood is rich brown with just a touch of painted grain and a little natural variance: not yellow or orange, no drastic colour change along it. **`Docs/ItemStyleGuide.md` is the recipe (the process, Meshy settings, prompt wording, proportions, and the approved pictures in `Docs/ItemStyle/`): read it before designing any item, so the balance between cartoonish and realistic never has to be found again.**
- **Armour** is the exception: it may lean more towards realism.
- **Grass** is the second exception (owner, 2026-11-15: "I'd like to lean towards realism with the grass"): natural, photo-real blades rather than painted strokes. Candidate: Project Nature's free "temperate Vegetation" grass library and meadow flowers on Fab (each blade its own mesh); see `Docs/TownLab/AssetScout.md`.

Say this in every Meshy prompt and check every new model, material and building against it.

## Model and effort

The owner wants a recommendation on model and effort for each request, to balance quality against token use. Before executing any large task (or a batch of tasks), always suggest the most optimal model and effort first. "Optimal" means balancing token usage while still getting a great result that won't need rework. For a mixed batch, say which items suit which model. For small requests, one line is enough, and only if it differs from the current setting. Don't switch unless asked.
- Sonnet 5.5, low or medium effort: tweaks to values, colors, sizes or positions; removing things; small single-file changes.
- Sonnet 5.5, high effort: moderate features or multi-file changes along existing patterns.
- Opus 5.5, high effort: new systems (skills UI, enemies, combat, dungeons), tricky visual or rendering bugs, anything Sonnet failed at.

## Ending a session

When the owner says goodbye, goodnight or anything similar, don't just sign off: first ask if they'd like you to commit and refresh the "Current state" notes below for their next chat. If they say yes, update that section (what was just done, what's unfinished, known issues, decisions, anything they want carried over), remove stale lines, keep it to about a screen, and commit. In the same question, also ask whether to repackage the game (only if code or content changed since the last package; then make `Packaged/Jeebsland.zip` without the `.pdb`, and remind them to restart the worlds with `Tools/HostWorlds.bat` and send friends the new zip).

- **Owner rules for buildings (2026-11-11):**
  - Triangle budget: about 300k per building design by default. Small buildings can go to about 150k; don't go below that, since the house frayed under it. Copies share one model.
  - A building model is only the shell. Lanterns, torches, flower boxes and signs are designed separately and added after, and they can carry real lights.
  - Delete the full-size originals once a model is reduced and checked; the Meshy link is enough to download it again.
  - Interiors: shared rooms elsewhere in the same world, reached through a door that the server moves you through.- MoveLab's sun runs 3x (`ADayNight::ClockSpeed`; the world stays at 1). New switch `-ShotAt=X,Y` (use it, `-ShotPos` doesn't move him); log JEEBSUN gives the phase at a still's end. Stills quit at 11 s, so a long `-ShotSeconds` film gets cut off there.

**Knight batch of 2026-11-11/12 (committed; the owner has not seen it, not tried online with two machines or in a package):**
- Swing 2 is Mixamo's Sword And Shield Slash (`SlashCross`) played as it is, a rising cut (owner: "just use the original clip"); all the bending (LevelCut on key 31, the borrowed lunge) is gone. Ocarina-of-Time pacing: Tuning.ini [Jab] `Jab2Share` 0.65, a hold after each swing (`Jab1Pause` 0.2, `Jab2Pause` 0.28, `JabPause()`; ServerAttack refuses a swing in the pause with 0.15 s slack), `Jab2Reach` = `Jab1Reach` 34. Measured: swing 1 0.27 s + 0.11 s hold, swing 2 0.36 s + 0.15 s hold, reach 96% of swing 1. Test `-ShotMove=jab3`. Motion (Opus) and visual reviewers passed. For the owner to judge: he stands taller in swing 2 with a narrow stance, the cut ends high on his right, the left boot pivots back 25 cm in the wind-up and the right resets in swing 1 (a forward body carry in the wind-up would add about 20 cm of reach), the shield hangs low edge-on in the hold, the guard's blade is 8 cm from the head.

The crossbow is held in both hands everywhere (`CrossLeftHand`, `LeftOnCross` turned so the stock runs through the left fist); the longbow's arrow sits on the fist (`BowRestDrop`). Visual reviewer: gauntlet fists and relaxed hands pass; the BARE mail fingertips look faceted (low-poly Meshy hand; smooth normals on the hand area, or more fingertip geometry in `knight_fist.py`, are the fixes). Its last round could not tell the new wrap from the old in game pictures: look closely.

A plain click no longer selects, drinks or cycles there; keys 1, 2, R, Q, F still do. Test `-ShotPanel -ShotSwap=<0 hand,1 shield,2 bow,3 arrows,4 potion>`.

- Meshy models: `Art/Props` -> `Tools/BuildProps.ps1` (editor closed; `prop_clean.py`, every fit number in PROPS; `prop_import.py`) -> `/Game/Props` (`JeebProps.h`). `M_Prop` tints what the vertex colour marks. Style recipe: `Docs/ItemStyleGuide.md`. Pictures of a prop must be judged in the game, in sun and in shade: until 2026-11-06 `M_Prop` failed to compile and every prop showed flat grey, unnoticed, because of a picture slot with no default. Wood faces are plain wood in the metal/roughness picture too (2026-11-16): Meshy had painted the bow's pinstripes and the longbow's whole stave as polished metal, so those parts mirrored the dark surroundings (the sun gives no shine) as black holes and blotches once the colour was evened to brown. `BuildProps.ps1 Bow Longbow` now imports only the props named (`JEEB_PROPS`); `despeck` lifts painted rust specks (the bow). **The longbow's holes (2026-11-16, third pass):** two real causes, neither the metal picture. (1) The model was reduced from 34,000 to 3,000 triangles without a bake, across Meshy's hundreds of small UV islands: its colour and normal pictures shattered into scaly shards with dark pits all along the stave. It is now baked (`bake=True`, 5,000 triangles, fresh UVs): smooth polished wood. (2) Cutting the model's string away left an open, ragged crater in each tip where it was tied on, the inside showing: `fill=True` closes holes once the model is welded (134 open edges, none left). `swatch=True` paints a steel-grey square into an empty corner of a baked picture, since a model with no steel of its own gave the added bands a black one to take their colour from. A re-made model can come in with a second material slot (the old `Material_0` beside the new `Baked`), its faces on the second: `prop_import.py` now gives every slot the prop's material and `JeebProps::Tint` tints every slot, or the prop draws in the engine's plain grey with no tier colour. Run `prop_clean.py` with absolute folders (as `BuildProps.ps1` does): with relative ones Blender writes the model but silently not the pictures. A `pythonscript` import started while another copy is compiling hangs loading TurnkeySupport: stop it and start it again.
- Last review round: a boot dragged across the floor in a swing's recovery or rising from a slide now lifts (`LiftDraggedFeet`); a move's legs mode changes gradually (`LegsW`), legs and hips can blend slower than the arm (`StartBlend`'s LegSeconds); the slide's speed runs out while he rises (`SlideGlide`); the low sweep's and `KeepTipUp`'s heading across the floor is rate limited (no flash when the blade passes vertical); the raised shield's forearm lies across behind it (`GuardForeTurn`); the off arm stays mostly with the stance in the smash and sweep. The potion's neck is stretched in `prop_clean.py` (`neck`), held by the neck at the mouth. Not passed by the visual reviewer, owner to judge: while drinking it still reads partly as a round ball. A taller bottle from Meshy is the likely fix if they want one (about 19 credits). Others don't see the fishing cast (`bFishing` isn't replicated; `RepMoveFlags` bit 128 is free).

**The suit's model (2026-11-04/05):** the owner makes each piece separately in Meshy. `Art/Knight Classic` holds the suit's pieces; `Art/Knight Chainmail` the slim bare knight and his rigged version (24-bone Mixamo skeleton). To rebuild: `Tools/BuildNewSuit.ps1` (cleans and fits every piece with `Tools/knight_piece_clean.py`; every fit number lives in that script), then `Tools/ImportNewSuit.ps1` with the editor closed (`knight_suit_export.py` writes `Saved/KnightBuild/Suit`, `knight_suit_import.py` makes `/Game/NewSuit`, wiping it, so then run `Tools/ImportKnightClips.ps1` and `Tools/ImportBareKnight.ps1`). Fitted to the owner's reference pictures (local copies in `Saved/KnightBuild/RefPictures`, not in git). The shoulder's dome is never sheared to tilt it (it stops being round). The arms are the owner's arm harness (still named `gauntlets` in the tools, `fit=arm`, numbers `A_*`). Limits of the skeleton, not fixed by fitting: arms about 8 cm longer than the pictures, knee joint about 9 cm lower. Mail never shows through armour (`Tools/knight_suit_mail.py`: the suit's own mail under plate is deleted), and since 2026-11-06 a whole mail body is always under the plate so nothing looks hollow (owner): `/Game/NewSuit/SK_NewSuitUnder` (`Tools/knight_under_export.py`, run by `ImportBareKnight.ps1`: the bare body without hands and feet, 6,000 triangles, pulled in 1.5 cm, each vertex with the bone weights of its nearest plate), a leader-pose follower in `MakeMesh`, with `M_SuitUnder` pushing it 4 cm from the camera as it draws. Check with `-ShotUnderRed`: red only in real gaps. Thighs (2026-11-17, owner: mail through the front of the thighs in the bow draw): the leg plates hug the thigh up to about 5 cm closer than his body, so the 1.5 cm inset left the mail outside the cuisse round the inner, front and back of the thigh, seen whenever the draw's stance turned a thigh to the camera; with the mail pushed 20 m away (a test edit of M_SuitUnder's push constant) every red patch there was solid plate, not a gap. Now every leg point is pulled in toward its bone until it is 1.2 cm inside the leg plate (`LEG_MARGIN`), hip points go 0.8 cm inside the suit's dark cloth under the skirt (`PELVIS_MARGIN`) so the waist gap between the tassets shows that cloth, and leg points take their weights from the leg armour, not a tasset. Close stills of a held draw from eight sides: `-ShotMove=bow4000 -ShotEyes=0_0_95_0_-4_0.1_1024_-1_<turn>_150+... -ShotEyesGap=3 -ShotFov=34` (a gap under 3 s drops pictures when the machine is busy). About 50k triangles, no LOD yet. In moving test pictures at the game's 60% render scale, fast edges look frayed; that is the upscaler (at `-ExecCmds="r.ScreenPercentage 100"` they are clean). Metal is lifted against Tiers.csv (`MetalScale` x 0.75, Desat 1, gain 2.3): at full metal the suit reflects dark surroundings.

**Polished plate (owner 2026-11-15: "very shiny ... reflective like a mirror"):** `M_KnightSkin` (graph listed by `Tools/dump_material.py`; nothing in it changed): BaseColor = lerp(near-black rim, desaturate(BaseTex, Desat*M) x lerp(1, DesatGain, Desat*M) x lerp(1, Tint, M), facing), where M = saturate(MRTex x 1.4) (about 1 on all plate) and "facing" = saturate(dot(vertex normal, camera) x 6 + 0.5) darkens the silhouette; Metallic = M x MetalScale x facing; Roughness = lerp(max(MRTex, MinRough), TierRough, RoughMix x M); Specular unset; flat normal. The matte look was not the material: the world's sun has SpecularScale 0 (no glare on grass), so pure metal got no highlight, and the sky light's lower hemisphere is the sky atmosphere's near-black planet ground (`bLowerHemisphereIsBlack` false, real-time capture), so plate facing down mirrored black (his back from the play camera). Fix: `ApplyPlateLook` (KnightAnimator.cpp) for metal tiers sets MetalScale `jeeb.Armour.Metal` 1, TierRough `jeeb.Armour.Rough` 0.14, RoughMix 0.95, DesatGain 2.3 x `jeeb.Armour.Shine` 2.6; `ADayNight` adds a glint sun (`Glint`: a second directional light, DiffuseScale 0, no shadows, lighting channel 1 only, following the sun; the suit mesh is on channels 0+1; `jeeb.Armour.Glint` 1; the real sun has ForwardShadingPriority 1 so water and fog keep using it) and `jeeb.Sky.Ground` 0.5 puts a meadow colour (0.11, 0.13, 0.07 at 1) below the sky light's horizon (shaded walls get a touch lighter and greener from below). `jeeb.Armour.Rough -1` is the whole old look. Metal below 1 looked chalky white; Ground 1 and 1.8 looked alike (a flat pale olive back); 0.5 keeps a darker, more mirror-like back. The glint cost nothing measurable (GPU 6.29 ms with, 6.39 without, one non-solo run). Quiver and flask: `jeeb.Knight.Quiver`, `jeeb.Knight.Flask` (QuiverWorld, BeltFlaskWorld). **The resting bow (2026-11-16, third pass; owner's drawing from behind: "the string between his wrist/forearm and his upper left hip/abs"):** the bow hangs level in the closed left fist, limbs fore and aft, with the string ABOVE the fist, up the inside of the forearm and tipped 15 degrees in toward his body (`BowRestQuatFor`, KnightAnimator.cpp): at wrist height, about 10 cm in from the forearm's line, between the gauntlet and the hip plates. The earlier tries turned the bow about his forward axis (`BowRestRoll` -85/-110), which laid the string level or below the fist against the thigh plates and needed the whole arm 20 degrees out. Three things make the new carry hold in every clip: (1) `BowFistTurn`: the bow is fixed in the fist, so the walk's turned-back palms swung the string through his hip; now the left wrist is turned each frame until the bow lies as `BowRestQuatFor` says for the forearm as it is (the fist turns with the bow, so it stays closed on it). (2) The limbs stay level and lean only `jeeb.Knight.BowRestFollow` 0.2 of the way with the forearm: square to a forearm swung forward they stood on end and the string's back end went down through the thigh. (3) `BowArmOut` no longer opens the arm a fixed angle: it keeps the fist at least `jeeb.Knight.BowFistOut` 40 cm out from his middle (standing about 2 degrees more than `HandsOffThighs` gives, 8-10 in all; walking up to 22 at the top of the forward swing, where the clip brings the fist in to 22 cm). Live cvars: `jeeb.Knight.BowRestTilt` 15, `BowRestTip` 0 (front limb up), `BowRestSplay` -6 (back tip in toward his body, so from behind the whole string shows between forearm and hip), `BowRestFollow` 0.2, `BowFistOut` 40, `BowArmBend` 6, `BowFistTurn` 1. JEEBBOWGAP (string against capsules on the bones, cm): bow standing hip +7.9 / forearm +5.4, walking +3.2 to +5.0 / +3.9; longbow standing +9.3 / +6.0, walking 0 to +2.4 / +3.7; running about -2 to -3 (was -12.7: the fist pumps at chest height close to the ribs; not fixed). Note the grip is 12 cm past the wrist bone and 6 cm toward the palm (`GripInLeftHand`), which is why a string 16.5 cm up the forearm from the grip sits at wrist height. `jeeb.Knight.BowRestRoll`, `BowRestOut` and `BowArmOut` are gone.

**Not yet seen working (2026-11-06):** a real ladder and a real seat (the films play those clips on the spot, unstowed), real hits between two players (the live test covered a server and two logins only), a cooked server, the mail body in a package, the new bows, drinking and the popup online with two machines. No frame rate number exists for the new knight in a package: the editor run gave about 8.8 ms GPU at 1280x720, with the frame capped near 60, so no real game-thread cost. Before friends ever play, play the new package and compare to 140 HIGH / 175 LOW. A LOD for the 50k suit is the likely first cost with many players. The under-mail costs about 0.14 ms in MoveLab with a dozen knights (173 fps against 177, editor -game, one run each).

**START HERE next session:** first ask how the 2026-11-13/14 batch looks (its "Owner to judge" list) and which way the storybook cottage goes. Then how the 2026-11-12/13 batch looks:
1. The ground, grass and roads; the far trees; the grade and haze; the smoke and glow.
2. Climbing on MoveLab's rock.
3. The new weapons.
4. Their picks from `Docs/Filler/` (then plan where they go).
5. The animal plan's questions.

Oak Hill is built (2026-11-12, see "Oak Hill is built" above): ask the owner how it looks (walk over the river from Blackrock), whether to keep the 300k buildings, and what to build next there (decorations, boxing ring, bridge, forts). Its plan is `Docs/OakHillPlan.md`; `Docs/TownBuildings.md` is the checklist of every town's seven required buildings plus its unique ones. New buildings come from Meshy's free library first (`Docs/MeshyCommunityLibrary.md`). Anchorage's bank is made and reduced, not in the game. After Oak Hill, the mountains are the next terrain job. Ask how the 2026-11-11/12 knight batch looks in MoveLab: swing 2 and the three-swing combo, the fists and relaxed hands, the crossbow in both hands, the helmet's eyes, Tin and Diamond, the props' metal, the battle axe and pickaxe with a shield. Then ask how the 2026-11-07 batch looks: the flat second swing against their red line, the charge going only backwards, the big sword (its hands and second cut), 10 swings on a rock with room in the pack, where the sweep's trail starts (`SweepTrailFrom`), the wall heights, the shield over the hand. Then how the batch before it feels: the combined swing and charge, the shield on the forearm, the mail under the armour, the crossbow's buttons and how far it leans (and ask for the lean picture that didn't arrive), the axe and pickaxe as weapons, the chop's hands and the pick on the rock. Also the shield health numbers and the walk to the tree. Then the earlier batch: the Meshy pickaxe, longbow, crossbow and potion in their real colours (they were grey until the last hour of the session), the belt flask, the swap popup, chopping and mining, and the crossbow's reload. Also ask how the knight feels: the run, the jump, the sword's fit, the guard, the boxes (H in MoveLab), other weapons. Making more weapons in Meshy is roadmap item 17. Most timing is `Content/Data/KnightMoves.csv` with no build. Then the things in `Docs/ToRecreate.md`, if and when they want them. Then remind the owner of the build order at the bottom of `Docs/Roadmap.md` and ask which to begin, with the model/effort recommendation. Likely next: the jumping lunge; the river's stone bridges and Oak Hill; the quest system; PvP (players' moves hit other players only in MoveLab until then). "Ask before starting each step." Keep the roadmap current.

**Decisions and preferences:**
- Every sword move with hit boxes leaves the white trail (cuts, the low sweep, the dash's stab as a streak behind the point; not the kick): `TickSwordTrail`.
- Weapon "ghosts" (2026-11-16, owner: the pickaxe "appears in multiple places", a pale copy of its head behind it): not TAA/TSR. Films of a swing over grass from behind (`-ShotMove=jab -ShotKind=2`, 120 fps, `-ShotEvery=1`) showed a long pale line hanging in the air where the weapon had been for about 0.08 s, with the sword too; it stayed with `r.AntiAliasingMethod 0`, went with `ShowFlag.Translucency 0`, and `ShowFlag.VisualizeTemporalUpscaler` showed it in Translucency.AfterDOF. It was the swing trail ribbon seen edge-on: the first swing runs in a plane going away from a camera behind him, so the ribbon (a band along the blade swept across the blade; the log's new `across` speed showed the tip really sweeping sideways) shows as a thin bright line along the path, like a pale haft. The held weapons, shield and bow draw correct velocity (opaque, moved by SetWorldTransform, nothing re-registered per frame). Fix (`TickSwordTrail`): each part of the ribbon fades as it turns edge-on to this machine's camera (Keep = SmoothStep(0.15, 0.45, |normal . view|)); sample strength uses the tip's speed across the blade. Every weapon that fights like a sword and every machine's view gets it (visual only). Test pictures: `-ShotEvery=1` now numbers every frame in order (it used to overwrite every other one).
- Water "ghosts" (2026-11-17, owner: the water "shows up multiple times in the same frame"): TSR history, not translucency or reflections. Films (`-ShotEyesMove=15_150_0 -ShotEyesFilm=2.2_6`, river at -2000,10600 and the pond, each eye with its own 9th-field commands) showed the HIGH water as a soft smear of several faint copies of its ripples, glints and fishing rings, moving camera or still; with `r.AntiAliasingMethod 0` the ripples were crisp, and `r.Water.SingleLayer.SSRTAA 0` or `ShowFlag.Translucency 0` left the smear as it was. The ripples drift by Time with no motion vector, so TSR kept old ripples (its anti-flicker and thin-detail history keeping). Fix: M_Water has `Has Pixel Animation` (Tools/water_material.py) and `r.Water.SingleLayer.VelocityOutputPass=2` (DefaultEngine.ini), without which the flag never reached TSR (mode 1 writes no velocity for still water). No measurable GPU cost (solo, river and pond, moving camera: 6.53/6.18 and 8.05/7.84 ms before, 6.57/6.38 and 7.65/8.02 after). LOW uses TAA and is unchanged. Test switches: `-ShotEyesMove=<yaw deg/s>_<sideways cm/s>_<forward cm/s>`, `-ShotEyesFilm=<from s>_<frames>` (`<ShotName>_<eye>_<NNN>.png`).
- River "blinking a little" (2026-11-21, owner's picture of grainy glitter on the river; investigated, NOT fixed): still-camera 120 fps films of the river by the bridge (eye -2500_10600_300_90_-10_0.1), scored as the mean of |L(t) - (L(t-1)+L(t+1))/2| over the water (one-frame blinks, not drift): water about 0.9 levels against 0.38 on the town and grass in the same frames. `r.Water.SingleLayer.Reflection 0` drops the water to 0.20, so it is the reflections. Nothing else tried moved it beyond run-to-run noise (about ±0.2; compare eyes within one run only): specular anti-aliasing in M_Water (roughness widened by the normal's change across a pixel; the ripples are 2-4 m across, never finer than a pixel, so it changed nothing and was taken out again), `r.SSR.Quality` 0/1/4, `r.Water.SingleLayer.SSRTAA` 0/1, `jeeb.Water.Rough` 0.02/0.08/0.1, `jeeb.Water.Ripple` 0.5, `r.ScreenPercentage 100`, `r.TSR.ShadingRejection.Flickering 0`, TAA, `r.ReflectionMethod 0` (worse: hard dithered sky-reflection edges), and M_Water rebuilt without Has Pixel Animation (0.65, inside the noise). Zoomed frames show the grain inside the reflected bank and grass and at the edges of the bright sky patches. Untried next: the reflection's own detail (the bank grass mirrored by SSR is the grain), a slight blur of the water's reflection, or `jeeb.Water.Spec` lower. Tools (in the agent copy, Saved/wscripts): flicker.ps1, blinkimg.ps1. `Tools/BuildWater.ps1` now passes `-LiveCoding=false` (without it the windowless editor waited 8+ minutes for other copies' builds).
- Grass on the drawn ground (2026-11-21, owner's picture: grass and knight "floating" beside a sunken dirt road by the river): the meadow (Meadow.cpp) placed every clump at `JeebTerrain::HeightAt`, but outside the square and the towns the ground is drawn as the world's 8 m triangles (`WorldMeshHeight`), which on the cut forest roads and river banks lie up to 1.5 m off HeightAt. `-FloatScan` (JEEBFLOAT; Meadow.cpp) traces down along every road and both banks of every river (54,527 spots, 7 m either side): grass more than 8 cm off the solid ground at 21,487 (mean 26.6 cm, worst 157 cm; within 500 m of Oak Hill 7,469 of 26,164, mean 25.3); the solid ground matched `DrawnHeight` within 4 cm everywhere but 154 spots (Oak Hill's 1 m lawn at pad edges and the bridge embankment, worst 39 cm), so the knight stood on the drawn ground. Fix: the grass, its flowers and our cards stand on `JeebTerrain::DrawnHeightWith` (Terrain.h, DrawnHeight with the grid-point heights cached per square, AMeadow::GroundAt). Later the same day the forest trees (OakForest.cpp; its undergrowth already used its own drawn-height grid), AutumnDecor (Ground(), post torches) and the fill (GroundUnder, posts, panels) moved to DrawnHeight too; `-FloatScan` logs `JEEBFLOAT placed <class>` (its "off by over 8 cm" there is against each mesh's median sink, which varies on purpose by scale and slope, so read only its "at HeightAt it would be" count: forest 537 of 7816, decor 76 of 21096, fill 26 of 1052 that were off by over 8 cm before). Hills.cpp now samples at the 8 m points too (agent/hills), which made HeightAt and the drawn ground agree more; DrawnHeight is built from HeightAt at the grid points, so there is no double correction.
- Grass filling in (2026-11-21, owner: "make the grass growing in less obvious while moving ... a few phases that fill in in steps"): `jeeb.Grass.OldSteps 1` is the old way for comparing. Old: three steps by the square's middle (17 and 32 m; in play they swapped at about 21-24 and 37-39 m because the build looks 1.5 s ahead), each a fresh random crop (the seed had the step in it), the realistic share 1, 0.47 and 0.09 of the spots: a square 37 m off went from 9% to 47% in one frame, every clump in a new place. Now: six steps (12, 17, 24, 32, 41 m), shares 1, 0.75, 0.5, 0.3, 0.16, 0.09 (FarThin scales the last two as before), widened by share^-0.45 so the cover stays, every kind within 24 m, two to 32, one beyond (a spot keeps its kind's place), flowers to 32 m; each spot draws from its own stream (the square's seed and the spot's number), with a fixed rank, so a nearer step only adds clumps among those already there. `-GrassStepLog` logs each change (JEEBGRASS step).
- Grass reach stretched (2026-11-22, owner: the six steps "about twice the distance in every direction on HIGH, the outer steps very sparse"): `jeeb.Grass.Stretch` S (Meadow.cpp, live, regrows round you) multiplies the reach (50 m), the sinking edge (`jeeb.Grass.PackFade`, its 8 m fade too) and each step edge past the first by S^(step/4) (the 12 m step stays, the 41 m edge goes the whole way); the outermost step's share is divided by S. Same nesting, nothing re-rolls. ApplyGraphics sets HIGH 1.5 (75 m), LOW 1. Solo, open land, walking, GPU: S 1 8.4 ms, 1.5 8.9, 2 9.3 (+0.9 was judged too much for about +0.5 more look; `jeeb.Grass.Stretch 2` to try it).
- Props sunk on slopes (2026-11-22, owner's picture of tulip beds buried at one end): Fill stood every piece at its footprint's lowest corner. A ground piece under 220 cm, a metre or more long, not a lamp, now leans with the drawn ground's plane (Fill.cpp PlaceSet; `-FillNoLean` the old way). `-FloatScan`'s `JEEBFLOAT ends <mesh>` counts pieces with a bottom corner over 10 cm off the ground: tulip beds 4 of 22 -> 0, raised beds 2 -> 0, hand carts 3 of 4 (44 cm) -> 1 (11), log piles, stone benches, mossy logs, firewood to 0. Barrels, stalls and lamp posts still stand on their lowest corner (upright on purpose).
- Lamp posts lit up whole (2026-11-22, owner: "why does this whole post light up instead of just the lantern?"): each Fill lamp post's light sat on the post's axis 35 cm under its top, inside the post and crossbar, shadowless. Now it is a spot pointing down from the middle of the lantern's glass (each mesh's Glow slot, measured by `-GlowLog`: gallows -72 0 186, pillar -36 0 239, stone -28 0 177; the well's point light -125 -11 187), 40 degrees each way, so the crossbar and the post beside the lantern stay dark; the gallows post's lower half and its stone foot still catch the cone. LampPostRope keeps the old point light (not measured). Checked the others: TownLab lanterns (+25 cm, the glass's middle), bridge lanterns, camp fires and torches already sit in their glow; the glow materials are only on each model's Glow slot.
- Sword and arrow trails follow the sunlight (`ADayNight::TrailLight` multiplies their Tint each frame: white at noon, about 40% and soft gold at the low sun; owner 2026-11-01).
- Movement (owner, 2026-11-01): walking 5 m/s (`WalkSpeed` 500); starting to walk is instant (`WalkAccel`, set each frame at the end of the speed code in KnightCharacterMovement.cpp), walk to sprint keeps its ramp (`SprintAccel`). Arrows do NOT carry the knight's speed: they go to the reticle. HIGH graphics uses TSR (LOW keeps TAA), render scale still 60%.
- Icon pictures are drawn with an anti-aliased fill (`AKnightHUD::Poly`): keep using it for any new icon.
- HUD text must go through `HDText` / `HDTextSize` (sharp, size-matched font copies), never `DrawText` directly.
- The Escape screen (also O; in the editor's preview window Escape stops Play) is opaque dark grey and always opens on the MAP tab (Save, Back to menu), then Settings, Controls, Dictionary, Lore. Graphics: two levels, shown to players as LOW and HIGH (code: GraphicsLevel 0 and 1; renamed 2026-10-29 from Lowest/Low); ALWAYS start on HIGH at render scale 60% (slider 35-100%), not loaded from the saved config. Both use standard shadow maps (virtual shadows overloaded in the pines) with soft PCSS shadows; Lowest: medium shadow quality, no leaf shadows, TAA.
- World map: simple (land, woods, paths, water, Blackrock, castle, your arrow). Never show other players' positions (not on the minimap either), villagers or shops.
- Fancy lighting (Lumen) was REMOVED 2026-10-12 at the owner's request, for frame rate and download size; they may want it back later. To restore: in Config/DefaultEngine.ini set r.DynamicGlobalIlluminationMethod=1, r.ReflectionMethod=1, r.GenerateMeshDistanceFields=True (and r.RayTracing=True, r.Lumen.HardwareRayTracing=True, r.SkinCache.CompileShaders=1 for hardware ray tracing); then add a third level to `ApplyGraphics` (KnightCharacterUI.cpp) setting GI 1, reflections 1, VolumetricFog 1, and a FANCY button in the O screen. Commit 1969fec has the working three-level version.
- Conversation text: large; the words start at the left and wrap at about the middle of the screen; no "Press E" hint.
- UI look (2026-11-18): `jeeb.UI.Style` 1 walnut and brass (default), 2 midnight blue and gold, 0 the old "liquid glass" (`KnightHUDStyle.cpp`; `Panel`, `Tile`, `Gauge`, `Btn`, `CloseBtn` branch there). Use the shared helpers for every new menu or HUD element; never plain rectangles.
- Rings and amulets share three miscellaneous slots (any mix).
- Roll (2026-11-15, owner): only the mouse wheel DOWN, with a direction held (W forward, S back, A left, D right, two keys the diagonal, relative to the camera; none held: straight ahead); 1 s cooldown. The double-tap on A/D/W/S and V/Alt no longer roll. (`ScrollDown` / `RollWheel`, KnightCharacterMovement.cpp; test `-ShotMove=wheelroll -ShotRollKeys=<fwd>,<right>`, log JEEBROLL.)
- Low sweep ("down tilt"): shelved 2026-11-15, owner (the wheel down rolls now). The code (`StartSweep`), its move row and data stay so it can come back; nothing binds it.
- Crouch (Ctrl) is a clip overlay; it can be combined with the shield guard.
- Pines and redwoods: whorls of drooping bark branches with needle-bough cards (T_PineNeedles / M_PineNeedle); needle boughs cast no shadow (shadow cost). The owner liked the pines' colour, needles and fullness.
- (2026-11-22: the test horse is gone; real online horses, the Oak Hill stable, Bram's quest and Horse Riding replace it: Horse.h, HowToTest "Horses". Riding still uses the sitting pose lifted by `jeeb.Ride.Raise`.) Riding steers like a car (`AKnightCharacter::DoMove`): W forward, A/D turn on a very tight radius (~1.75 m), S backs up slowly (half the knight's walk, never sprints), no sideways movement. On foot there is no backing up in normal movement; with the shield up the knight is in a combat stance (faces the camera, moves at full walk speed in every direction since 2026-10-29, tower shield slower, no sprint). Holding the shield up drains the guard (full guard lasts `GuardDrainSeconds` = 15 s, KnightCharacter.cpp), no refill while raised. Riding speed (2026-11-16, owner "twice as fast"): `HorseMul` 3.2 x the knight's walk (1600 cm/s) and sprint (about 2350 cm/s) in `TickGroundSpeed`, the gallop's build-up 660 cm/s/s and easing off 1800; backing up still half the walk; the turn-rate cap (230 deg/s) is unchanged, so at speed the turning circle is wider (about 4 m trotting, 6 m galloping). The horse's legs canter, and from 13 m/s gallop (2026-11-26, a real gallop clip: HowToTest "Gallop") and the T call runs at up to 20.8 m/s (Horse.cpp). Dismount bug fixed 2026-11-16: `Mount` saved the live `bOrientRotationToMovement`, which strafe mode (on by default) had already switched off through `bBackOrient`; while riding `TickGroundSpeed` handed strafe's saved value back, and `Dismount` restored the "off" for good, so with strafe mode off after a ride the knight no longer turned to where he walked. Now `Mount` saves strafe's saved value when `bBackOrient` is set and clears `bBackOrient`, and `Dismount` clears it too, so strafe takes over cleanly next frame.
- Map fog (2026-10-29): `USkillComponent::Explored` (64x64 bit grid, 45 m reveal radius, server marks it every 0.5 s, saved, kept on death). World map draws a fog texture over it and hides names; minimap hides markers under the fog. Speed pass 2026-10-29: TAA, no AO, cached shadows, fewer cascades, Substrate off; packaged build ran 140 fps on HIGH, 175 on LOW (owner: adequate). Settings has a "Show the frame rate" checkbox (`AKnightHUD::DrawFps`). Window titles use `NorseTitle` (rune-cut lettering); normal text stays HDText.
- Village palisade wood uses `M_WoodV`/`M_WoodH` (painted grain texture T_Wood from gen_textures.py: logs use the mesh UVs, beams follow their long axis); other buildings still use flat colours. The first bark-blob wood looked fake: avoid contour-line patterns.
- Candles and carved pumpkins glow via `M_Flame` halos plus flickering point lights (see AutumnDecor.cpp); every 4th candle and every 2nd lit pumpkin has a real light.
- The original walled town is named BLACKROCK (lore text, and a banner when you walk in through the walls).
- Grass shading: darkest parts lifted by `GRASS_SHADOW_LIFT` in build_materials.py (lights untouched), almost-even normals for a fluffy look.
- Grass overhaul (2026-11-14, owner: like the reference pictures; the owner has not seen it yet):
  - One field colour (`Tools/grass_field.py`: patches, swathes, dry patches and one desaturation, the approved town lawn's recipe) is shared by the blades (`M_Grass`, worked out per tuft in the vertex shader), the world's ground (`M_Ground`) and Oak Hill's lawn (`M_TL_Ground`, `Tools/BuildTownLab.ps1`). Change it there, then rebuild all three. Flat ground alone gets `Lift`: the blades are lit through.
  - Ground under the grass (2026-11-15, owner: the ground "seems to glow" in shade at dusk): the cause was M_Ground's low-sun fill (SkyFill), an emissive term no shadow dims, so in tree shade at dusk the painted ground between the blades stayed lit while the blades went dark (with SkyFill 0 the glow is gone; `jeeb.Sky.Ground` made no visible difference). The fill now comes in only past the blades (FillFrom..FillTo: half of `jeeb.Grass.PackFade` to all of it), and among the blades the grass ground is times `jeeb.Ground.Floor` 0.9 at the low sun (1 at noon, eased in with Dusk); `jeeb.Ground.UnderFill` 1 is the old look. `AWorldTerrain::SetUnderGrass` sets all of it on M_Ground's shared copy and Oak Hill's lawn (M_TL_Ground) from the one setting `jeeb.Ground.SkyFill` (`jeeb.TownLab.GroundFill` removed: a LOOK slider moved one and not the other, a hard seam; the town also only updated when the sun moved). Shared nodes: `grass_field.beyond_blades`, `under_grass_floor`, `under_grass_fill`.
  - Grass colour line at Oak Hill's edge (2026-11-17, owner: "a line in the grass where it changes color ... not being applied to the sliders?"): Oak Hill's lawn (M_TL_Ground) had last been built 2026-11-15, so it had no FarTint (the LOOK "Ground past the grass" sliders reached the world's ground only) and missed the later ground changes: along the town box's straight edge the ground changed colour. Rebuilt; `Tools/BuildWorldMaterial.ps1` now builds M_Ground and then M_TL_Ground, so the two can't drift apart again. Test switch (non-Shipping): `jeeb.Test.HideTownGround 1` hides the town's ground mesh, showing exactly where it meets the world's.
  - `AMeadow`: dense grass within 50 m everywhere, in 15 m squares (about 35 draws), thinning past 17 m. Blades sink into the ground over `FadeStart`..`FadeEnd` (26-49 m, M_Grass) and lose a little contrast as they go, and past that the painted ground carries the look.
  - Blade tint (0.52, 0.46, 0.33), measured against Breath of the Wild's meadow (the old yellow 0.63, 0.44, 0.16 read lime at noon and hay at the low sun). At the low sun the blades cool (`Dusk`, set each frame on `AMeadow::GrassMid` from `ADayNight::Dusk`). The cut-out reads the picture two mips sharper (seen from above, the low mips made half-opaque squares).
  - Sporadic long-grass patches (`LongGrassNoise`/`LongGrassRoom` in Meadow.cpp: about 19% of the land, up to 2.3x the height, light tips through per-instance data 0). Never on or by roads, at buildings, under trees, on trampled ground or within 45 m of a plaza.
  - Small flower clumps (`MakeFlowerMesh`, `M_GrassFlower`, colour from per-instance data 0-2) within about 27 m. The old sphere flowers are gone.
  - Log JEEBGRASS (coverage; `-GrassNear=X_Y` finds a long patch and a flower clump).
- The wind (BotW-style soft gust field in `add_wind`, Tools/build_materials.py) is approved: keep it.
- Defence XP comes only from blows blocked with a raised shield (armour hits and hazards train nothing). Attack and Defence level quietly: no skill popup. Hazards (`Hurt(..., bEnvironmental=true)`) ignore armour and train nothing.
- Inventory drag and drop (`AKnightHUD::ProcessDrag`): drag between the 5x5 pack (24 slots + fixed ingot cell) and the weapon bar; items trade places; only weapons go on the bar; clicking empty screen leaves Tab pointer mode. `FillPracticePack()` is called from KnightCharacter::BeginPlay as a TEMPORARY stock of random gear: remove it when the owner is done practising.
- Three cats (`ACat`) roam near the town; `AFireflies` swarms drift in and out (60-120 s on, 60-120 s off). Tuning constants at the top of each header.
- `ASmokeCloud` (purple poison smoke test hazard) sits at the horse's old spot (650,420); delete the spawn line in KnightGameState.cpp when the owner is done with it.
- Health (knight and horse) heals only after 10 s without a hit, and a full bar takes 10 s. In PvP areas health must NOT regenerate (not built yet). The horse is meant to get its own hit boxes and take damage separately from the knight (`HurtHorse`); not built yet. The stamina bar is hidden on purpose but stamina still works.
- Look: soft, natural, "Breath of the Wild" feel; nothing pointy or jagged. Autumn palette, leaves a little brighter than before but not glowing.
- Day and night (2026-11-17): the sun sets. Noon 41 degrees, midnight 25 below (`jeeb.Sun.Low`); 20-minute loop, about 11.6 min of day and 8.4 of night. Was a midnight sun that never set (lowest 11).
- Armour can be taken off: an empty slot shows the chain mail body. New characters start in a full Light Tin suit.
- Capes are ordinary items (owner, 2026-11-03; the PvP-rank cape is gone): one per tier in that tier's colour (Tiers.csv CapeColor), just for looks, taken off, bought (store), sold, dropped and lost in the death sack. New characters and deaths get a Tin cape.
- Save versions: `UJeebSave::SaveVersion` defaults to 0 and Capture always writes it. Before 2026-10-30 it was never stored (Unreal leaves out values equal to the default), so old files get their version from the fog grid size (small = 1, full-size = 2) in `Migrate`. randy and tester_1 were still unconverted version 1 files; they convert on their next login.
- Tiers (2026-10-30): Tin, Iron, Bronze, Steel, Silver, Gold, then legendaries Onyx (knights), Emerald (trolls), Diamond (wizards) = JeebItems::ETier 0-8. Equip gates JeebItems::CanEquip (Attack/Archery/Magic for weapons, the gathering skill for axe/pickaxe/rod, Defence for armour and shields; levels 1/5/10/20/30/40/50) checked on the server in every equip path (EquipFromSlot, SwapInvHotbar, SwapInvWorn, ALootDrop::EquipTo). Recipes: tin ore, iron ore, copper+tin, iron+coal, silver ore, gold ore, gem+gold. Each mining rock holds one fixed ore (AMiningRock::SetOre, a shuffled fixed mix in KnightGameState). Save version 2 migrates old ids; backups of the pre-redo saves are in Saved/*_before_tier_redo_2026-10-30. 
- Minimap rotates with the camera. Store restocks hourly with a mystery satchel.
- Always keep grass near buildings (fort, cottages, castle, etc.) a bit shorter than open-field grass (about 22% shorter, the owner found 40% too short and the crossed cards showed), with wider overlapping tufts there to hide the cards, and taper the height gradually with distance from the building so it never reads as tall-then-suddenly-short. Apply this to any new building or grass work.
- Owner disliked: the Milky Way, streaky cirrus clouds, hay-coloured grass, flat-colour ground.

**Open ideas:** a thicker layer of ground leaves under trees if they still hide in the grass; cheaper cloud settings if needed; moving the model/effort preference to the user-level CLAUDE.md.

## Combat and UI systems (built 2026-10-11, Opus session; not yet seen in-game by the owner)

- **HUD windows** (`AKnightHUD::DrawSidePanel`, no tabs now): Inventory lower right (Settings toggle: always shown, or only with Tab); Tab frees the cursor and also shows Armor (above the inventory) and Skills (left side, only DISCOVERED skills: `USkillComponent::Known`, set on first XP; Attack and Defence known at the start; grows to 5 columns then more rows). Old tab notes: Caps Lock no longer matters. Right-click a stack or drag it onto the world to drop an amount (`DrawDropBox`), drops are `ALootDrop` bags. compact, dim smoky-grey glass (`bGreyGlass`), tabs Inventory / Armor / Skills. Items show small badges (`ItemBadges`): red offence lower-left (weapons: plain damage number; rings: +% / +N), blue defence lower-right (+N). The Armor tab is laid out like a person (helmet top; gloves, chest, shoulders across; shield, boots, cape below; misc down the right) and ends with total offence and defence (big, centred). The top-left Inventory cell always shows ingots (not an item slot): the pack is 15 slots (`NumSlots`). No on-screen instructions for Tab or Caps Lock (owner asked to remove them). Tab frees the cursor (camera look and attack clicks pause, movement still works); Caps Lock cycles tabs. Tab still closes any open screen first. The old centred inventory window is no longer opened by Tab. The skill popup stays bottom left.
- **Shield guard** (`Hurt` in KnightCharacter.cpp): a raised shield facing the blow takes ALL of it out of the guard pool (the shield's Defence; 2026-10-31); the blow that empties it breaks the guard and drops the shield until fully recovered (10 s after the last blocked blow, then 10 s to refill, like health). A steel bar like the health bar, just above the weapon slots, shows it: it appears when the shield is raised and stays until the guard is full again (it throbs while broken). The defence slot at the end of the weapon bar shows a shield icon and a blue number.
- **Stowing**: while climbing, riding or sitting, the shield goes on the back (over the sword, under the cape) and the weapon sits diagonally on the back (`PlaceShield`, `RebuildHeld` with `bStowed`).
- **Shield stance stepping**: legs step backwards or sideways in the shield-up stance (`StepFw`/`StepSd` in Tick).
- **Pen knight** (`AFoeKnight`, FoeKnight.cpp): replaces the pigs. Iron armour, sword and kite shield, 90 HP, hits 11-15 raw, 0.65 s overhead windup tell, sometimes raises his shield after being hit (it guards like a player's, GuardBack 6 s), can be kicked down, never leaves the pen, drops a small purse and respawns 20 s after defeat. Tuning constants are at the top of FoeKnight.h. (Pigs, and with them pork, are gone; `APig` is unused.)
- Cape: heavier cloth settings; the pale back panel of the tabard was removed (it showed as a white patch under the cape).

## Built in the second Opus session (2026-10-12; not yet seen in-game by the owner)

- **Death** (`AKnightCharacter::Die`, called from `Hurt` for ANY death, poison gas included): everything (pack, weapon bar, worn gear, potion slot, ingots) goes into a big burlap sack (`ALootDrop::SetupSack`, lasts 10 min) where you fell; you wake just inside Blackrock's east gate in the starting kit (`USkillComponent::ResetToStartingKit`) at full health. Skills are kept.
- **Loot windows**: E on a sack frees the cursor and shows the sack's pack (with its ingots), its armour (laid out like the knight) and its weapon bar to the LEFT of the player's windows (`AKnightHUD::DrawLootWindows`). Click, drag onto your own windows, or "Take all". Walking 4 m away or putting the cursor away closes it. Meat/hide drops still take-all on E.
- **Potions** (ids 950-952): swiftness (+50% speed 8 s, 1 min cooldown), healing (full heal, 2 min), vigour (+50% health bar for 60 s, extra comes full, 1 min). A potion slot sits between the weapon bar and the defence slot; Q drinks (or click it with the cursor out); drag potions in and out. The slot darkens and counts down while that kind cools. The store always stocks all three.
- **Shields**: three styles share the shield slot, encoded in the id (gear ids are now 1000 + kind*100 + variant; shields 1000 + 1100 + style*10 + tier; `StyleOf`, `ShieldId`, `ShieldGuard`). Kite (all-rounder), Round (guard x0.75, recovers after 5 s instead of 10, 15% quicker in the stance), Tower (guard x1.5, 20% slower in the stance). Each has its own model (`KnightDesign` shields, `FDef::Style`) and icon. The store sometimes sells round and tower shields; the practice pack has one of each.
- Shield ideas: guard break and the 3x guard pool were built 2026-10-31 (see "Hit boxes and hurt boxes"); heavy-weapon guard drain still unpicked.

## Items are data (2026-10-30)

Every item number and text lives in CSV spreadsheets in `Content/Data/` (Tiers, Kinds, Materials, Potions, Accessories, ShieldStyles), read by `JeebItemData.cpp` (`JeebItems::Tables()`); `Inventory.h` keeps the ids and rules and answers from the tables (`TierRow`, `KindRow`, `MaterialRow`, `PotionRow`, `AccessoryRow`, `ShieldStyleRow`). The owner's guide is `Docs/EditingItems.md`. Saves store items by number: never reorder or delete rows in Potions or Accessories (add at the end); Tiers, Kinds and Materials rows are found by their Key. A new item number or value goes in the CSV, not in code. Potion effects (speed, heal, vigour, radar) are code; their strength and duration are data. `Content/Data` is staged as UFS (DefaultGame.ini). Store stocking rules (Store.h) and arrows are still in code.

## Chat and radar (2026-10-30)

Chat: `AKnightPlayerController::ServerChat` / `ClientChat`; `JeebChat.h` (swear filter, the owner's `Saved/Moderation.txt` mute/ban file, each player's own `Saved/ChatMuted.txt`); drawn in `KnightHUDChat.cpp` (Enter, `/w`, `/mute`). Radar potion: `AKnightCharacter::UpdateRadar` (server, owner-only `RadarDots`), drawn on the minimap.

## Tuning numbers live in Content/Data/Tuning.ini (2026-11-02)

- How fast, far, strong and long things are (movement, the bow, jab, smash, kick, sweep, dash, knockdowns, guard, the armour cap, headshots, armour weight) are in `Content/Data/Tuning.ini`, explained line by line. Code says how a move works; the file holds its numbers. Change a number there, never in code: NO BUILD needed.
- Live: while a knight exists (editor Play, test pictures), the file is checked twice a second (`JeebTune::Poll` in TickHousekeeping) and the next swing or arrow uses the new value. 
- Code: `JeebTune::V().Name`; the list is `JeebTuningList.h` (one line each: section, name, fallback). AKnightCharacter and KnightCharacterCombat.cpp keep their old names as `static inline const float&` references, so old code reads live values. A missing, misspelled or non-number line is logged ("Jeebsland tuning:") and keeps its fallback.
- To make a new number tunable: a line in JeebTuningList.h AND a commented line in Tuning.ini (one home: the file). Only tuning numbers go in, never behaviour; pose and key-pose numbers stay in code.
- Tuning by request: edit the file, measure with a test-picture run (log lines), no rebuild. Online, worlds and players must run the same file (repackage after movement changes).

## Armour, shields and moves (2026-11-02)

- Armour by body part: hurt boxes carry a zone (`JeebHit::EZone`, set in `FKnightAnimator::GatherHurt`); `FirstTouch` reports it and every blow passes it to `Hurt` / `DealDamage` / `ApplyDamage`. The piece covering it (helmet head, chestplate torso, shoulders upper arms, gloves forearms, boots legs: `JeebItems::ZoneArmour`) lets through its tier's `ArmourTakes` (Tiers.csv: Tin 0.8 to legendary 0.5; rings' DefenseMul cut the stopped part further, never below `MinTake` 0.5). Blows with no zone use the average of the five pieces. The old flat armour and Defence-level subtraction is gone. Damage numbers show what got through. Badges and tooltips show armour as "stops N%" (`ArmourProtect`).
- Armour weight (owner, 2026-11-02: a balancing act, strength and weight not tied together): each piece weighs Kinds.csv `Weight` (helmet 3, chest 8, shoulders 4, gloves 2, boots 4 kg) x Tiers.csv `WeightMult` (Tin 0.85, Iron 1.3, Bronze 1.15, Steel 1.0, Silver 0.8, Gold 1.5, Onyx 1.0, Emerald 1.3, Diamond 0.6; a full steel suit 21 kg). Over Tuning.ini [Weight] `FreeWeight` 18 kg, each kg slows walking, sprinting, crouching, the stance and the charging/drawing caps by `SlowPerKg` 1%, at most `MaxSlow` 30% (`JeebItems::WeightSpeedMul`, as `Burden` in KnightCharacterMovement.cpp; full gold measured 432.5 cm/s, x0.865). Rolls, the dash and riding are not slowed; shields and weapons weigh nothing. Shown in the Armor window's bottom-left cell (kg and -N%), tooltips, the store, smithy and dictionary. Test pictures: `-ShotWear=<tier>` (a full suit) and `-ShotPanel` (Tab windows open), log JEEBWEIGHT.
- Projectile headshots: arrows and bolts to the head x1.5 (`JeebCombat::HeadshotMul`) before the helmet; number type `N_Headshot` (gold, pops in 1.5x and settles over 0.18 s). Sound later (roadmap "Sound").
- Shields: the guard's SIZE comes only from the Defence level (`GuardForLevel`: 20 at level 1 to 40 at 99); each blow on a raised shield costs the guard Raw x `ShieldDrain` (tier `ShieldTakes` Tin 1.0 to legendary 0.5, divided by the style's GuardMul). The shield's size (the wedge) never changes, so low-level players block just as well. Shields show a "strength" (10 / drain: tin kite 10, gold kite 17). Pen knight: guard of Defence 10, iron kite drain.
- Low sweep: no launch any more; the target drops on the spot (`FReact::bDrop`, `KnockDownBy(From, true)` -> `MulticastKnock(true, 1)`, `AFoeKnight::KnockDown(From, true)`), then gets up or rolls. The launch and DI code is kept for later moves.
- Dash: the whole dash brakes steadily with no friction (`DashSlideBrake` 4500, `bDashSliding` in TickAttack): stab out about 100 cm in, skids to a stop at about 150 cm (log JEEBDASH).
- Every arrow drops from the moment it leaves the bow (owner 2026-11-02: `BowStraight` 1 cm in Tuning.ini, was 50 m, 20, then 5); a full draw at about real gravity; draws from half up all drop at about real gravity (`BowSagMax` 980, was 300).
- Dropped from the plan (owner): grab and throws, crouch block.

## Hit boxes and hurt boxes (2026-10-31, Smash Ultimate style)

(Since 2026-11-05 poses come from clips, and the sphere tables and the kick's spheres are generated from the weapon and the bones: see "The only knight". Anything below about key poses, `PoseKick`, or `SwordJabBoxes` is history; the rules and timings hold.)

`HitBoxes.h` (namespace JeebHit). Hurt boxes are capsules on the bones (`FKnightAnimator::GatherHurt`: helm, torso, upper arms, forearms, thighs, shins, feet; anything with health can give its own through `UHealthComponent::GatherHurt`, else an upright capsule). Hit boxes are spheres on the weapon, live only in a move's active frames, swept from the last frame, lowest ID wins, one hit per target per move (`AKnightCharacter::TickHitboxes`, server lands the blow). Only the sword's jab uses them so far (`SwordJabBoxes`; its old cone check in `AttackLands` is skipped); new moves should get their own hit box tables and active windows. View: `DrawBoxes`, material M_HitBox (see-through, no depth test); H toggles, on by default in MoveLab only. MoveLab has a still sparring knight (`AFoeKnight::bStill`; `-LabKnight=170` moves him closer for test pictures) and a still guarding one (`bStillGuard`; `-LabGuardFirst` puts him straight ahead, `-LabKnightYaw=0` turns both knights' backs to you). `ShotPos` does not move the player in MoveLab.

Shields, kick and knockdown (owner, 2026-10-31): `TickHitboxes` takes whatever each hit box meets first along its sweep (`FirstTouchWedge` vs `FirstTouch`; a tie goes to the shield): body first is a clean hit (no rebound). A blow on a raised shield never hurts the holder: the guard takes all of it (`Hurt` for players, `AFoeKnight::TakeOnShield`); the blow that empties it breaks the guard and drops the shield until it refills. The kick (`EAtkStyle::Kick`, wheel up, `StartKick`; pose in `PoseKick`, Ken's forward smash mirrored to the right leg) goes through shields and knocks a shielding target down (`UHealthComponent::KnockDown`, `AKnightCharacter::KnockDownBy`; players' kicks hit other players only in MoveLab until PvP). `MoveLocked()` covers the smash lock, the kick and lying down. Knocked-down players get up with `GetUp` (0 up, 1 roll left, 2 roll right). Measured from above: a hip's roll is NEGATIVE to swing the leg out to his right, and an UpperPivot/BodyRoot roll + tips the top to his right (Unreal's roll; an older note here said the opposite). Strafe mode is on from the start (`bStrafeMode = true`). Kick timing (second pass): `KickDur` 0.65 s, active 0.18-0.5; the leg keys in `PoseKick` are ABSOLUTE angles blended in by W (not added to the stance), so the leg goes straight out toward the target with the toes turned to his left; the first hit sphere sits 24 cm past the ankle along the shin, ahead of the sole.

Combat rules and balance (owner, 2026-10-31, second pass): rock-paper-scissors, attack > kick > shield > attack. The jab's hit boxes cover its whole swing (hit 1 0.30-0.80, hit 2 0.36-0.74). The kick knocks anyone down (`UHealthComponent::KnockDown`, `KnockDownBy`) and on a raised shield also crushes half the guard; a kick into a blade that is coming (`BladeComing`, `UHealthComponent::IsSwinging`) loses and the kicker falls; a blow landing during a kick knocks the kicker down (`Hurt`). Hitstun (`StunLeft`, `TakeReact` / `MulticastReact` / `ApplyReact`, `JeebHit::FReact`): jab 1 0.45 s (hit 2 is a sure combo), jab 2 0.32 s with a push clear, smash 0.5-0.8 s; clean blows no longer give 0.4 s of invulnerability (hazards still do). The low sweep (`EAtkStyle::Sweep`, wheel down, sword only, `SweepDur` 0.55, active 0.24-0.6 (out in 0.13 s since the third pass), key poses `Sweep` in PoseArms, tip about 10-15 cm off the floor) used to launch whoever it hit (`JeebHit::LaunchVelocity`, DI; since 2026-11-02 it drops them on the spot instead, see "Armour, shields and moves"); launched knights land knocked down (get-up waits for landing; `KnockAuto` 1.5 s from landing). The pen knight (`AFoeKnight`) has hitstun (`EAct::Stun`), can be launched (`Launch`, `bAir`), never gets up the same way twice running, reaches `StrikeReach` 200 and stuns players on a hit. Rebounds are 0.3 s (jab) / 0.6 s (smash); a shield's guard is sized by the Defence level since 2026-11-02 (`JeebItems::GuardForLevel`, see "Armour, shields and moves"). In MoveLab every move hits other players (PvP test). A payoff-matrix model of the four options (scratch, PowerShell fictitious play) settled at attack 64%, shield 26%, kick 11%; the sweep is for punishing kicks and catching get-ups. Test pictures: `-ShotMove=sweep`, `kicksweep` (log JEEBSWEEP gives the tip height).

Third pass (owner, 2026-10-31): a kick that lands (`KickLanded`, server, multicast `MulticastKickLanded`) plays the rest of itself `KickLandedSpeed` (2.2x) faster and the low sweep may cut it short at once (`StartSweep`; a wheel-down during the kick is buffered in `QueuedSweep`; `ServerAttack` lets that combo through its rate and end-lag checks; the leg eases out over 0.1 s, `KickFade`). The dash attack (`EAtkStyle::Dash`, sprinting forward + click with a sword, `Interact`): a lunge with the blade stabbed straight out (key poses `Dash` in PoseArms keyed by `DashU`, legs in PoseKick; a poke, not a cut: from the draw-back to the stab the chest, the arm's yaw and the blade's heading stay put), `DashLunge` 1100 cm/s, active 0.11-0.28 s, smash boxes, damage / stun / push of a smash charged `DashCharge` 0.2, end lag `DashLag()` = a smash's, rebounds like a smash. R skips slots without a weapon. Test pictures: `-ShotMove=dash`, `kickcombo` (with `-LabGuardFirst -LabKnight=150`).

## Bow and arrows (2026-10-31, Breath of the Wild style)

(Since 2026-11-05 the body's pose comes from the bow clips, not the code-built `PoseBow` and `FBowTune` described below. The rules, timings and arrows below still hold.)

`KnightCharacterBow.cpp`. The bow is two-handed now (Kinds.csv: no shield with it) and held in the LEFT fist on `BowHold` (RebuildHeld; its straight string `BowString` hides while drawn and `BowBits` draw the pulled string, the nocked arrow and its head). Hold attack to draw (`EBow` Drawing: `BowDrawTime` 1 s to full, `BowHoldMax` 2 s more, then it looses by itself; Recover 0.3 s); the player's machine decides (`StartDraw` / `ReleaseDraw` / `CancelDraw`), the server checks against its own timer (`ServerBowDraw`, `ServerBowShoot`, `ServerBowCancel`) and fires (`FireArrow`: by the draw, none / half / full, speed `BowSpeedMin/Mid/Max` 1000/2400/4800 and drop `BowSagMin/Mid/Max` 2900/1000/300 cm/s/s, so a level shot lands about 3 m / 12 m out (owner: 10 ft, 40 ft); a FULL draw is Breath of the Wild style (2026-11-01): dead straight for `BowStraight` (now 1 cm in Tuning.ini: it drops at once) (`FProjLaunch::Straight`), then falls at `BowSagFull` 980; damage x0.12-1.3 by the draw to the power 1.5; `FProjLaunch::Sag`), others see it through `MulticastBow`. Drawing: half walking speed, facing the camera, camera boom 540 -> `BowCamLength` 220 with the socket 75 cm right (`TickBow`), reticle `AKnightHUD::DrawReticle`; the arrow flies in one straight line from `ShotStart` (just left of and below the middle of the screen, level with the bow: `ShotLeft` / `ShotDown`; checked by the server in `CheckShotStart`; 2026-11-01) toward what is under the reticle (`BowAimDir`: the world trace, cut short by the first body or raised shield on the line, `AProjectile::FirstBodyAlong`, since knights and foes are not solid to traces; without it arrows aimed past the target at the floor behind). A white streak follows each flying arrow (`AProjectile::TickTrail`, M_SwordTrail with Opacity 1 and Bright 1.3, `TrailLife` 0.35 s, `TrailWidth` 4.5 cm), and flying arrows are drawn `FlyScale` 1.7x bigger (true size once stuck). The aim looks from abreast of the bow on, so a dummy or anything between the camera and the knight never catches it (it once fell back to firing parallel to the camera, about 80 cm left of the reticle); measured with `-ShotStrafe=1` / `-1` (walks sideways while shooting) and the log line JEEBAIM, arrows land within 7 cm of the reticle's line still or strafing. While aiming the camera keeps up with him (CameraLagSpeed 8 -> 80), so arrows no longer seem to curve as he moves; it pulls back out as soon as the arrow is away (owner). Weapon switch, roll, kick, swing, hitstun, knockdown, climbing etc. cancel without shooting. Pose (`PoseBow`, end of PoseArms): the arrow runs from the bow back to an anchor beside his right cheek (`FBowTune` Up / Side from the neck joint), so the string hand stays outside his body; the left arm is straight with the fist on that line where it just reaches, the bow upright; the right arm a two-bone reach to the string (the elbow behind the hand in line with the arrow, an archer's draw; owner 2026-11-01), chest +55 degrees (side-on). Reference game for the bow (owner asked, 2026-11-01): Kingdom Come: Deliverance. At ease (`BowRestQuat`, owner's drawing 2026-11-01) the limbs run forward and back through the left fist (front tip up 10 degrees, splayed 10 so the back tip stays off his hip), the bow's curve a U below and the string above, tipped 40 degrees in so the string runs between the elbow and his body (`WeaponDesign::BowBrace` 14 cm); the arm hangs normally (the owner disliked it held out); while walking the bow takes out 60% of the forearm's swing so it never stands on end; the empty right arm hangs and swings like the left (PoseArms, EaseKind K_Bow). Aiming, `PoseBow` turns BowHold upright with the string straight back along the arrow (in front of his face), the fist 12 cm under the arrow (the glove's top measures 11.3 cm up the bow: it rests on top), chest 70 degrees side-on. The pose numbers are `FBowTune` in KnightCharacterBow.cpp (anchor 11 up and 22 right of the neck: closer and the arrow or string cut the left shoulder). Quiver at the back of the right hip (`UpdateQuiver`, on BodyRoot at (-26, 28, -29), pitch -5, roll -6, hanging nearly straight: a spot `-QuiverSearch` found clear of armour, arms, legs and cape standing, walking and aiming); its arrows show only while he has some (others see it through `RepLook`'s last entry, `LookArrows`). Arrows: the arrow slot right of the shield slot (`ArrowSlotId`, F / click = `CycleArrows`); fire arrows are id 909 (`JeebItems::IdFireArrow`, count as steel; `AProjectile::Burn` 3 x 2 damage, a glow light). Starting kit: sword, bow, axe, pickaxe, rod + 99 steel and 99 fire arrows (stacks of 99; save version 9 merges and tops up to that); save version 8 tops every character up to the starting arrows (40 steel, 15 fire; a full pack sends its last non-arrow things to the bank), version 4 gave old characters the arrows and version 7 a tin bow right of the sword on the weapon bar when none is there (a full bar sends its last weapon to the pack, trading places with a pack bow if any; JeebSave Migrate). Test runs (`-JeebShot`) never write the local save and get 10 steel arrows when out. Test pictures hide the hit boxes unless `-ShotBoxes`; `-ShotMove=bow` (or `bow150`), `dash`, `kickcombo`; log lines JEEBBOW, JEEBKICK.
Arrows (`AProjectile`, rewritten): hit boxes like swords (a raised shield's wedge first, then hurt boxes; other players only in MoveLab); on the wedge, the shield's own parts (`GetShieldParts`, `UHealthComponent::ShieldParts`) are tested: in them the arrow sticks in the shield, otherwise it glances off (`Deflect`, relaunched with `FProjLaunch::Seq`) and is spent; the shield's guard takes the damage, no rebound. Anything else: sticks (`FProjStuck`, replicated: on a knight at the nearest joint pivot by name, the ground and trees in world space) for `StuckSeconds` 120. Only arrows in the world are pickable (E, `EInteract::Arrow`, `EInvOp::PickArrow`); in armour or a shield they are spent. Test pictures: `-ShotMove=bow` (full draw, it looses by itself) or `bow60` (let go after 0.6 s); a side-on picture keeps the camera put and aims straight ahead (`ShotSideOn`).

Sword smash attack (2026-10-31, Smash Ultimate timing): `ESmash` phases in `AKnightCharacter` (KnightCharacterCombat.cpp, `TickSmash` / `SetSmash`). A press starts hit 1 as usual (no delay for jabs); held past `SmashHoldDelay` (0.25 s) hit 1 waits at its pull-back (`SmashApexT`) and charges at half walking speed (he may turn): full in 1 s, held full 2 s more, then it goes by itself. Release = hit 1's swipe with `SwordSmashBoxes` (bigger), damage `SwordSmashDamage` x 1 to 2.5 by the charge. End lag is fixed: a pause of `SmashEndPause` at the end of the swipe (`SmashPauseT`), then hit 1's recovery; no moving, turning, blocking, rolling or jumping (`SmashLocked`). Online: the player's machine decides from the button (`bAttackDown`) and sends `ServerSmash`, which checks it (`SmashInputOk`, charge capped by the server's own timer) and multicasts; end lag and auto-release are timed on every machine; `ServerAttack` refuses swings before the end lag is over. Test pictures: `-ShotMove=smash` (full) or `smash40` (0.4 s of charge: hundredths, the command line stops at a dot); the log line JEEBSMASH shows the phases.

Shields as hit boxes see them (2026-10-31, owner's spec): a raised shield is a thin curved band in front of the body (`JeebHit::FShieldWedge`: `Inner` 58 to `Radius` 78 cm from his centre, where the real shield sits, spanning 45 degrees either side of straight ahead; seen from the front an arch: straight sides from his feet up to `ArchFrom` (his belt), then a half ellipse up to the top of his helm (`HelmTop`) in the middle; drawn blue). Checked before hurt boxes; a touch is a block (`UHealthComponent::GatherShield` / `ShieldBlock`; the attacker's damage then skips `ModifyDamage`). The pen knight's shield stops 70%; MoveLab's second still knight (`AFoeKnight::bStillGuard`, `-LabGuardFirst` puts him straight ahead for pictures) has a player-style guard pool (iron kite shield: 21, three times its Defence). The player's own guard (`Hurt`) now counts a blow as in front only within the same 45 degrees. Hitting a shield: `StartRebound` (server, multicast to all): `ESmash::Rebound`, the blade springs back, a step back, locked `ReboundJab` 0.3 s / `ReboundSmash` 0.6 s; `ServerAttack` refuses swings until it is over.

## Animation direction (owner, 2026-11-05; replaces the 2026-10-30 rule)

The knight may use animation clips as well as code-driven poses; the old "keep the knight code-driven" rule is scratched. The goal is natural movement, with moves that fit together and never look choppy as they switch. References: Breath of the Wild, Skyrim, Super Smash Bros, and other games.

How the suit's animator works (`FKnightAnimator`, 2026-11-05):
- Code writes the bones of a `UPoseableMeshComponent`; clips are read at load as dense frames x bones from `Content/Data/KnightClips.bin` (list `ClipNames`). Game state chooses a key (`ChooseAction`; the old rules, timers and hit windows still decide everything) and each key change starts an inertial blend (a decaying offset from the old pose, so no pop and no cross-fade), with the right hand's rate capped so the blade doesn't whip.
- Layers: locomotion (9 clips by direction and speed on a shared, distance-based phase), overlays from the waist (crouch, block, bow), then actions (one-shots, time-warped so their hit and follow-through land on the game's own hit windows).
- `Content/Data/KnightMoves.csv` holds each move's clip, cut points (a tool swing's ClipHit is the top of the wind-up and ClipFollow the blow; sit and slide play to ClipHit and hold), legs mode (`LegsFull` 2 full, 1 by speed, 0 upper only), blend time, `HipLock` (removes a clip's own forward hip travel where the capsule does the moving) and `SpineKeep` (how much of the chest's turn stays with the stance, more while running, so a slash doesn't turn his back on the target). No build needed; for falls `ClipHit` is seconds shown, for jump, land and hits it is playback rate.
- `PlaceGear` moves `WristR`, `ElbowL` and `UpperPivot` onto the clip's bones so the weapon, trail and hit boxes follow it. `SolveGear` measures how the weapon, shield, bow and quiver sit on the bones, once, at load.
- Last step each frame, after the blend: the hips rise if a boot would sink (`Sole`), and `KeepTipUp` turns the wrist to keep the sword's tip at least 5 cm off the floor.
- After a fight he stands on guard (`Idle_SS`, 4 s, `CombatT`) so sword clips start and end in the same stance, then eases back to `Rest`.
- Adding a clip: download FBX from Mixamo (on its stock Y Bot) into `Art/Knight Chainmail/Clips`, run `Tools/ImportKnightClips.ps1` with the editor closed (`knight_clip_retarget.py` in Blender, `knight_clip_import.py` in Unreal), add the name to `ClipNames` and a CSV row; the editor rebakes `KnightClips.bin` at its next run, so commit that file. `knight_suit_import.py` wipes `/Game/NewSuit`, so re-import the clips and the bare knight after any suit import.
- Test pictures: `-ShotMove=...`, `-SuitWalk=<dir>_<share>_<from>_<for>`, `-SuitSprint`, `-SuitDo=roll<deg>|stun<hundredths>|knock|drop|crouch`, `-SuitClip=` / `-SuitClipAt=`, `-SuitGallery`, `-SuitLogClips`, `-LabNoDummies`, `-ShotSeconds=N`. Log: JEEBANIM (key, worst per-frame bone change, feet, hip, foot slide, sword tip), JEEBPATH (each move's hand, foot and hip paths).

## Climbing and the mantle (2026-11-12; Opus agent, not yet seen by the owner)

- Free climbing like Breath of the Wild and a Fortnite-style mantle are custom movement modes in the knight's own movement component, `UKnightMovement` (KnightMovement.h/.cpp; set on the knight through `AJeebslandCharacter`/`AKnightCharacter`'s FObjectInitializer constructors). They run inside Unreal's movement prediction: the player's machine predicts, the server runs the same moves from the same inputs (the keys' direction, Space, Ctrl) and corrects position, mode, stamina and the scripted move (`FKnightMoveResponse` carries the climbing state in corrections). Never the player's word for where he is. Modes (`JeebClimb::EMode`): 1 on the rock, 2 the climb jump, 3 the ledge climb-up, 4 mantle over, 5 mantle onto. The ladder is separate and unchanged (`bClimbing`, RepMoveFlags bit 16).
- What can be climbed (`Climbable`): a component tagged `JeebClimb` (the world's ground in WorldTerrain.cpp, the original square in GrassField.cpp, MoveLab's rock), only where too steep to walk and at most 15 degrees overhanging. Never buildings, trees, fences, walls, gear, mining rocks or Oak Hill's ground (untagged). Grabbing: walk or jump into it until he touches it (within 50 degrees of square on; the probes run only after a move that touched tagged rock, `HandleImpact`, so a busy server pays nothing for everyone else), not holding Ctrl, not right after letting go (until he lands), not with an emptied bar.
- Mantle (general rule since 2026-11-16, owner: "any object just too high for the knight to jump over, but not so high that it looks weird ... stumps and crates and other medium sized objects he can't walk through"): any solid whose top is `MantleMin` 60 .. `MantleMax` 145 cm above his feet, judged from its own collision by `UKnightMovement::MantleCandidate`, no tags needed: it blocks pawns, isn't a pawn or a replicated actor (creatures, foes, loot: only the world every machine builds for itself from the same seeds, so server and client agree), isn't simulating physics, isn't tagged `JeebClimb` (rock climbs instead) or `JeebNoMantle` (component or actor: the opt-out), its own box (for an instanced set, that instance's box: the fill's solids are thousands of boxes in one component) is at least `MantleMinWidth` 40 cm long (no posts or poles) and under 25 m, its top no more than MantleMax + 30 over his feet (buildings, trees, palisades, lamp posts fail here), and the side he meets stands on his ground (not a beam or a deck). `JeebMantle` still forces one in (MoveLab's boards). No traces in that check, so `HandleImpact` runs it on every bump and only then does `PlanMantle` trace: the first thing at knee, waist or chest height ahead must be a candidate and square on (a side, not a slope), the top just past its face flat and a candidate too (a crate on a crate: the stack's top), nothing above; then samples along the top every 15 cm find how deep it runs: deep enough to stand on (2R+10) he climbs onto it, else he vaults to the ground just past its far side (never closer than the old fixed 2R+30, so the vault keeps its stride; a landing nearly as high as the top or with no room is refused). Space by one, walking or running square into one (`MantleAuto` 1, owner 2026-11-13; 0 = Space only), or running into one in the air. The pose scales the vault's and climb-onto's own hip rise by the real height over the heights they were approved at (1 m fence, 1.2 m block; `WallPlay`). The pig pen's solid now stops at its top rail (1.08 m, was 1.6) so it can be vaulted. Too tall by their crude full-height boxes (not mantleable though they look low): carts and wagons, wells, the round hay bale (1.56), the chicken coop. A refused Space logs why (`JEEBMANTLE space: no mantle, a jump: too high (...)`); a mantle logs its plan (top height, from, to). Online: the bump only marks a flag, and `OnMovementUpdated` acts on it inside the same move (never carried into the next one, since the client merges and replays moves); the carry speed comes from his input (push toward the face times his 500 running speed, not GetMaxSpeed, which each machine eases frame by frame outside the moves), so server and client plan the same vault (2026-11-16 test: same plan on both, no corrections during the mantle; the few left come from that per-frame MaxWalkSpeed ramp as he starts walking and after landing, in KnightCharacterMovement.cpp, older than the mantle). In the air the height counts from the ground at the obstacle's foot, so a jump can't lift him onto something too tall (the round bale). Test: `-MantleAt=<x>_<y>_<yaw>_<back>_<share>[_<side>_<up>]` (several joined by x, one every 5 s, still side camera), online `-NetMantle=` on server and client (log JEEBNETFIX). Just a movement: no stamina, no XP.
- Controls: on the rock W/S up and down, A/D sideways, diagonals; Space the climb jump the way the keys point (`ClimbJumpDist` 100 cm in `ClimbJumpTime` 0.6 s for `ClimbJumpCost` 0.2 of the bar; less left than that, Space does nothing); Ctrl lets go. At the top he climbs up onto the ledge by himself.
- Climbing is a skill: a full bar lasts `AKnightCharacter::ClimbSeconds(level)` climbing upward (Tuning.ini [Climb] ClimbAt1 5 s, ClimbAt10 17.5, ClimbAt30 23, ClimbAt99 30, straight lines between; the skills window says it). Hanging still drains 1/5 as fast (`ClimbHangShare`), sideways 2/3 (`ClimbSideShare`), down half (`ClimbDownShare`), a diagonal mixes its parts. Emptied: he falls (no fall damage, owner) and can't grab again until the bar has refilled to full on the ground. Climbing XP: `ClimbXPPerMetre` for new height above the best on that grab (server). All numbers in Tuning.ini [Climb].
- Owner-approved (2026-11-12), keep as they are: 4 Climbing XP per metre of new height, climbing down drains half as fast, an emptied bar refills fully before he can grab again, Ctrl lets go and Space mantles.
- HUD: a green liquid-glass bar under the middle of the screen while climbing and while refilling (red and throbbing when emptied).
- Pose (KnightAnimatorClimb.cpp, keys 80-91): Mixamo clips WallHang, WallUp, WallDown, ShimmyL/R, HopUp/Down/L/R, LedgeUp, VaultOver, ClimbOnto (rows in KnightMoves.csv; for the loops ClipHit is cm per cycle, the stride follows distance climbed). On the rock the whole body tilts about the hips to lie along it and is set so the hands are on it; scripted moves keep the clip's path first and the capsule's by the end. Weapon and shield go on his back on the rock, in the climb-up and in both mantles (`ClimbStowed`, any climb mode), so the hands are free for the fence or the block. What is on his back follows the back's line, hips to neck (`BackLineW`), and moves in toward him as he goes flat over a ledge (`StowPull`, 18 cm at flat; test `-StowPull=<cm>`). The vault is Mixamo's VaultTwo cut to its knee tuck (0-0.34 s; later it leans right back) and tipped 24 degrees forward about the hips from mid-vault to the landing (`VaultTipDeg` in KnightAnimatorClimb.cpp) so he drops forward onto his feet instead of sitting, with both hands pressed onto the fence top while he goes over (`PlantHands`, the edge from `PlanMantle`: `ActEdge`, others' machines find it as the mantle starts; log JEEBVAULT). After the ledge pull-up and a climb onto he stands on top for `MantleStandTime` 0.25 s before he walks on (inside the move: `ScriptedHold`; the pose goes to rest at once and rises out of the crouch). On the rock each hand and boot rests on the surface traced under it, 1.5 cm off, never in it (`ClimbIK`: the gauntlet to the fingertips and the boot to the toe cap with their thickness, two-bone reaches, the body eased off the rock for the hands; limbs swinging to the next hold are left alone; log JEEBCLIMBIK, before and after). Every way off the wall eases out (`WallExitBlend`: 0.6 s from the ledge and block, 0.4 the vault, 0.5 letting go), held 0.4 s so a second key change can't cut it short.
- Test: MoveLab rock at X 1150-2000, Y -1080..-300 (west face sheer toward the start, south 70, north 55, east 40 walkable), fences at X 1000 (Y 300-600 1 m, 620-900 1.35 m), block at (1000, 1150). Switches `-ClimbKeys=<up>_<right>_<from>_<for>[x...]` (hundredths), `-ClimbDo=jump<at>|letgo<at>|space<at>`; logs JEEBCLIMB, JEEBMANTLE, JEEBCLIMBPOSE (hands and feet to the rock, slip), JEEBCLIMBCLIP.

## How to test

The owner's cheat sheet is `Docs/HowToTest.md` (editor Play never needs the worlds; the .bat files are the real thing). Point them to it, and keep it up to date when testing steps change. Hosting friends: `Docs/PlayWithFriends.md`.

## Plan the fastest way to run every request (owner, 2026-11-14: "It needs to speed up")

Before starting any request or batch, work out the most efficient way to run it, and say the plan in a line or two with the model and effort recommendation:
- Split it into independent parts. Mark which need Unreal and which don't (Blender, Meshy, Mixamo, data, research, docs): those run beside the others freely.
- Every agent that builds or runs Unreal gets its own project copy from its first step.
