  - Reuse kept copies with `Tools/AgentCopy.sh reset <name>`, so builds are incremental (minutes, not the 10-20 of a new copy).
  - Make or reset the copies and start their builds in the background while writing the briefs.
- Keep all three machine slots (`Tools/uqueue.sh`) busy. Check `uqueue.sh status` now and then, and unstick an OVERDUE holder at once.
- Inside each agent:
  - all edits first, then ONE build;
  - all its pictures in as few runs as possible (several shots per run), MoveLab rather than Field when it will do;
  - log lines measured before any pictures;
  - every matching reviewer at once on the same build, asked for every problem in one round.
- Frame-rate timing (`uqueue.sh solo`, holds all three slots): batch it. Each agent puts all its timing views in one run, and agents that need timing coordinate to measure back to back in one solo window (owner, 2026-11-14). Not upgrading the PC for now (6 cores and an 8 GB GPU make three slots the useful limit).
- Pictures in MoveLab (or a TownLab lineup) whenever the real map isn't needed; Field only for what depends on the town or the world (owner, 2026-11-14).
- Merge each branch as soon as it passes review and push it. Don't hold finished parts for the slowest one.
- Model per part: Sonnet for data, colour and small tweaks (saves usage for the rest), Opus for motion, visuals and new systems.

## Parallel agents for every multi-part request (owner, 2026-11-11; standing rule, never wait to be reminded)

When a request has several independent parts, split them across background agents from the START of the turn, scaled to the size (a quick fix needs none; Opus for motion, visuals and new systems, Sonnet for data and colour tweaks). Each agent gets its own files and a full brief (the owner's words, picture paths, files it may and may not touch). Agents run their own reviewers but do not commit; the main session keeps the work that touches the most shared files, runs shared rebuilds once (e.g. ImportNewSuit, then clips, then the bare knight), and commits everything together.

Usage limit (owner, 2026-11-13, said twice): whenever background agents run, start a CronCreate guard every 5 minutes that reads `mcp__ccd_session_mgmt__get_usage`; at about 97% of the 5-hour limit, have every agent finish its command, release its Unreal lock and stop, write a resume note, and schedule a one-shot 2 minutes after the reset that resumes each agent with SendMessage. Cron jobs die with the session: set the guard again each session.

Each agent works in its own project copy (owner, 2026-11-13, "let's do it"). This is the default for EVERY agent that builds or runs Unreal, from its first step: make the copies before briefing the agents, never "from the next batch". (2026-11-14 the switch was put off to the next batch while seven agents queued for hours in the main folder; the owner had to ask "I thought we said we were going to run multiple copies at the same time".) If agents are already working in the main folder when this comes up, move them at once: each stops at a point that compiles and releases its lock, one local checkpoint commit (not pushed), `AgentCopy.sh new` for each agent with real work left, and a brief with its copy's path. Agents only minutes from done may stay. Any process change the owner approves applies to the work running now, not just to later work.
- `bash Tools/AgentCopy.sh new <agent>` makes `../JeebsAgents/<agent>` on branch `agent/<agent>` from main's latest commit, with the big git-ignored assets linked in (Content/Filler, Art originals); `remove <agent>` unlinks them first, then deletes the copy (only once merged). Commit everything in the main folder before making copies, or the copies start without it.
- The agent edits, builds (Build.bat with the copy's .uproject) and runs test pictures only in its copy, and commits to its own branch (never pushes). Another agent's half-finished code can never break its build.
- `Tools/uqueue.sh` shares the machine: at most 3 Unreal processes at once (6 cores, 32 GB, 8 GB GPU; a fourth only slows the others), first come first served, one build at a time per copy, `solo` for frame-rate timing (holds every slot). Same rules as below: run it in the background, release the moment Unreal exits, `-RenderOffScreen`, `-abslog=<copy>/Saved/Logs/<agent>.log`.
- A copy's first build compiles the whole game module (about 10-20 minutes, ~5 GB); shaders come from the shared engine cache. Disk: about 8 GB a copy (127 GB free on 2026-11-14). Keep copies between jobs and `reset` them after merging, so their builds stay warm. `remove` only when the disk runs short.
- The main session merges the branches into main, settles conflicts, runs the shared rebuilds once, checks the whole, commits and pushes. Unreal assets (.uasset, .umap) can't be merged: each asset folder has one owning agent per batch.
- Linked shared assets belong to the main folder: a copy rebuilds them only if its brief says so.

How agents shared Unreal in one folder (owner, 2026-11-13: "find a smarter/optimal way"; a free-for-all with one lock had five agents waiting while one held it 75 minutes; still the rule for work in the main folder):
- `Tools/ulock.sh` is a first-come-first-served queue. `build NAME` holds the exclusive build lock (`Saved/unreal.lock`) for builds, importers, material builds, the editor and packaging. `run NAME` holds one of two run slots for `-game` / test-picture runs, so two can go at once but never during a build. `release NAME` gives it back; `status` shows the queue. Waiting blocks, so agents run it in the background. Stale locks clear themselves when no Unreal process has run for 3 minutes; holders past 30 min (build) or 12 min (run) show OVERDUE.
- Every test run: `-RenderOffScreen -Multiprocess -LiveCoding=false` (Live Coding is also off in `Config/DefaultEditorPerProjectUserSettings.ini`: a running Live Coding console blocks every copy's build; no window over the owner's games; `-Multiprocess` skips the startup SDK check, which otherwise sits idle until any build on the machine ends, and stops runs writing shared config files) and `-abslog=<repo>/Saved/Logs/<agent>.log` (two runs at once would share Jeebsland.log). GPU timing is measured alone.
- Never edit `Tools/uqueue.sh` or `ulock.sh` in place while agents are waiting in them (bash reads a running script as it goes, so the waiting copies break): write a new file and `mv` it over the old one.
- Launching Unreal from Git Bash: give it Windows paths (`cygpath -w`; a `/c/Users/...` path reaches it as `c/Users/...` and pops a visible "Failed to open descriptor file" dialog, 2026-11-14) and put `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` before the command (or launch from PowerShell). Otherwise bash rewrites the map `/Game/Field` into `C:/Program Files/Git/Game/Field` and the run hangs with no level (2026-11-14: one run sat 36 minutes holding every slot). Give every run a time limit, and kill it if it overruns.
- Hold only while Unreal runs: never while thinking, editing or waiting for reviewers.
- Batch per agent: finish every edit first (a build compiles everyone's code, so never leave a half-edit in a file), one build, then all pictures in as few runs as possible.
- A build failing in another agent's file: release and tell the coordinator at once with the error lines.
- Size the batch: at most about four agents that need Unreal at once; Blender, data, research and Meshy/Mixamo work can run beside them freely.

## How much checking a change gets (owner, 2026-11-15: "make sure we're not doing an unnecessary amount of checks"; overrides "every change" below)

- **Small** (numbers, colours the owner named, removals, one-file tweaks): no pictures, no reviewers. Build, and the owner looks in the editor.
- **Medium** (tuning a look, moving or resizing things): build, one quick look of my own (a few pictures or log lines), then the owner judges it live. A reviewer only where something could clip, break, cost frame time or go wrong online.
- **Large** (new systems, combat, online, new models or materials in the world, anything the owner can't easily judge by eye): the full reviewer rounds below.
- **Taste first, polish after:** for looks, show the owner a rough version quickly (live console variables `jeeb.*` whenever possible, so they can try values in Play themselves). Only polish and review what they said they like.
- **At most two review rounds.** After two FAILs, stop and bring the owner the pictures and the problem; change the approach, not just the values.
- **Timing only when frame time can change,** batched into one solo window. Pictures and other work run in the free slots meanwhile; nothing sits idle waiting for solo.

## Reviews before the owner sees a change (owner, 2026-11-01)

Large changes (see above) are checked by the matching reviewer agents in `.claude/agents/` (Sonnet) BEFORE they are reported to the owner as done. Fix whatever they FAIL and review again. Only results that pass reach the owner. Say which reviewers ran in the report. Routing:
- Cosmetic (poses, held items, gear, models, trails, HUD looks): `visual-reviewer`, with pictures from `Tools/ShotViews.ps1` from every side that matters (B, R, F, L).
- Moves, animation, walking or running, any movement feel: `motion-reviewer` (frame sheets, `-Frames`), plus `visual-reviewer` if the look changed.
- Lights, shadows, meshes, materials, world building, heavy Tick code: `performance-reviewer` (baseline 140 fps HIGH / 175 LOW packaged).
- Anything players do to each other or the server decides (combat, items, XP, deaths, shops, what others see): `online-reviewer`.
- Before repackaging, and after anything loaded by name or new data files: `package-reviewer`.
Small value tweaks the owner dictated exactly (a number, a colour) need no review, unless they change how something looks or moves.

How to keep changes fast (owner, 2026-11-02; a batch used to take about 20 minutes per change):
1. Batch: make every code change of a request first, then ONE build and ONE round of pictures for all of it.
2. Run the reviewers at the same time (background agents, one message), all on the same build.
3. Pictures: `Tools/ShotViews.ps1` already gives the reviewers half-size copies (1280x720 and crops); never hand them the raw 2560x1440 frames.
4. Only the views that matter (a jump or a dash: one side view frame sheet; a held item: the sides it can clip on).
5. Reopen the editor once, at the end. Measure with log lines (JEEBDASH, JEEBSWEEP, JEEBANIM...) before taking pictures, so a wrong number costs a rebuild, not a review round.

Lessons and plan reviews (owner, 2026-11-02):
- `Docs/ReviewLessons.md` lists what reviewers have caught before, by area. Read the matching sections BEFORE building a change. Each reviewer ends every FAIL with a `LESSON:` line; add it to that file (one general rule, merged with similar ones) in the same commit as the fix.
- New systems (the Opus-level work: dungeons, PvP, quests, enemies, new moves): before writing code, send the plan to the matching reviewers in plan mode (online-reviewer for anything the server decides; motion-reviewer for the key poses and timing of a move). Fix the plan, then build. Small changes and tweaks skip this: build first, then review.

## Checking visuals

Test pictures (`Saved/Shots`, about 3.5 MB each) are deleted once a check is done (owner, 2026-11-01: they had grown to 8 GB). Take them only through `Tools/ShotViews.ps1`, for the reviewers and for moves. The owner still checks the final look themselves and sends screenshots. Build, rebuild materials, commit, reopen the editor, and say what to look for.

Exception, ANIMATION and moves (owner, 2026-10-30, after a guessed jab looked bad): compare against reference frames instead of guessing. The owner records clips with Win+Alt+R (Videos\Captures); ffmpeg (installed with winget, Gyan.FFmpeg; full path under %LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages) cuts them into tiled frame sheets in the scratchpad. Our side of it: `UnrealEditor.exe Jeebsland.uproject /Game/Field -game -JeebShot -ShotMove=jab -ShotName=jab -ShotFacing=-90 (side-on; 0 = from behind) -ShotYaw=0 -ShotPitch=-8 -ShotPhase=0.25 -RenderOffScreen -ResX=1280 -ResY=720 -benchmark -fps=30 -nosound` saves the move every other frame to Saved/Shots (pictures come out 2560x1440). Add new moves to `AKnightCharacter::ShotPlayMove`. Use `/Game/MoveLab` instead of `/Game/Field` for move pictures (2026-10-31): a copy of Field where `AKnightGameState::BuildMoveLab` builds only a flat 40 m floor with a 1 m grid and two still training dummies (`AScarecrow::bDummy`); about 30 s per series instead of 90. The owner tests moves there too (double-click MoveLab, Play). Made by Tools/make_movelab.py.

## Direction: online multiplayer (decided 2026-10-12)

The owner dropped the single-player story mode: Jeebsland is becoming an online game set up like RuneScape (many players in a shared world, server-owned characters, several world servers). Build everything with that in mind.
- Chosen path: Unreal dedicated server first; accounts are files on the server for now (a database service later). Done 2026-10-12: two players in one world; server-owned characters; accounts and saves; shared combat.
- The game map is `/Game/Field` (not Lvl_ThirdPerson). `ServerDefaultMap` points at it.
- `AKnightGameState` builds the world on EVERY machine (server and clients) from fixed random seeds; `AKnightGameMode` exists only on the server and just picks where players appear (`SpawnDefaultPawnFor`, a 2 m ring round the start). Purely visual actors (moon, night sky, bats, fireflies) are skipped on a dedicated server.
- ONE game, online first (owner, 2026-10-12): never build separate single-player behaviour. When something is removed or changed for online play, do the same in single player (e.g. the horse is gone from both until it is shared online). Single player in the editor is only a quick test of the online game.
- Rules for new code: anything that changes game state (inventory, XP, damage, drops, deaths, shops, the bank) must end up decided on the server (client asks via a Server RPC, server checks, state replicates back). Never trust the client. Don't use `GetPlayerPawn(this, 0)` / `GetFirstPlayerController()` for gameplay (there is no "player 0" on a dedicated server, and there are many players); only for purely local visuals, null-checked.
- What is shared now: movement; the look (`RepLook`, worked out by the server); the pack, gear, XP and money (owner-only replication of `USkillComponent`); swings (`ServerAttack` / `MulticastAttack`); stance flags (`RepMoveFlags`: crouch, guard, roll, slide, climb); health, guard, deaths and sacks (`ALootDrop` replicated); arrows and bolts (`AProjectile` replicated); the pen knight and scarecrows (spawned by the server only); rock and tree charges, store stock and cottage doors (lists in `AKnightGameState`); damage numbers (`MulticastNumber`).
- Movement: the knight never uses unshared world geometry as its movement base (`SetBase` override). The world is built separately on every machine, so the server could not name it and the client threw away every correction (the snap-back-to-spawn bug). Climbing: the client moves the knight and the server trusts its position until it is back on the ground (`ServerClimb`).
- NOT networked yet: cats, NPC animations and dialogue (local; the trades go through the server), sitting, time of day. Single player keeps a local save (`JeebSave` slot); online characters live on the server (`Saved/Characters`).
- Test: see `Docs/HowToTest.md`. World servers: `Tools/HostWorlds.bat` (both) or `Tools/RunServer.bat <1|2> [debug]` (debug logs every position correction: big logs, only for chasing movement bugs). `-JeebAutoLogin=Name:Password:World[:new]` logs a test account in (headless tests: add `-nullrhi`).

## Online systems (2026-10-12)

- Login: the starting screen (`KnightHUDMenu.cpp`, with a Slate key catcher for typing) shows in a standalone, non-editor world (`UJeebGameInstance::InMenu`; every editor Play mode skips it). Picking a world travels there; `AKnightPlayerController` sends `ServerLogin` (the password goes by RPC, never in the URL or logs); `AKnightGameMode::TryLogin` checks `JeebAccounts` (Saved/Accounts, PBKDF2-SHA256 via OpenSSL), refuses repeats (5 wrong in 5 min) and double logins across worlds (`.online` marks), then spawns the knight from `Saved/Characters/<key>.sav`. Editor "Play As Client" logs in as "Tester N" without a password.
- Saving: on login, every 60 s, when a player leaves (`PawnLeavingGame`) and when the world shuts down; the Save button asks the server.
- Worlds: listed in DefaultGame.ini (`[/Script/Jeebsland.JeebGameInstance] +Worlds=...`: World 1 port 7777, World 2 port 7778, MaxPlayers 200); each answers `<players> <max>` over HTTP on port + 100 (`HTTPServer`, bound to all addresses in DefaultEngine.ini).

## Trade, the Market and shops by town (2026-11-26; plan in Docs/EconomyPlan.md)

- Ingots and the bank were already account-wide (one .sav per account; FJeebClassPack holds only pack, bar, worn gear, potion), so no save migration was needed.
- Trade (KnightCharacterTrade.cpp, KnightHUDTrade.cpp): E or right-click on a player within 3 m; two-sided window; ACCEPT then CONFIRM with offers locked; every change bumps a version and clears both accepts; the server re-checks both packs and room on copies, then swaps in one step. Ends past 8 m, on death, logout or Escape. Log: Saved/Trades/trades_<port>.log.
- The Market (JeebMarket.cpp; MARKET tab at every bank): files under Saved/Market shared by every world server on the machine. A sale claims the listing by an atomic no-replace move Live -> Done, so only one buyer ever wins (tested with two worlds racing for 400 listings). Sellers are paid by mail (Mail/<account>/), delivered every 5 s by whichever world they are on; delivered ids are saved (MailDone) so a crash never pays twice. 10 listings per account, 5% tax, no fee or expiry, whole stacks.
- Shops by town (JeebShops in JeebEconomy.h): wizard goods at Silver Point (the broom stall became the wizard's shop, which also buys), knight goods at Oak Hill and Blackrock, troll goods at Steele Creek, everything not class-specific in every general store.
- Tests: Tools/economy_net.sh (1 one world with cheat attempts, 2 the two-world race, 3 buying across worlds and payday); pictures -JeebShot -ShotUI -ShotUIEcon.

## The whole world (2026-10-30)

- WorldMap.h (namespace JeebWorld) traces the owner's sketch (Docs/MapSketch.jpg; Blackrock WEST of the river, Oak Hill east): 1 sketch px = 1.3 m (FromSketch), north +X, east +Y, Blackrock's sketch spot = the existing village. Bounds X -126400..105600, Y -36000..134400 (about 2.3 x 1.7 km). Mountains (soft union of every sketched peak; rounded ridges, MountainBase + MountainRise = up to ~50 m, broad rolling lowland hills; BotW/Skyrim feel). NOTE: FMath::SmoothStep(A, B, X) only works with A < B (reversed it returns 1 almost everywhere, which once made the whole world mountains): use JeebWorld::Smooth for ramps that go down plus walls on the west, south and north edges; rocky south-west; town shelves (Towns()); the east coast (CoastY), sea at SeaLevel -60 and islands; the river and its two branches (Rivers()) carved as valleys and channels, water at RiverLevel. Banks (2026-11-15, owner: the knight could not climb out): one smoothstep from the bed (RiverDepth 240 under the water) to the valley floor (RiverFloorUp 130 over it) out to BankTop = (half width + 1 m) / 0.6, the water's edge staying at half width + 1 m; then UnderBankCap keeps the land under a 23 degree cone for 15 m past the bank's top (in Oak Hill it trims the world and the hills before the level plots go on, then the finished ground everywhere but on a house plot and 1.5 m round it, fading over 4 m: OakHill.h BuildingKeep; no house ground is cut, the empty fort and bridge plots by the water drop up to 2 m). Measured (-BankScan, 1 m stencil, 20 m each side): steepest dry bank 57 degrees before (a 70 cm lip at the old channel's edge, 1.9-2.8% of bank samples unwalkable), 36 after in the hills, 39 at one Oak Hill house plot's fade (0 unwalkable). Always compare a river distance with its half width in two statements, or with JeebWorld::NearRiver: "RiverDistance(X, Y, Half) < Half + M" let MSVC read Half before it was set, which made the river water only 2.5 m wide for swimming (JeebWater::SurfaceAt) and also skewed InWater, the town's river margins for decor and road ends.
- JeebTerrain::HeightAt = the original square (HomeHeight, unchanged) blending over 12000..15600 into JeebWorld::LandHeight, then SeaAndRivers everywhere (the river crosses the east side of the original square around Y 12000-13500).
- AWorldTerrain (spawned by KnightGameState on every machine) draws it: 8 m grid in 40x40-cell chunks with collision and a hole over the original square, vertex-coloured masks read by M_Ground (Tools/BuildWorldMaterial.ps1; since 2026-11-13 the same material on all land, masks from `JeebGround::At`); water sheets for the ocean and rivers (clients only). Meadow grass grows everywhere since 2026-11-13 (`-NoWorldGrass` hides it in tests); no trees outside the original square and Oak Hill yet.
- Map fog grid and the Escape-screen world map now cover the whole world (fog reset once for everyone).
- The moon and night sky are drawn 25x further out and bigger (SkyScale in Moon.h / NightSky.h, about 5 km) so they sit behind the mountains; height fog now reaches 4 km (FogCutoffDistance).

Owner: "Please remove Blackrock and everything in it from the game. It's out of date and we'll put something else in its place soon." Blackrock was the walled village in the original square (Village.cpp). The site is `JeebTerrain::VillageCenter()` = (-2900, -300) cm, a 40 x 40 m square (`VillageHalf()` 2000 cm), west of the river and of the stone bridge from Oak Hill; its sketch spot is (400, 870) in `WorldMap.h FromSketch`. It stays a level pad (`Pads()`), tree-free (`TreeBlocked` 3100 cm) and prop-free (fill zone `OldVillage`, off in FillZones.csv); its roads (PathDefs) and the bridge road still lead to it. Build the new town there.
- Removed: palisade, gates, wall walk, corner towers, fountain (and AVillage's Tick), benches (`Seats()` empty), wall ladders (`Ladders()` empty), the five huts inside (`VillageHuts()` empty), Sir Aldous, villagers 0/2/7 (Mabry, Orsolya, Ansel) and their Townsfolk rows, the fountain-yard fill rows, the HUD walk-in title, the Tab map square, the world-map name (`JeebWorld::Towns`), the fast-travel entry (saves store town names, so a save that knew Blackrock just drops it), NpcLife's Blackrock town.

Quest and dialogue lines that named Blackrock were reworded (Quests, QuestsSteps, QuestsPeople, Townsfolk, Lore.h); no quest, step or key changed, so saves with them in progress load as before. The fill set `Blackrock` is now `HutSide`.

Oak Hill (Store, MarketStall, Smithy, Bank plots) and Steele Creek still have all three, trades checked by the server through their counters (NearTrader). Kept: the market yard road stubs, huts, cottage, pond, pig pen, castle, graveyard. AVillage is now just the huts and seven villagers (no Tick). ## Code layout (split 2026-10-12; function bodies unchanged)

`AKnightHUD` (header `KnightHUD.h`): `KnightHUD.cpp` main DrawHUD, bars, weapon bar, minimap; `KnightHUDGlass.cpp` liquid-glass drawing helpers, buttons, tooltips; `KnightHUDIcons.cpp` Poly/Disc and all item icons; `KnightHUDInventory.cpp` inventory, side panel, badges, drag and drop, drop box, loot windows; `KnightHUDShops.cpp` store, smithy, bank; `KnightHUDScreens.cpp` settings, dictionary, lore, skills screen, dialogue; `KnightHUDText.cpp` HDText, DrawLinesAt, NorseText.

`AKnightCharacter` (header `KnightCharacter.h`): `KnightCharacter.cpp` constructor, BeginPlay, input setup and the big Tick; `...Body.cpp` the suit, cape, shield and weapon holding (`ShieldRest()` is declared in `KnightShieldRest.h`); `...Combat.cpp` attacks, Hurt, block, Die, potions; `...Movement.cpp` sprint, crouch, climb, slide, roll, jump, DoMove; `...Interact.cpp` interaction prompts, doors, horse, seats, gathering; `...Inventory.cpp` hotbar, equip, drops; `...UI.cpp` screens opening/closing, dialogue, graphics settings; `...Fx.cpp` dust and footfall bits; `...Pose.cpp` what the animator sees and where his gear goes (`FillAnimInput`, `TickPose`, `PlaceGear`, test switches); the pose itself is `KnightAnimator.cpp`. Put new code in the matching file. `Tick` (split 2026-10-30) is now a short list of steps (TickHousekeeping, TickRollSlide, TickAttack, TickHealth, TickStamina, TickGear, TickGroundSpeed, TickPose, TickDust); the comment above it says which file each lives in.

Save/load is planned in `Docs/SaveLoadPlan.md` (not built yet).

Packaging: `RunUAT BuildCookRun ... -clientconfig=Shipping -cook -stage -pak -iostore -compressed -archive -archivedirectory=Packaged` (about 355 MB zipped; add `-build -nocompileeditor -unattended`, which works with the editor open; leave out the `.pdb` when sharing). The game loads materials/textures by name (`LoadObject`), which the packager cannot see, so `/Game` and `/Engine/BasicShapes` are always cooked (DefaultGame.ini). Any new asset loaded by name must live under `/Game` (or add its folder there), or it shows as grey checkerboard in the packaged game only. Code must also compile for Shipping (no editor-only fields, e.g. `FStaticMaterial` takes two arguments).

## Never open a visible Unreal window (owner, 2026-11-14; every session, agent, reviewer and script)

The owner plays games on this PC; an Unreal window popping up over them broke their game. Every `-game` / test-picture run uses `-RenderOffScreen` (never `-windowed`, not even as a fallback; if off-screen pictures come out black, stop and ask). Headless servers use `-server`/`-nullrhi`. Any new script, tool or agent brief that launches Unreal must do the same. A project hook (`.claude/settings.json` -> `Tools/hooks/no_visible_unreal.sh`) blocks Claude's commands that would open one. The owner's own `Tools/RunClient.bat` is for real play and keeps its window. Opening the full editor for the owner happens only when they ask.

## Build

The editor must be closed before building (Live Coding is not used). The owner has given standing permission to close a running editor (`Stop-Process -Name UnrealEditor`) whenever a build or material rebuild needs it, without asking, and to reopen it afterwards.

```
& "C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.8\Engine\Build\BatchFiles\Build.bat" JeebslandEditor Win64 Development "C:\Users\johnl\Documents\Unreal Projects\Jeebsland\Jeebsland.uproject" -waitmutex
```

Success ends with `Result: Succeeded`.

`bUseUnity = false` in `Jeebsland.Build.cs` is deliberate: several .cpp files define file-scope helpers with the same names.

## Materials

Foliage and bark materials (`M_Grass`, `M_Bush`, `M_TreeLeaf`, `M_BarkTint`, incl. wind sway) are generated by `Tools/build_materials.py`. Edit that script, then run `Tools/BuildMaterials.ps1` with the editor closed. Don't rerun the old `MakeGrassCards.bat` from Downloads; it rebuilds the materials with a wind bug.

Procedural textures (moon, cirrus, dirt) are painted by `Tools/gen_textures.py` and cached in `Saved/` by version number; bump the version to repaint.

## Palette (2026-11-15)

One world palette, `Content/Data/Palette.ini`, read by grass (our cards and the realistic Fab grass), the ground (M_Ground, M_Dirt, M_Stone), Oak Hill's lawn (M_TL_Ground), tree leaves, needles and bark (near and far), bushes, fallen leaves, water and haze. The guide is `Docs/Palette.md`.
- Five knobs, live in Play: `jeeb.Palette.Autumn|Warmth|Greenness|Saturation|Brightness` (-1..1, 0 = today's look exactly). Also `jeeb.Palette.Reload` and `jeeb.Palette.Set <Name> R G B`.
- The materials grade their own final base colour from `/Game/MPC_JeebPalette`. Plants get a season shift and a matrix, mixed materials grade only their grass layer by season, and earth and bark get only the world matrix. The game writes the matrices when a knob changes (`Source/Jeebsland/Palette.cpp`). Code-set colours come from `JeebPalette::Raw`/`Bind`. The haze and water take `JeebPalette::Graded`.
- Python side: `Tools/palette.py` (`colour`, `grade_plant`, `grade_world`, `ensure_mpc`) and `Tools/BuildPalette.ps1` (the collection, a one-time grade of the committed `/Game/GrassReal` copies, instruction counts).
- Two catches, both in `Docs/Palette.md`:
  - A material may read only two collections, so the Fab grass `M_GrassLibrary` and `M_MeadowFlowers` (whose wind reads two)
    take the grade as `Pal*` parameters set by Meadow.cpp.
  - Never rewrite the collection's list from Python: it gives the parameters new hidden Ids, and every material built before
    loads grey. `ensure_mpc` goes through the C++ `UJeebPaletteTools::SyncVectorDefaults`. `BuildPalette.ps1` checks every
    palette material after a fresh load.
- Not yet on it: buildings (phase 2), M_Color props, the town's weeds and ivy.

## Time of day

`ADayNight` (DayNight.h): the sun circles once every 20 minutes (`HalfLoopSeconds` 600, the server's clock), 41 degrees up at noon (phase 0) and 25 below at midnight (phase 0.5): elevation = 8 + 33 cos(2 pi phase), so the loop grew rather than shifted (2026-11-17; until then 26 + 15 cos, a midnight sun that never set). It sets at phase 0.289 and rises at 0.711: about 11.6 min of day, 8.4 of night. `ADayNight::Dusk` (1 - smoothstep 13..24 degrees) is still 0..1 and now stays 1 all night (lamps, lit windows, grass and water dusk tints); `ADayNight::Night` (1 - smoothstep -10..2) drives the night look and the stars.

**Night lighting (2026-11-17).** Stylised, readable nights (Zelda, Sea of Thieves, Fortnite) rather than real darkness. The handover: the sun's light on the ground (its diffuse and specular scale, in 1/20 steps since each change rebuilds its render state) fades from 4 degrees up to 1 down, and its shadows stop there; the sun stays the sky atmosphere's light 0 for the twilight glow until 16 down, then is hidden (no light pass). The **moonlight** is a second directional light made by `ADayNight::TickMoon`: sky atmosphere light index 1 (lights the night sky and clouds faintly; disc hidden, the moon is `AMoon`), from `MoonDir` (30 degrees up, azimuth 205; was 15), fading in from 1 to 8 degrees down and casting the shadows from 1 down (the same reach and cascades as the sun's, no far cascade), so there is never more than one shadow caster; hidden by day (no cost). Forward shading priority 2 (water, glass, fog take it at night). The armour glint follows it at night. On top: the sky light times `jeeb.Night.Ambient`, exposure plus `jeeb.Night.Exposure` stops, a blue paler grade (`jeeb.Night.Tint`: ColorGain, less saturation, no warm highlights), a blue lift in the shadows (`jeeb.Night.ShadowLift`), the haze a dim blue fixed on screen (divided by the exposure), the sun's haze glow off, the LOW cloud ring and NightSky's painted clouds dimmed and moonlit, trails a moonlit blue-grey. Live settings (LOOK tab, SKY + HAZE, "Night:" sliders): `jeeb.Night.Moon` 0.5, `jeeb.Night.MoonColor` "0.60 0.72 1.00", `jeeb.Night.MoonShadows` 1, `jeeb.Night.Ambient` 2, `jeeb.Night.Exposure` 0.5, `jeeb.Night.Tint` 0.5 (eased in with Night squared, so the golden hour keeps its colour), `jeeb.Night.ShadowLift` 0.012, `jeeb.Night.Sky` 0.5 (the sky atmosphere's SkyLuminanceFactor at night: a deep blue where the stars show), `jeeb.Night.Twilight` 0.6 (extra stops round the horizon, none 7 degrees either side: the handover never dips), `jeeb.Sun.Low` -25. Ramps: sun on the ground smoothstep -1..4 degrees, moon 1 - smoothstep -8..-1, Night 1 - smoothstep -6..8, the sun as sky light until -16. Phases for pictures: 0 noon, 0.2645 sunset glow (5 up), 0.289 sunset, 0.3417 10 down, 0.5 midnight, 0.72 dawn (2 up).

**Sunset haze (2026-11-17, owner: "why does the distant horizon glow grey at sunset?").** The height fog's inscattering colour is a self-lit luminance: it stayed at full strength through sunset while the exposure lifts about 2.5 stops there (LowLift 1.56 + Twilight 0.6 + part of Night.Exposure), so far hills and forest showed a flat haze about 6x brighter than noon's, tone-mapped to grey-white over a dark land. `ADayNight::TickLook` now scales the day haze by the light on the land as a share of noon's (the sun's on the ground, SunGround x sin(elevation), plus a sky term fading from 10 degrees up to 8 down), blended by `jeeb.Look.HazeFollow` (1; 0 the old fixed glow), and tints it toward the low sun's colour by `jeeb.Look.HazeSunTint` (0.5) times Dusk. Noon (41 degrees) is unchanged (the share is 1 there); night still blends to the exposure-compensated moonlit haze; LOW's painted cloud ring takes the fog's colour, so it dims too. LOOK tab, SKY + HAZE: "Sunset haze dims with the light", "Sunset haze takes the sun's colour".

## Meshy: use the API (2026-11-05)

The owner has a paid Meshy account. Reach Meshy through its API, not its website.
- Connector: `.mcp.json` runs Meshy's official MCP server (`@meshy-ai/meshy-mcp-server` through `cmd /c npx`, Node.js is installed) and takes the key from the `MESHY_API_KEY` Windows user environment variable. The key is in no file in this repo; never ask for it in chat, print it or write it to a file. To replace it, have the owner create one at https://www.meshy.ai/settings/api and save it themselves.
- The connector's tools only load when the Claude app starts, so a session opened before the key was saved won't have them. The connector works (`mcp__meshy__*`, load with ToolSearch; free balance check passed 2026-11-05 after an app restart). Its own guide says to confirm every cost first; the owner's credit rule below replaces that.
- No tools, or the app hasn't restarted: read the key with `[Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('MESHY_API_KEY','User')` and call `https://api.meshy.ai` directly with `Authorization: Bearer <key>`. `GET /openapi/v1/balance` is a free check. A 401 reads `Invalid API key`, the placeholder or a bad key being the usual cause.
- Credits (owner, 2026-11-05): spend them whenever a task calls for it, without asking, but be smart about it. Look up what a job costs first (https://docs.meshy.ai/en/api/pricing), use the cheapest option that gives a good result, try a cheap preview or a single piece before a batch, and reuse what already exists. Don't keep retrying a failing job or regenerating on a whim. Say in the report what was spent. Checking the balance is free.
- NEVER reload the account: no buying credits, no upgrading the plan, no changing billing. Only the owner does that.
- Check the balance after each spend. When it hits 0, or is too low for the next job, tell the owner straight away and stop spending; the work waits until they reload it.
- Finished pieces follow "The suit's model" above: into `Art/`, then the usual build and import tools. The owner has been making each piece themselves; the API adds a way to do it, it doesn't replace their say on what gets made.
- **Free library first (owner, 2026-11-11).** Before generating anything, look in Meshy's public community library: its models are CC0 (terms section 3.3) and free to download. Read `Docs/MeshyCommunityLibrary.md` first.
  - Search many synonyms through `https://www.meshy.ai/tags/<word>`.
  - Open pages one at a time: a quick burst of fetches got the site to answer 403 for a while.
  - Use the owner's Chrome (Claude in Chrome, where they're signed in). Never sign in yourself.
  - This applies to decorations too (lanterns, barrels, signs).
- Licence (Meshy's terms of use, section 3.2, last updated 2026-09-19, read 2026-11-06): customers on a paid plan own their output and no credit is required; free-plan output is CC BY 4.0 and needs credit, so the Credit note below still applies to the free-tier pieces.

## Mixamo: use Claude in Chrome (2026-11-05)

Mixamo has no official API, so there is no connector or key. Use Claude in Chrome (`mcp__claude-in-chrome__*`, owner's choice), where downloads save straight to Downloads with no dialog (tested 2026-11-05). The owner is logged in there; their name shows at the top right. Open your own new tab and close it when done. `navigate` works with URLs like `https://www.mixamo.com/#/?page=1&query=breathing%20idle&type=Motion%2CMotionPack`.
- If `list_connected_browsers` is empty, ask the owner to open Chrome with the Claude extension. It only works while Chrome is running on this PC.
- The login can expire. Then ask the owner to log in again in Chrome; they use "Continue with Facebook". Never type their password, and never act on their Facebook account.
- Fallback only: the app's built-in browser is logged in too, but every download there opens a Windows Save dialog that only the owner can click (see below).
- Don't use unofficial Mixamo download scripts or `mixamo-mcp`: they rely on undocumented web behaviour, and it is unconfirmed that Adobe's terms allow them.
- Mixamo opens on whichever character was last chosen, but our clips are downloaded on its stock Y Bot (see "Adding a clip" under "Animation direction"). Check that the viewer's title says "... ON Y BOT" before downloading.
- Download settings (tested 2026-11-05 with Breathing Idle; `Tools/knight_clip_retarget.py` took it fine): FBX Binary, Without Skin (the dialog defaults to With Skin), 30 fps, keyframe reduction none. In Chrome the file lands in `%USERPROFILE%\Downloads` under the clip's name, with `(1)` added if that name is taken, so look for the newest file. This relies on Chrome's "Ask where to save each file" staying off. Rename it and move it to `Art/Knight Chainmail/Clips` only when it is really being added, because the importer reads every `.fbx` in that folder. Then follow the normal "Adding a clip" steps.
- Mixamo clips cost nothing, so sessions can download what a task needs without asking.
- In the built-in browser, the file stays a `.tmp` until the owner clicks Save, and the app has no setting to skip that dialog. The pane also refuses `navigate` while a page-opened pop-up is open in any tab; don't close a pop-up you didn't open.

The pickaxe, longbow, crossbow and potion were made on the paid plan (`Art/Props`): no credit needed. ## Look board (2026-11-15)

`Tools/LookBoard.ps1` (see Docs/HowToTest.md): 12 fixed views at noon and the low sun from one `-ShotEyes` run, tiled 2 views a row with the owner's `Docs/TownLab/Refs` pictures under them (`Saved/LookBoard`); `-Compare` cuts the newest two boards back apart by their fixed layout. No C++ changes. A stale copy binary showed no eyes at all (the run took the single-still path): build the copy first. The field of view is one value per run (80).

Soft-shadow speckle fix (2026-11-15, owner: "glitchy looking shadows near my feet"): the PCSS soft shadows (`r.Shadow.FilterMethod 1`) scatter their samples per pixel and rely on TSR/TAA history to smooth them; where a shadow moves (the walking knight's own, swaying grass) the history is rejected and the raw scatter shows as a dotted dither. Still pictures never show it (the history settles); a walking film does (`-ShotMove=bowwalk -ShotAt=X,Y -ShotCmds=...`). Fix: `r.Shadow.MaxSoftKernelSize` 12 on HIGH, 6 on LOW (engine default 40 shadow-map texels), set in `ApplyGraphics`; branch shadows stay soft. PCF (`FilterMethod 0`) also cures it but makes far trunk shadows hard. Test switches added: `-ShotCmds=a~1;b~2` (console commands at 3 s, after ApplyGraphics) and a 9th `-ShotEyes` field with the same form, per eye.

## Look panel (2026-11-15)

The Escape screen's LOOK tab (`KnightHUDLook.cpp`, `#if !UE_BUILD_SHIPPING`) tunes 40 console variables live: `jeeb.Look.*`, `jeeb.Cloud.*`, `jeeb.Water.*`, `jeeb.Grass.*`, `jeeb.Ground.SkyFill`. The list is the `Items[]` table at the top of the file (group, label, cvar, min, max, deferred); add a row to add a slider. RGB string cvars get one slider per number (grass tint) or a brightness multiplier (water glow). Water cvars whose default is "the material's" (negative) use the numbers from `Tools/water_material.py` as their default. "Default" on the panel is the cvar's value when the tab was first opened in that run. Presets: `Saved/LookPresets/<name>.ini`; `<name>_defaults.txt` lists old -> new defaults. Name typing reuses the Slate key catcher (`bLookTyping`, like the test kit's level box). Test: `-ShotLookPanel` (JEEBLOOK lines).

## Tree groves (2026-11-15; TEMPORARY test setting)

Owner: "Can you make a few small forests with the trees and bushes so I can pick?" then "make the testing forests in Field so that it has grass too". `Source/Jeebsland/TreeGroves.cpp` (called from `AKnightGameState::BuildWorld`, line marked GROVES, after the woods and before the autumn decor) plants a row of small groves (about 23 m across, 13 m between) in the real world on open meadow east of Oak Hill, running north-south, each under a plank sign on its west side facing the town: Spruce Forest (Project Nature), DZ Sample Trees, Megaplants English Oak, Megaplants Hornbeam, and "OUR OWN TREES" (the woods' oaks and pines, as `AForest::AddTree` plants them). Open World Foliage (PN_OpenWorldFoliage) is only grass and flowers, so it has no grove. The site is picked at load from a short list of candidates (the first where every grove is dry, level, off roads, buildings, the river valley and Oak Hill's box); log `JEEBGROVE site` gives the row's centre, `JEEBGROVE <n>` each grove's tree count and kinds. Nothing in it is solid (the trees from the packs have no collision; the world's own are choppable as always). Each tree records a canopy (`Canopy.h`) so the real grass thins under it and, for broadleaves, the autumn decor drops fallen leaves in its colour (conifers record a canopy with Color -1: thinner grass, no leaves; `AutumnDecor.cpp` skips them). The DZ aspens and cork oaks get autumn through twin leaf materials in `/Game/TreeGroves` (`Tools/grove_materials.py`: the pack's own leaf instance with its static "Use Fall Setup?" on; never cooked) and a random fall tint (red, orange, amber, gold). The same was tried for the Megaplants (their master has a static "Seasons" switch, "Season Color 1/2", "Health Offset", "BaseColor Tint"), but their leaves stayed summer green in the pictures whatever was set (a Nanite skeletal mesh may ignore the component material override, or the season needs per-leaf health data the USD import lacks): the oak and hornbeam show green with autumn leaves on the ground. Spruce stays evergreen.
**Four DZ forests, solid trunks (2026-11-16, owner: "four forests of DZ sample trees, one green, red, orange, and yellow ... tone back the brightness ... three sliders to adjust their rgb"; then "the knight can't run through them and arrows stick to them"):** the one DZ grove became DZ GREEN, RED, ORANGE and YELLOW (2 cork oaks, 4 columnar aspens each; palms and pine dropped). Why every tree showed every colour: the DZ leaf master (`M_Leaves_Needles`) makes its fall look as an Overlay blend of the leaf picture with `Fall Color Tint` x a mosaic texture (`Fall Overlay Image`, T_Fall_Colors_1: red, orange, yellow, green leaf by leaf), desaturated by `-Fall Color Amount` (1 doubled the saturation, hence the glow). `Tools/grove_materials.py` now sets the mosaic to `/Engine/EngineResources/WhiteSquareTexture`, `Base Color Desaturation` 1 (grey leaf picture, so its shading gives the light and dark shades), `Fall Color Amount` 0, `Fall Color Power` 2; the tint alone sets the colour (linear, about 0.45 x tint reaches the screen as albedo). One dynamic instance per leaf kind per forest (`GForestMids`), shared by its trees, recoloured from the `jeeb.Trees.Dz*` cvars' change delegates; LOOK tab group TREES. The green forest records canopy colour -1 (no fallen leaves). Trunks: a `BlockAll` capsule (camera ignored) per tree, built on every machine including the dedicated server, radius and crown height per mesh in `TrunkOf()` (measured from bark vertices 20-80 cm up and the lowest leaves); spruce saplings 01-04 have none. `-JeebShot` logs a Visibility trace across each grove's first trunk.
**Remove them:** in a build, `jeeb.Test.Groves 0` (console, or `jeeb.Test.Groves=0` under `[SystemSettings]` in `Config/DefaultEngine.ini`) leaves them out at the next load, no build. For good: delete `TreeGroves.h/.cpp`, the `JeebGroves::Build` line and include in `KnightGameState.cpp`, and the `Sp.Color < 0` line in `AutumnDecor.cpp`. They are compiled out of Shipping builds, so a package never has them (the asset folders are never cooked until chosen).

**Spruce saplings as flat tan cards (2026-11-16, owner: "these two smaller pine trees never seem to want to load in"):** a fault in the Project Nature Spruce Forest pack, not loading, streaming or Nanite (none of its meshes are Nanite). Several lower LODs point their needle section at material slot 0 or the branch slot (the master's bark or branch path, which has no opacity mask), so the needle cards draw as solid bark-coloured quads. Saplings `spruce_small_02` from LOD 1, `03` LOD 1-2, `04` LOD 1; young spruces `spruce_small_05/06/07` at LOD 3; half-grown `spruce_half_01/03/04` at LOD 3 (mid-distance). Each mesh's LOD 0 only shows at screen size 1.0, so in play the saplings were almost always on their broken LOD 1. Fix in `TreeGroves.cpp` (`SpruceFix`): a Spruce Forest tree with a LOD that has no leaf or imposter material holds LOD 1 if that is whole, else LOD 0 (`SetForcedLodModel`; saplings 133-206 triangles, young spruces 1.3k-3.2k, half-grown 10k-14k). `jeeb.Test.SpruceFix 0` shows the pack as shipped (live); log `JEEBSPRUCE`. `r.ForceLOD 3` near the grove shows the broken LODs. Real fix, before these trees go into the world (the editor, with the pack open; we never edit `Content/PN_*` from agents): in the Static Mesh editor's LOD sections, set the broken LOD's needle section to the leaf material slot: `spruce_small_02` LOD 1, 2, 3, `spruce_small_03` LOD 1, 2 and `spruce_small_04` LOD 1 to slot 1 (`21 - Default`, `_leaf`); `spruce_small_05/06/07` LOD 3 (one section, on slot 0) needs the leaf slot 1 too, or that LOD deleted; `spruce_half_01/03/04` LOD 3 section 1 from slot 1 (`_branch`) to slot 2 (`_leaf`). Then `SpruceFix` can go.

LOOK defaults = Save1_20261115 (owner, 2026-11-16): every built-in cvar default now equals that preset (jeeb.Sun.Phase, Water.Flow -1 and Knight.Gravity -1 left: time of day / "use the built-in value"); the red DZ forest 15% darker (jeeb.Trees.DzRed 0.425 0.0595 0.02975); new jeeb.Trees.Spruce (needle tint, default 1 1 1; Leaf Tint / Branch Tint / imposter Tint on dynamic instances in TreeGroves.cpp, TintSpruce) with three TREES sliders.

## Oak Hill's trees and the forest round it (2026-11-16)

Owner: "Delete the big oak tree from the middle of the town square ... Full delete"; "replace all the trees in oak hill that we created with trees we've added from the packs ... a mix of colors ... roughly a 2-1 ratio of fat to skinny trees"; "fill in the trees for the 500 meters surrounding oak hill with dense forest using combinations of all trees (including the spruce) ... boulders, roads, and other natural elements".

- **Plaza oak deleted:** `BuildPlazaOak`/`LoadPlazaOak` (OakHillTown and TownLabTown), its trunk capsule, `Content/Trees/{M_PlazaOak,SM_PlazaOak,SM_PlazaOakShadow,T_PlazaOak_Base}`, `Art/Trees/PlazaOak*.glb`, `Tools/BuildPlazaOak.ps1`, `plaza_oak_clean.py`, `plaza_oak_import.py`; the switches `-NoPlazaOak` and `-OakFill=` went with it. `AOakHillTown::PlantTrees` and `ATownLabTown::PlantTrees` (our choppable oaks and pines round the town) are gone too. Left for the fill's owner: Fill.cpp's "plaza oak roots" keep-clear circle and a test row in `Tools/FillTestData/FillOakHill.csv`.
- **`AOakForest` (OakForest.h/.cpp, spawned in `AKnightGameState::BuildWorld` after AForest and the groves, before AutumnDecor and AWorldFill):** town trees at FillTrees.csv's spots (conifer = spruce, street = aspen, orchard and shade = cork oak) and at the old PlantTrees spots (same seed 5150 and tests), each oak's crown kept off the buildings (shrinks, or becomes an aspen); the forest on a 6.5 m jittered lattice (seed 9090) everywhere within `JeebOakForest::Reach` (500 m) of the plaza except the original square + 12 m, the town box + 25 m, water, the rivers' banks + 12 m, slopes over 0.5 and 4 m round the roads; glades by noise, thicker in hollows; spruce fields; aspen clumps; undergrowth (seed 9191: TownDecor bushes, the spruce sapling small_01, mossy logs, four stumps) and boulder clumps (seed 9292: TownDecor's SM_BoulderMossy big and small, SM_RockPale). Colours: two slow noises give four colour patches (80-150 m), one tree in eight another colour, using the groves' `jeeb.Trees.Dz*` tints and `/Game/TreeGroves` leaf materials (OakForest registers the cvars itself in Shipping, where TreeGroves.cpp is compiled out). Town broadleaves drop fallen leaves in their colour (AutumnDecor via JeebCanopy); forest trees thin the grass under them but drop none.
- **Online:** deterministic, built on every machine; the server builds only the hidden solids (`ForestTrunks` cylinders sized per kind, `ForestLogs` boxes, `ForestStumpsRocks` cylinders; BlockAll, camera ignored). Log `JEEBFOREST ... hash=` to compare machines. `jeeb.Forest.Density` changes the solids: same value everywhere.
- **Roads:** `JeebOakForest::RoadLines()` walks 15 m steps from the north road end (toward Silver Point), a fork off it 40 m past the town (toward Anchorage) and the road behind the bank (toward Birch Grove, stops at the river's east branch), choosing the least climb and turn, off water, banks, the square and the plots; then `JeebTerrain::SmoothPath`. They are `FPathDef` Kind 2 in `JeebTerrain::PathDefs` (Kind 0 Blackrock, 1 Oak Hill cobbles), cut into ~100 m pieces, so grass, fill (`Fill.cpp` now asks `JeebTerrain::ForestRoadNear`) and trees keep off them; Paths.cpp draws their ribbons on `JeebTerrain::DrawnHeight`. The ribbons now show (fixed the same day: see "Dirt roads, the forest on the map, texture caps" below).
- **Frame cost:** every tree casting CSM shadows cost about 7 ms in the forest (Nanite masked leaves in every cascade). The drawn tree sets cast none; each has a hidden twin (`bCastHiddenShadow`) refilled every 8 m of camera movement with the trees within `jeeb.Forest.ShadowReach` (40 m HIGH, 20 m LOW, set in `ApplyGraphics`). Measured (editor, solo, 1080p, GPU ms and draws; before = the old woods and the plaza oak): plaza HIGH 7.67/1357 -> 7.64/1268, LOW 5.81/1158 -> 4.73/1078; forest north (20000, 24000) HIGH 5.89/405 -> 10.86/534, LOW 3.85/389 -> 5.24/497; forest east (-9000, 50000) HIGH 5.86/420 -> 9.02/565, LOW 4.06/409 -> 5.60/533; game thread unchanged (about 4 ms). Without any tree shadows the forest costs about +1.3 ms on HIGH; shadow reach 60 m +5 ms, 40 m +3.5, 25 m +1.9. `jeeb.Forest.Reach` (draw distance) barely matters (Nanite). Build time about 0.6 s at load.
- **Cook:** DefaultGame.ini cooks only the pack folders in use (see its comment); `Tools/forest_dump.py` lists the used set's dependencies and flags any in a never-cooked folder.
- **Groves:** `jeeb.Test.Groves` now defaults to 0; the forest grows where they stood.
- **Hills round Oak Hill (2026-11-16, owner: "add hills similar to the hills we have in the oak hill town and apply them to the full surrounding area where we added trees ... to cut off the horizon"):** `Hills.h/.cpp` (namespace JeebHills). `JeebTerrain::HeightAt` adds `JeebHills::Offset` to the land before `SeaAndRivers`, so every river keeps its channel, banks and bank cap (HeightAt's new 4th argument: 2 as built, 1 the raw hills, 0 none). Shape: a ring term rising from 80 to 320 m from the plaza (15 m base plus up to 15 m more where a broad two-octave Perlin is high: valleys and saddles about 200-300 m apart) plus small bumps of up to 9 m everywhere; times a keep-out mask: none within 30 m of the town's box (full by 140 m), of the original square + 15 m (full by 120 m), of the rivers' water + 30 m (full by 200 m; measured with its own distance, since `JeebWorld::RiverDistance` skips a river 70 m past its bounds and that made a 71 degree cliff), and fading out between 470 and 620 m from the plaza. The land and hills together ease under 56 m world height (TopZ, a soft knee over 8 m): M_Ground paints snow on gentle ground from 60 m (SnowFrom), and the north-east, where the land already climbs toward the mountains, grew a white snowfield on a forested hill. Highest hill 34.5 m over the land, highest ground 55.7 m; slopes (8 m mesh, ring): 0.6% over 27 degrees, none over 35, steepest 33. The ground stands about 30 m over the plaza 250-500 m out (up to 45-49 m W, E and NE, where the land itself rises). The 8 m mesh sags up to 94 cm from HeightAt in places, the same without the hills (logged both ways). The forest roads are routed on the raw hills (`RoadLines` uses HeightAt(...,1): they found valleys, steepest now 0.8-2.8 degrees), then each road's corridor is levelled in the grid to a profile no steeper than 5 degrees (land and hills together: the mean of the highest and lowest 5-degree lines through the road's own heights), flat 4 m from the middle and easing back over 3.5x the cut or fill (12-45 m), roads mixed by w/(1-w) weights so the fork stays smooth. Worked out once into a 4 m grid over 640 m round the plaza (110 ms, game thread, `JeebHills::Prepare` first in BuildWorld), bilinear lookups. TownLab map: the lab site is masked at lookup. `jeeb.Hills.Scale` (or `-HillScale=`) read once per process, in editor and test builds only (Shipping is always 1: online review, the scale shapes solid ground); 0 is the old land exactly. `JEEBHILLS stats` also logs a finished-ground hash of the ring's 8 m heights after the rivers and towns. Log `JEEBHILLS grid ... hash=` and `JEEBHILLS stats` (slopes, the ground over the plaza by direction, the 8 m mesh's sag from the true heights). **Pass 2 (2026-11-16, coordinator: the first hills were barely visible; replaces the shape numbers above):** the ring now rises from 30 to 160 m from the plaza (18 m base + up to 15 m where the broad Perlin is high), plus ridges (up to 11 m along the zero line of a 170 m Perlin, gated by another) and bumps up to 8 m; keep-outs start 10 m past the town box (full by 90 m), 15 m past the square (full by 120 m), 30 m from rivers (full by 170 m). After the roads, an 8-neighbour chamfer pass lowers any grid point steeper than 35 degrees (0.7) over the plain land, then a 3x3 blur; the soft snow cap is now 57.5 m (a 6 m knee). The forest's slope limits went up with it (trees 0.72, undergrowth 0.75, rocks 0.8), and trunks, undergrowth and boulders sink by the slope. Road routing weighs grade 6 (was 14: with taller hills the north road wandered 740 m south-east and the east road copied it), and a road that forks off an earlier one starts at that road's height, eased in over 60 m. Result: highest hill 45.2 m, highest ground 57.6 m, slopes 5.1% over 27 degrees, 0.15% over 35, steepest 36; ground over the plaza 250-500 m out up to 35-51 m in every direction (dips to the river valleys); roads 6.1 / 4.0 / 3.9 degrees. Timing (editor, GPU ms, plaza/north/east): HIGH 7.38/10.56/9.09 before, 7.41/9.00/7.35 after; LOW 5.14/5.72/5.99 before, 5.18/4.62/5.22 after. Hashes standalone = server: grid 8138b9ce, ground 01668613, forest 685f37f9, fill a9002694.
  - Forest on slopes (OakForest.cpp): trees sink by slope x 45 cm x scale, bushes and stones by half their footprint x slope, logs only on slopes under 0.2 (171 logs instead of 274); the 0.5 slope limit for trees is unchanged (the hills stay under it).
  - Online: hills, forest and fill hashes equal on a standalone client and a dedicated server (2026-11-16).


**Far shadows and the cheaper forest (2026-11-16, owner: "Why aren't the trees that are kind of far away not have shadows? ... Looks too bright then when I get closer it turns dark"; then "soft and blurry far shadows are fine", "I don't care if the distant trees don't blow in the wind"):**
- Cause: the trees cast shadows only within `jeeb.Forest.ShadowReach` (40 m), so past it the ground, grass and crowns were fully sunlit; at the 11-degree low sun a 15 m tree's shadow is 75 m long, so even near ground lost the shade of trees just past the reach. The grass casts no shadows itself; it only receives them. The sun's shadow range is 200 m (the map's light) x `r.Shadow.DistanceScale` 0.6 = 120 m on HIGH, 100 m on LOW (log `JEEBSHADOW`).
- Fix: shadow proxies. `AOakForest` keeps one hidden instanced set per tree kind (`ShadeSets`) of a stand-in mesh built at run time (`MakeShadeMesh`: an egg crown, or a cone for spruces, from where the trunk ends to the mesh top, x `jeeb.Forest.FarShadeSize` 0.75, plus a six-sided trunk; 32-112 triangles, the engine's default material, `bCastHiddenShadow`, `bCastFarShadow`). `UpdateShadows` fills it, on the same 8 m camera step as the leafy twins, with every tree between `ShadowReach` and `jeeb.Forest.FarShadeReach`. `ADayNight` gives the sun one far cascade to `jeeb.Shadow.Far` m that only far-shadow casters (the stand-ins) draw into. Real shadows, so they track the time of day by themselves; the colour-blend hack (darkening far foliage by distance from the sun's height) was not built: it would also darken open meadows, and the grass pack masters live in `Content/PN_*`, which no copy may save.
- Defaults (ApplyGraphics): HIGH ShadowReach 20, FarShadeReach 400, Shadow.Far 400, `r.Shadow.MaxCSMResolution` 1024 (was 2048); LOW ShadowReach 15, FarShadeReach 100, no far cascade; both `r.Nanite.MaxPixelsPerEdge` 2. Cvar defaults: `jeeb.Forest.SpruceLod` 2 (the spruces' LOD switch sizes x2, a run-time edit of the loaded meshes' `ScreenSize`, never saved; LODs 24k/12k/5k/3k triangles and a 4-triangle impostor), `jeeb.Forest.TwinLod` 1 (spruce shadow twins forced to LOD1), `jeeb.Grass.FarThin` 0.5 (tier 2, 32-50 m: half the clumps, sqrt(2) wider).
- Measured (editor, solo, 1080p, GPU ms, sun creeping; plaza / forest north / forest east). HIGH old defaults about 6.9 / 9.8 / 8.8. Doubling the real reach to 80 m: +1.5 / +2.2 / +5.3. Doubling the shadow range (`r.Shadow.DistanceScale` 1.2): within noise (+0.2). Stand-ins to 200 m at 40 m reach: within noise of the old. Stand-ins to 400 m plus the far cascade: +0.2-0.5. Real reach 25 with stand-ins: -0.1 to -1.1. Shadow map 1024: -0.3 to -1.2. One cascade: -0.4 to -1.5 (not used: too coarse near). Reach 15: -0.9 in the forest. Nanite 2 px per edge: -0.4 to -0.75. SpruceLod 2: -0.1 to -0.7. TwinLod 1, FarThin 0.5: within noise singly. `jeeb.Sun.Step` 0.25 (sun direction in steps so CSM caching holds): no gain. Virtual shadow maps with every tree casting (`r.Shadow.Virtual.Enable 1`, `jeeb.Forest.ShadowAll 1`, sway off past 30 m): 48.8 / 12.9 / 13.5 (26.8 / 13.2 / 12.7 with Sun.Step); VSM near + stand-ins far: 18.3 / 11.7 / 11.5: VSM is out (the sun moves every frame, so its page cache never holds, and Nanite masked leaves fill every page). Distance field shadows (`-ini:Engine:[/Script/Engine.RendererSettings]:r.GenerateMeshDistanceFields=True`, `jeeb.Shadow.DF` 300, every tree casting, CSM cut to 0.2): +2.3 / +9.4 / +9.0 and the same dark look as the stand-ins; they would also need distance fields built for every mesh: out. Final (performance-reviewer, two samples each): HIGH 7.05 -> 6.73 / 9.16 -> 6.96 / 8.50 -> 6.74; LOW 5.08 -> 4.82 / 5.61 -> 5.16 / 5.53 -> 5.00; game thread unchanged.
- Look: at noon the near view barely changes; at the low sun the forest floor is now mostly shade (it really is; the sunny gaps came from missing shadows) and far crowns get a lit and a shaded side. Stand-in sizes 0.6 to 0.85 look the same in the dense forest. Pictures: `Saved/Shots/shade_*_before|after.png` in the townbuild3 copy.
- Not built: grass with darker roots or a far ground colour match (the grass pack masters are in `Content/PN_*`; `Tools/BuildGrassPacks.ps1` must run from the main folder), Nanite leaf fallbacks for the DZ trees (MaxPixelsPerEdge was the lever found). Known: `UpdateShadows` refills a few thousand stand-ins every 8 m of camera movement; no hitch was measured, but walking films were not checked for a one-frame spike.
**Soft hand-over of the near tree shadows (2026-11-17, owner: "the trees/environment seems to blink when new shadows are applied to the trees near the knight ... make these phase in and out instead of all or none"):**
- Cause: at `jeeb.Forest.ShadowReach` a tree's shadow swapped in one frame between the plain stand-in (`jeeb.Forest.FarShadeSize` 0.75, a solid egg or cone that also darkens the crown it sits in) and its leafy twin, every 8 m of camera movement. Shadow depth is all or nothing per texel, so the fade is done with the stand-in only.
- How: `AOakForest` has a second stand-in set per kind (`BandSets`, same runtime mesh) in `M_TreeShade` (masked; `build_tree_shade` in `Tools/build_materials.py`, one MID per kind in `BandMids`). Its material measures each instance's flat distance from the camera (`FadeCenter`, set every frame in `UpdateBandFade`) per vertex (a VertexInterpolator: ObjectPositionWS read in the pixel shader did not fade at all) and keeps that share of its shadow-map texels (screen noise; the sun's shadow filter and TSR turn it into a partial shade), and grows the stand-in by WPO about its crown's middle (`Anchor`, local). Distances (`BandFor`, R = reach): the twins and the band refill every 2 m (`NearStep`), the far stand-ins every 8 m (`FarStep`); the stand-in is whole and grown to `jeeb.Forest.ShadowFadeSize` from R - 2 m out (so it covers the twin's swap, which happens within 2 m of R), fades out from there down to R - 2 m - `jeeb.Forest.ShadowFade`, and shrinks back to the far size by R + 14 m, where the far sets (default material, from R + 6 m by their own refill) take over inside it. `jeeb.Forest.ShadowFade 0` is the old hard swap exactly (everything on the 8 m step).
- Defaults: ShadowFade 6 m (HIGH: pure leafy shadows within 12 m, mixed 12-18 m, grown stand-in 18-34 m), ShadowFadeSize 1.1 (1.6 popped again as it shrank). `jeeb.Forest.ShadowLog` 1 logs each refill (`JEEBFOREST refill frame ... cam ...`), 2 also leaves the twins out to see the stand-ins alone; every forced refill logs `JEEBFOREST shadows:` counts.
- Check (Field, free camera along the north road from 2190 20250 yaw 36 at 3 m/s, low sun 0.15, 200 frames each; share of pixels changing by over 25/255 frame to frame): the old swap showed two single-frame jumps (at its 8 m refills), the fade none; ShadowFadeSize 1.6 showed four. Frame cost (editor, solo, 1080p, GPU ms, three still forest views, sun creeping): before 5.52 / 6.32 / 7.79, after 5.48 / 6.23 / 7.32, same build with ShadowFade 0 5.53 / 6.03 / 7.53: within noise; game thread unchanged (3.5-4.5 ms). Pictures: `Saved/Shots/keep/shadowfade_*` in the look copy.

**Seamless hand-over: see-through stand-ins and shrinking twins (2026-11-18, owner: walking up to a tree at noon "it lights up brighter and then adds a shadow ... make the low render and high render shadows mesh more seamlessly"):**
- Measured (shadow copy): one cork oak (18203 21453) seen from 16 m (camera 19121 20142, 2.5 m up, yaw 125), exposure fixed (`ShowFlag.EyeAdaptation 0`). `jeeb.Forest.ShadowReach` was swept from 30 to 6 m, so the tree passed through every stage of the hand-over with the picture otherwise identical, and the mean brightness of its ground shade and crown was compared. Before: the ground shade went from 43 (leafy) to 29-32 (the grown solid stand-in: about a third darker and much wider), and the crown jumped from 39 to 46 at noon (19 to 34 at the low sun) in one step where the twin was swapped. The leafy twin shades its own crown; the stand-in barely does at any size (0.75-1.1) or density (0.4-1). The 2026-11-17 fade covered only the ground. LOW showed the same (ground 50 to 38, crown 45 to 52 in one step). Cascades, Nanite LOD and contact shadows (off: `jeeb.Look.Contact` 0) played no part: the leafy shade looked the same at every distance within the reach.
- Fix: (1) the stand-ins are see-through at every distance: `jeeb.Forest.ShadeDensity` 0.7 (`M_TreeShade` keeps that share of the stand-in's surface in fixed 20 cm world cells; the trunk stays solid, UV x 1). They are full crown size (`jeeb.Forest.FarShadeSize` 1, was 0.75 solid), and the far sets use `M_TreeShade` too (`FarMid`, never faded), so near and far match. (2) No growing (`jeeb.Forest.ShadowFadeSize` 0; above 0 is the old grow). (3) `jeeb.Forest.TwinShrink` 1: inside the fade each leafy twin shrinks about its crown's middle to sqrt(1 - the share the stand-in has faded in) (`ShrinkTwins`, every 10 cm of camera movement, only the twins in the fade ring, kept last in their set: `TwinRing`), so it is a speck when it is swapped out. The twins are plain instanced sets now (the spruces' were hierarchical). The stand-in trunk is 0.7 of the real one's radius (as wide, it shaded the real trunk's sunny side at the low sun). `jeeb.Forest.ShadowLog 3` lists lone trees near the camera (for tests like this).
- After: ground shade 41-46 at every stage at noon (leafy 41), 26.5 throughout at the low sun, 49-52 on LOW; the crown now eases over the 6 m fade instead of jumping (noon 41 to 47, low sun 19 to 35, LOW 45 to 52). It still ends lighter past the reach, since a stand-in cannot shade its own crown like leaves do. Rough frame times (ordinary run, not solo; forest north and east at noon, sun creeping, two samples each; "before" = the old settings in the same build): HIGH GPU 5.32/5.42/5.43/5.39 before, 5.29/5.40/5.35/5.37 after; LOW 4.38/4.19/4.44/4.24 before, 4.35/4.22/4.38/4.19 after; game thread unchanged. Not checked: walking films frame by frame, the packaged game, Steele Creek's trees (same system).
- `M_TreeShade` alone: `Tools/BuildMaterials.ps1` rebuilds every material; the shadow copy built it alone by running `build_materials.py` up to its first top-level build and then calling `build_tree_shade()`.

**Leafy close-up tree shadows removed; tree shade in sun flecks; dark spruce cones (2026-11-21, agent shadows; owner: "set both of these as default settings and remove the sliders. In other words, I want the expensive shadows taken out of the game. I like how they look, but the transition from cheap to quality shadows doesn't look good. If there's any cheap way to imitate the good shadow look please add it"):**
- Gone, on HIGH and LOW: the hidden leafy shadow twins (`ShadowSets`), the near band of stand-ins and its fade (`BandSets`, `BandMids`, `UpdateBandFade`, `ShrinkTwins`, `BandFor`), and the console variables `jeeb.Forest.NearShadows`, `ShadowReach`, `ShadowFade`, `ShadowFadeSize`, `TwinShrink`, `TwinLod` (ApplyGraphics no longer sets ShadowReach). The two LOOK sliders (TREES: "Close-up leafy tree shadows", "Spruces: simpler models sooner") are gone. Everything in the three sections above about the hand-over is history. `AOakForest::UpdateShadows` now only refills one stand-in set per tree kind with the trees within `jeeb.Forest.FarShadeReach` (400 m HIGH, 200 m LOW), from the camera out, every 8 m of camera movement. `M_TreeShade` lost its fade and grow parameters and its world position offset (FadeIn, FadeWidth, FadeCenter, GrowK, GrowFrom, GrowWidth, Anchor, Cell).
- `jeeb.Forest.SpruceLod` stays as a console variable, default 0.5: the left end of the slider the owner had it on (the slider ran 0.5 to 3; its label read "1 pack, 2 faster", so the left end is the fuller models kept to about twice the pack's distances, not the faster setting). 1 is the pack's own, 2 the faster one.
- The imitation: `M_TreeShade`'s holes. The stand-in was see-through as an even 20 cm grain, which the shadow filter turned into a flat grey blob. Now the holes are soft blobs about `jeeb.Forest.Dapple` cm wide (70) with smaller ones mixed in: three octaves of value noise in world space on the stand-in's own surface (x2.43 finer and x0.55 weaker each), cut at the level that keeps the share `jeeb.Forest.ShadeDensity` (0.6, was 0.7; threshold 0.5 + 0.124 x logit(D) / 1.702, checked by a quick count: the kept share is within 2% of D from 0.3 to 0.9). The sun comes through in flecks and the shade's edge is ragged, most near the rim (there a sun ray crosses the shell's front and back at nearly the same place, so about 1 - D gets through; through the middle about (1 - D) squared). It is fixed in the world and the same at every distance, so nothing is handed over; only the shadow map's own sharpness changes with distance (HIGH's second cascade and LOW's single one show softer flecks). `jeeb.Forest.Dapple 0` is the old even grain. Both are live (`ShadeMid`, one dynamic instance for every kind). Tried: 90 cm two-octave (blobby, like camouflage), 110 and 140 cm (too few, large), density 0.7 (centre of each shade still solid), 0.5 (too thin). Not built: a sun light function (it would dapple open meadows too) and a ground-material pattern under canopies (needs a canopy map in M_Ground and M_Grass).
- Rebuild `M_TreeShade` alone with `Tools/build_tree_shade_only.py` (it runs the defining part of `build_materials.py`, then `build_tree_shade()`; `BuildMaterials.ps1` resaves every material). A commandlet run waits at start-up while any other copy's build runs, even with `-Multiprocess -LiveCoding=false` (it queries UnrealBuildTool, which waits for the other build).
- Checked (shadows copy, editor build, 1280x720 stills): forest north-east of Oak Hill and the town from the air and from 3 m up, noon and the low sun (phase 0.2), HIGH and LOW, the same build with `Dapple 0; ShadeDensity 0.7` as "before". A film on HIGH and LOW with the camera turning 15 degrees a second and sliding 1.5 m a second at 60 frames a second: the flecks keep their place and edges from frame to frame, no smear. The old leafy shadows for reference were shot from the idle `shadow` copy (commit 7b05884). Pictures: `Saved/Review/Shadows/` in the main folder. Not checked: a packaged game, a walking knight's own film, Steele Creek's trees (same system).
- **Spruce cones (owner, same day: "less orange and more dark brown/green ... I don't need a slider"):** the cones are painted into the Spruce Forest pack's needle texture `Leaf_A_02` (two cones on the sheet) and baked into the far cards' colour textures; the needle tint `jeeb.Trees.Spruce` (0.51 0.84 0, no blue) turned their brown bright orange. The pack's master material `MA_Summer` samples `Leaf_A_02` with a plain texture node, not a parameter, so a dynamic instance cannot swap it. `Tools/BuildSpruceCones.ps1` (with `Tools/spruce_cones.py`; editor closed; once per machine, like the grass packs) exports `Leaf_A_02` and the four far-card albedos in use, repaints every strongly orange texel (hue 8-38 degrees, saturation over 0.56, soft at both ends) to a dark brown (sRGB 84 68 50 times the texel's own brightness), brings them in as `/Game/TreeGroves/SpruceCones/<name>_Cones`, and copies the needle materials there: `MA_Summer_Cones` (the copy's texture node swapped), `<tree>_MA_Cones` and `<tree>_leaf_Cones` re-parented onto it. The pack's files are untouched; the folder is git-ignored (about 100 MB, made from a paid pack). At run time `JeebTreeLook::ConeLeaf` (TreeLook.cpp) gives each spruce's needle slot its `_leaf_Cones` copy and `DarkCones` swaps the far cards' `Albedo`, in `OakForest.cpp` and `TreeGroves.cpp`; without the folder the pack's cones show and the log warns once (`JEEBSPRUCE no ...`). A first rule (saturation over 0.3) also repainted the far cards' dull brown padding and bare trunk: keep the saturation floor high. The sprigs' orange-brown twigs go a little darker with the cones. Agent copies get the pack by link but not this folder: run the script in a copy that needs dark cones. Before packaging: run it in the main folder, or the package ships orange cones.
- **Far spruce cards matched to the near models; SpruceLod back to 2 (2026-11-21 evening, agent spruce; owner: "adjust the picture of the far away spruce to be more similar to the actual spruce ... too much detail and bright orange pinecones ... so we can still use the faster one"):** `Tools/BuildSpruceCones.ps1` now also repaints the far cards' bright yellow cone tips (hue 34-62, saturation over 0.55, brightness over `$FarLo` 110) to the cone brown and softens the far-card colour with a 3-texel box blur on the 4096 sheet (`$FarBlur`; the needle texture untouched). New live `jeeb.Forest.SpruceFar` "brightness saturation glow" (default `1.2 0.9 1`) multiplies MA_Imposter's own Brightness, Saturation and SS Strength on the forest's far-card instances (OakForest.cpp `ApplySpruceFar`; TreeGroves.cpp's spruces not changed): the cards had read darker and crisper than the near models, which are a lighter yellow-green. `jeeb.Forest.SpruceLod` default 2. Measured (spruce copy, editor, uqueue solo, HIGH, 1280x720, the Steele Creek stand below, each twice): 0.5 GPU 6.56 / 6.65 ms, 2 GPU 5.63 / 5.69: about -0.95 ms. Comparison pictures use `jeeb.Forest.SpruceLod 0.25` (all near models) against `8` (all far cards); `r.ForceLOD` does not reach the instanced spruces. Rerun BuildSpruceCones.ps1 on every machine and before packaging.
- **Round 2, same evening (owner, with pictures: the far card "a blurry solid cone, too yellow-green", the near "airy drooping fronds with sky showing through, darker"; "Is there any way to transition the trees from low to high without it being so obvious with them blinking to a different shape?"):** (1) the blur is off (`$FarBlur 0`); (2) the far cards' opacity sheets (`<tree>_imposter_O`, red channel) are exported too and cut where the card's colour, smoothed to frond size (radius/6), is darker than its surroundings (radius `$AirRadius` 60; soft from `$AirLo` 0.82 to `$AirHi` 0.97 of the local mean): 3-17% of the opacity goes, in frond-sized gaps; DarkCones swaps `Masks` too; (3) `jeeb.Forest.SpruceFar` default `0.9 0.8 0.8` (darker, less yellow; the earlier 1.2 suited aerial views, the owner's ground view wanted darker); (4) the LOD change crossfades: the copied master `MA_Summer_Cones` has Dithered LOD Transition on (MA_Imposter had it already), and the trunk and branch instances now get `_Cones` copies on it too (ConeLeaf takes `_branch`/`_trunk`; OakForest's TintSpruceSet sets the trunk copy without a tint), so near and far models dither into each other over the switch instead of popping. Not re-captured from the near model: the pack's card silhouettes stay. Film (120 fps, `-benchmark -fps=120`, camera flying in at 15 m/s over the Steele Creek spruces, `Saved/Review/Spruce/film` in the main folder): no ghosting or visible dither noise under TSR. Cost (solo, same stand): 0.5 GPU 6.53 / 6.50, 2 GPU 5.52 / 5.47: the dithering costs nothing measurable.
- **Round 3, same night (owner: "The low render pine tree has holes in it, and it's not blurry enough, the tree is too HD. Also ... it look[s] like the tree moves from left to right"):** the gaps are off (`$AirStrength 0`; the opacity sheets are still copied, unchanged, so DarkCones' swap is harmless); the blur is back and stronger (`$FarBlur 6`); `jeeb.Forest.SpruceFar` default `1.05 0.85 0.8` (0.9 read much darker than the sunlit near models from the ground). Position: the far cards' vertices sit within about 25 cm of the mesh origin, the trunk's foot within 5 cm (probed in the editor), so the card stands on the same spot; from four sides of one spruce_full_03 (15966 9406, logged by the new `JEEBFOREST <mesh> one at` line) and in a ground-level walk-in at 120 fps the near and far trees stand in the same place. What can still read as a sideways move: the card shows the pack's picture from the nearest of its captured directions, so an uneven crown can lean the other way for a moment as you circle; the dithered crossfade softens it. Pictures `Saved/Review/Spruce/v3/` (main folder): `ang_sheet.png`, `walk_sheet.png`, `walk_zoom.png`, `film/`.
- Frame cost (shadows copy, editor, `uqueue solo`, 1280x720, GPU ms medians of about 8 s each, sun creeping, two forest views north-east of Oak Hill, each measured twice in turn): the flecks against the old even grain, HIGH 6.75 / 6.77 to 6.87 / 6.89 and 7.91 / 7.81 to 7.96 / 7.92; LOW 5.21 / 5.22 to 5.33 / 5.30 and 5.79 / 6.15 to 5.64 / 6.18: about +0.1 ms at most, at the edge of the noise; game thread unchanged. The leafy shadows they replace were measured earlier at about 0.1-0.3 ms and could not be timed in this build (their code is gone). `jeeb.Forest.SpruceLod` at a spruce stand near Steele Creek (-45351 14443, looking up into the crowns): 0.5 costs 7.70 / 7.74 against 7.02 / 7.03 at 1 and 6.77 at 2 on HIGH, and 6.15 / 6.13 against 5.49 / 5.55 at 1 and 5.24 at 2 on LOW: about +0.7 ms where spruces fill the view (nothing where there are none). The owner chose it by eye; `jeeb.Forest.SpruceLod 1` in OakForest.cpp's default is the way back.
