## Calmer tree colours, dimmer leaves, amber lamps (2026-11-16, townbuild2)

Owner: lamps like harsh LEDs, ground leaves too bright, trees too bright and colourful (Skyrim, Hogwarts as the bar).
- **Lamps:** `LampLook.h/.cpp` (`JeebLamp`): `jeeb.Lamp.Tint` (default `1.0 0.38 0.06`; the lights use a paler 1 0.49 0.13; they were 1,0.62,0.3) and `jeeb.Lamp.Glow` (default 0.9; the TownDecor `_Glow` materials had GlowAmount 5, which clipped the glass picture to white). Every `_Glow` slot of a mesh drawn by Fill.cpp and TownLabTown.cpp gets a shared dynamic material (GlowTint x GlowAmount); the lantern, lamp post and shop lights register through `TrackLight`. The parent materials `/Game/TownDecor/M_TownDecor` and `M_TownDecorTwoSided` got a vector parameter `GlowTint` on the emissive (`Tools/patch_lamp_materials.py`, run by `Tools/PatchLampMaterials.ps1`, editor closed). The forge's shop light stays red-orange; AutumnDecor's pumpkin glow and the castle/tree-fort lights are unchanged.
- **Leaves on the ground:** AutumnDecor.cpp tints M_FlyLeaf/M_LeafLitter from the palette colour x `jeeb.Trees.Saturation` x `jeeb.Leaves.Brightness` (0.65); the same patch script lowers their built-in "never pitch black" emissive from 0.14 to 0.04 (that is why they glowed in shade; they did already receive shadows). `Tools/build_materials.py` still says 0.14: change it there too if the materials are rebuilt.
- **Trees:** `TreeLook.h/.cpp` holds `jeeb.Trees.Saturation` (0.7). Dz defaults (TreeGroves.cpp and OakForest.cpp, now identical) were multiplied by 0.85. OakForest `ColourAt`: green about 40% of the land and of the strays, the rest shared (red a quarter of it). That changes which trees get which colour a little (same on every machine).
- **Spruce:** OakForest.cpp now has TintSpruceSet/ApplySpruce (the same parameters as TreeGroves' TintSpruce) on the forest's and the town's spruces (and the small ones), live from `jeeb.Trees.Spruce`; default `0.8 0.9 0.92`.

- **Not done:** the town trees are no longer choppable; the LOOK tab's spruce colour sliders (`jeeb.Trees.Spruce`) aren't applied to the forest's spruces.

## Dirt roads, the forest on the map, texture caps (2026-11-16)

- **Why the dirt roads didn't show:** M_Dirt's opacity mask (Tools/world_material.py, `build_dirt`) built its edge noise ("rag", "grit") from T_Dirt's red channel. T_Dirt is a dark rust picture stored as sRGB, so a sample averages about 0.07, while the sums assumed noise round 0.5: the grit term came out near -0.56, the track's middle summed to about 0.44 under the 0.5 cut, and nearly the whole ribbon was clipped away (only cobbles, always solid, and a few pale specks showed). The mesh was always fine: `jeeb.Test.PathsRed` (Paths.cpp, not Shipping) paints the ribbons flat red with the engine's plain material and showed them whole, on the ground, along every road. Lesson: noise read from a colour picture, or from any sRGB texture, isn't 0..1 round 0.5; T_Patches is stored sRGB too (a sample is about the painted value squared).
- **The new track:** the outline comes from T_Patches read back to its painted values (square root): `reach` = across the ribbon (0 side, 1 middle) plus a wander of a few metres (0.32) and small bites (0.16); opaque from about 0.45 in, gone by 0.17, broken by fine world-space crumbs (23 and 37 cm) so it frays without a line (Python can't make a DitherTemporalAA node). Colour: the packed earth with its pebbles a little quieter and 15% less saturated (it read rust-red at the low sun), 10% lighter down the worn middle, and toward the edges patches of the meadow ground's own colour (T_GrassGround x GroundGrass, as M_Ground) so the edge melts into the grass. The cobble part (vertex red 1) is unchanged; Oak Hill's real-site streets have no ribbons anyway. `JEEB_WORLD_ONLY=dirt` makes world_material.py rebuild M_Dirt alone from the textures already in /Game (M_Ground and M_Stone untouched); run it with `UnrealEditor-Cmd ... -run=pythonscript -script=<Tools/world_material.py>` (the editor closed). Log `JEEBPATHS material ..., bounds ...`.
- **The forest on the Tab map** (KnightHUDMap.cpp, `GatherOakForest`): read from AOakForest's own drawn instanced sets on this machine (fixed seeds, so the same everywhere): cork oaks and aspens take a darker shade of their leaf material's live `Fall Color Tint` (the `jeeb.Trees.Dz*` colour), full and half-grown spruces the map's pine green; seedlings, bushes, logs, stumps, rocks, the hidden shadow twins and the collision sets are skipped. The world picture gets a soft wash (alpha 0.6, crown radius from the mesh bounds), then the Kind 2 roads stamped again over it; the town close-up each crown (alpha 0.85, log `JEEBMAP forest round Oak Hill: N trees`). Zoomed in, the roads are drawn as lines as before. Green trees read faintly on the green land.
- **Texture caps** (Config/DefaultDeviceProfiles.ini, `[Windows DeviceProfile]`, the engine's full GlobalDefaults list after `!TextureLODGroups=ClearArray`): `TEXTUREGROUP_World` and `WorldNormalMap` MaxLODSize 4096 (was 16384); the editor follows through WindowsEditor. Survey (`Tools/texture_survey.py`, writes Saved/TextureSurvey.txt): every cooked pack texture is in World or WorldNormalMap (DZ 66 over 2048, the largest 8192x4096; Spruce Forest 38, normals to 8192; the grass packs a few 8192s); ours top out at 4096 (24 TownLab building textures, 3 of the church), the knight and suit are Character at 2048. Estimated texture data before pak compression: the packs' oversized textures 1977 MB -> 1378 MB at 4096 (about 600 MB less, perhaps 350-450 MB off the zip); 2048 would be 345 MB (about 1.6 GB less) but also halves our 27 building textures. Seen in a test run (`ListTextures`): the streamed DZ 8192x4096 leaves now load at 4096x2048. Not checked: the non-streamed 8K grass and flower pictures (springGrass_Albedo_8k_I, Combined_Flowers_A) still showed 8192 in the editor build; the cook should drop their top mip by the same group rule, check in the next package.
- **To reach 2048** (the owner's target): move our own textures over 2048 (TownLab/Buildings `T_TL_*_Base/_Normal`, Buildings `T_OakHillChurch_300k_*`) to `TEXTUREGROUP_Project01` (colour) and `Project02` (normal), give those two groups MaxLODSize 4096 in the same profile, and make the importers set them (a rebuild resets the group); then set World and WorldNormalMap to 2048. Do it when no agent owns those folders.

- **Texture cap now 2048 (shadow agent, 2026-11-16):** the 27 own textures over 2048 (24 in TownLab/Buildings, T_OakHillChurch_300k_Base/Glow/Normal) are in TEXTUREGROUP_Project01 (colour) and Project02 (normal), uncapped (16384) in DefaultDeviceProfiles.ini; World and WorldNormalMap are capped at 2048, so only the Fab packs (DZ, Spruce, grass) shrink. Tools/townlab_import.py and Tools/building_import.py set the groups on every import (always, because the editor's own 2048 cap hides the real size). Re-survey (Tools/texture_survey.py, run in the editor, which follows the cap): no non-pack texture is left over 2048 in World groups; the Project groups show 12+12 TownLab and 3 church at 4096. Saving against the 4096 cap: about 1.0 GB of cooked texture data before pak compression (1378 -> 345 MB for the packs' oversized textures), perhaps 0.6-0.9 GB off the zip. Not packaged yet. The Project groups have no editor display names (UE has no setting for it).

## New shields and the forest ruins (2026-11-16)

Owner: "Here's one world item and three shields I think look cool ... These three shields can replace the ones we already have ... From left to right it's the Kite shield, the tower shield, and the round shield", then for the ruin: "Scatter in the forest but sparingly. Maybe only like 10 of them so far." All four are Meshy community models (CC0, 0 credits); sources in `Art/Props/Sources.md`, `Art/WorldProps/Sources.md` and `Docs/MeshyCommunityLibrary.md`.

- **Shield models:** `/Game/Props/SM_ShieldKite` (style 0), `SM_ShieldRound` (1), `SM_ShieldTower` (2), 6k triangles each (from 445k, 69k, 85k), baked and repainted by `prop_clean.py` (`SHIELD` settings and its `paint` classes: rivets, steel and charcoal face on the kite; red ring, iron and rich brown wood on the round; on the tower, since 2026-11-17, its Meshy retexture split into rich brown planks and steel). Built with `Tools\BuildProps.ps1 ShieldKite ShieldRound ShieldTower` (the originals in `Art/Props/Originals/` are git-ignored; keep them on this PC).
- **Kite repainted from its shape (2026-11-21, owner: "The paint on this shield looks horrible. It looks all smudged and the line where gold stops and black starts isn't defined. Please repaint."):** the kite no longer uses the paint classes and zones below. `prop_clean.py`'s new `repaint` option (`Tools/prop_repaint.py`) measures the full 445k-triangle model from the front and the back (a height picture each, 2048 rows, about 0.3 mm a pixel), finds the face by fitting its dome and flooding out from the ring between rim and cross until a wall stops it (`wall` 1.0 cm/cm), and takes the raised parts as the rim (joined to the outline) and the cross (the one piece in the middle, out to the foot of its low slope). New pictures are drawn from those shapes: one clean charcoal face (hue 220, a little lighter in the middle), rim, rivets and cross in plain grey steel that takes the tier, a 1.1 mm dark line where steel meets the face, the cross's border bright with a light bevel shaded from the height itself, its sunk panel a darker shade of the tier and the scrollwork in it lighter. They are laid on the full model straight through it (front picture on the front, back picture on the back) and baked like the old ones, 16 rays a pixel, so paint and relief line up. Base and mask are 2048 now (were 1024), the normal map 1024 and baked from the shape alone (the library normal picture was all scratches and pitting), with the face's dents and cracks smoothed out of it (`calm` 0.85). The mask is drawn, not sorted from colour, so the back has no specks: only its rim and rivets take the tier. Every number is in `prop_repaint.design` (`look=` in the PROPS line overrides any). Still one mesh (6000 triangles), one material. Rebuild: `Tools\BuildProps.ps1 ShieldKite` (needs `Art/Props/Originals/ShieldKite_Original.glb`; on 2026-11-21 it was only in `../JeebsAgents/look/Art/Props/Originals`, not in main: copy it back). A round or tower shield could use the same option if the owner wants them cleaner.
- **How the parts are told apart (review round 2):** the paint classes run on each model's own picture before the bake (`src_paint`), and the tier mask is baked like the colour. Colour alone failed: the tower's grey planks and worn bronze, and the kite's worn chips and its steel, are the same colours, and at Silver they came out as chalky blotches. So the tower's frame and boss go by shape (`relief`: raised more than about 1 cm above the planks round it) and the kite's metal by where it is on the front (`zones`: the rim, the cross, the rivets; its face keeps no metal). The tower's outer frame came out mostly brown (it is barely raised), so on the tower the tier shows on the boss, its ring, the X bands and the top.
- **Tier colour by pixel:** M_Prop has a new `MaskTex` (default black): the tier tint goes where `max(vertex colour R, MaskTex R)`. The shields' `T_<Name>_Mask` marks the kite's rim, rivets and cross, the round shield's rim, studs and boss, and the tower's star boss, its ring, the X bands and the top. Other props are unchanged (their mask is black).
- **In the game:** the procedural shield parts still exist (the server's guard, arrows sticking in `GetShieldParts`), hidden on clients; the model is a child of each style's Plate part (`JeebProps::AddShieldModel`, placed by `JeebProps::ShieldPlace`), tinted by the tier in `ApplyArmorSlot` (`FArmorPart::Model`). So holding, blocking, the back (`PlaceShield`) and arrow sticking work as before. The pen knight (`AFoeKnight`) carries the kite model. The gear wall and dropped loot use `JeebProps::MeshForItem` / `MakeItem`; the HUD icons (`KnightHUDIcons.cpp`) were redrawn to match (kite with a cross, round with planks, red ring and boss, tower with a frame and star).
- **Ruin model:** `/Game/WorldProps/SM_Ruin` (40k triangles from 820k; LODs 12k and 2.4k; Nanite off; 481 x 730 x 500 cm, origin at the bottom centre) with 59 convex hulls from `worldprop_clean.py` (column hulls on a 70 cm grid), `MI_Ruin` on the shared `/Game/TownDecor/M_TownDecor` (flagged for instanced meshes). The dirt pile was cut by texture colour; the dark roof-like eave round the tower's upper wall is rebuilt as a solid stone capping course (`course`, 2026-11-17, below); the stone is repainted pale, weathered grey to sit with Oak Hill. Built with `Tools\BuildWorldProps.ps1 Ruin` (editor closed). The window arch is too small to walk through; the hulls fill it.
- **Placing them (`AForestRuins`, ForestRuins.cpp/.h, spawned by `AKnightGameState` right after `AOakForest`):** 10 ruins from seed 47471 within `JeebOakForest::Reach` of the plaza: 25 m + the ruin's reach outside the town box, clear of the original square and the TownLab site, 8 m + its reach from water, river banks and roads (`PathClearance`), at most 110 cm of ground rise under the footprint (`DrawnHeight`, sunk 15 cm), 60 m apart, never on any of the forest's solids (trunks, logs, stumps, rocks; every machine has them). One instanced set with its own collision on every machine (hidden on a dedicated server); clients also remove the forest's bushes and plants under each ruin, and shrink any seedling there (a tree set's shadow twin keeps its own list, so its instances are never removed). Log `JEEBRUINS` (each spot and `hash`). First run (2026-11-16): 38 spots tried, 5 plants cleared, 18 ms, hash 2d04eab8, ruins 241 to 460 m from the plaza.
- **FillKinds.csv:** a `Ruin` row (landmark, mesh collision) so `-FillGallery` shows it; `town_decor_copy.py` now keeps rows whose mesh is outside `/Game/TownDecor` when it rewrites the file.
- **Review (two rounds, 2026-11-16):** the package review passed. The visual review passed the held and raised poses, the round shield and the kite at every tier. Two things still failed after round 2 and are left for the owner to judge, with the approach to change rather than more tuning:
  - The tower at Silver still showed chalky white wear streaks (the model's own picture is full of pale wear). **Fixed 2026-11-17:** a Meshy retexture (10 credits, `enable_original_uv`, so the same mesh and UVs; prompt and task in `Art/Props/Sources.md`) gave clean painted planks and clean dark steel (`Art/Props/Originals/ShieldTower_Retex.glb`, git-ignored, keep it). `prop_clean.py` now splits it by colour alone (saturated planks to rich brown, the grey steel keeps its own paint), so all its metal takes the tier: the frame, X bands, star boss, edging and the knots on the sides. The visual review passed it at Tin, Silver, Gold and Iron (no streaks; Tin and Iron are close, both dark). `-ShotEyesSet=<eye>:wear=<tier>` now re-tiers a worn shield too, so one run shows every tier.
  - The ruin kept a small dark roof slab and thin curved sheets near the top of one wall. **2026-11-17:** most of it was not geometry but flipped faces: `worldprop_clean.py` ran Blender's recalculate-normals on the open scanned shell, which turned whole patches inward, so the game culled them and a solid column read as a paper-thin panel with hanging sheets. The ruin now keeps the model's own winding (`recalc=False`) and is solid from every side. The real ledge round the curved wall's top (a sheet 30-50 cm wide at 3.1-3.5 m) can't simply be cut: it is that wall's top, and cutting it left a see-through band under the upper courses. It is now pulled back onto the wall line (`tuck`), but the visual review (round 1) still fails a ragged thin edge, a dark gap and a green line under the top courses in close-ups and from below (look_ruin4_6, 7, 8, 11 in the look copy). **Fixed later on 2026-11-17 (owner: "Fix the ruin wall top"):** the "ledge" was really a dark, roof-like eave, a zero-thickness skirt from the lower wall's face (about 3.1 m) sloping up to the upper courses, which stand 40 cm further in (about 3.55 m). `worldprop_clean.py`'s `make_course` deletes every face from 3.02 to 3.60 m in the wedge of the tower it ran round, from the model and from the bake source. It stands the wall's own cross-section at 3.02 m up as one closed block, 4 cm proud of the outer face, with grooves about every 70 cm as joints. The block is voxel-remeshed (2.5 cm), smoothed across the ground and joined, overlapping the cut edges above, below and at the ends. Its faces go into the bake source textured with the row of stones 62 cm below it, so it bakes as two rows of squared blocks under a flat 40 cm stone ledge. `tuck`, `thin`, `cut_final` and the cut of the darkest faces are gone: with the eave rebuilt they only punched see-through pinholes into stone joints and the doorway. The game frame is now applied before the reduce. Still 40k triangles, LODs 12k and 2.4k, 59 hulls, 479 x 730 x 500 cm, JEEBRUINS hash unchanged (05f9b10d). A voxel remesh of the whole model was tried first: the cuts' open edges make the remesher's inside test leak, and most of the ruin vanished. The visual review passed the band in round 2: the round-1 FAILs on the tower's crumpled top stones, the left arm's thin slab courses and the inner wall's dark smudges were checked against before/after renders and are the original scan's look, unchanged. Left as they are: the original scan's few tiny see-through specks between the tower's top stones.
  - The tip of the kite and tower touches the saddle when it's on his back while riding. The old shields hung in the same place.
- **Shields on the forearm (2026-11-17, owner: "make sure the shields are up against the knight's arm and not floating slightly in front of it"):** the three models had been placed where the old code-built shields' back plane was (`JeebProps::ShieldPlace`), but each model's back sits at its own depth (the kite and round dish away from the arm, the tower's handle stands off its back). Measured on the real meshes (the suit's arm piece skinned as posed against each model's back as a 1 cm height field; `-ShotShieldGap`, log `JEEBSHIELDGAP`, every frame of the swing up and down `JEEBSHIELDSWING`): raised on the forearm the kite and round stood 2.7 cm off the plate and the tower's handle sat 2 cm inside the arm; held up to guard the kite stood 6.2 cm off, the round 3.0. Fix: `FKnightAnimator::ShieldWorld(BlockAlpha, Style)` moves each style along its own face axis (`jeeb.Knight.ShieldSnug`, 9 numbers: kite, round, tower, each hanging_strapped_guarding; default 2.4_2.4_5.9_2.35_2.35_2.9_-2.3_-2.3_2.05), so every one now leaves about 0.3 cm. During the swing up to guard and back the moving forearm crosses the blend's path, so the snug lets go there (`jeeb.Knight.ShieldSnugSwing` 1) and the round and tower stand 1.5 / 3 cm further off half way (`jeeb.Knight.ShieldSwingOut` 0_1.5_3): least gap 0.1 cm over the swing, walking and standing. `PlaceGear` places each style's parts with its own transform; the pen knight is the kite (default). Armour styles are numbers only (one arm model), so one set of numbers fits every armour; with the arm piece off the mail is thinner, so the shield stands a little off, never in. The hanging pose (forearm down) was not seen in any sword clip, so it takes the raised numbers unmeasured. The visual review passed all three, idle, guard and walk; it noted the kite and round lie about 25-30 degrees off the forearm's line seen from above, touching near the elbow with a thin wedge of floor toward the wrist: if the owner still sees that as floating, the fix is a turn of the shield (`ShieldAtGuard`'s Face), not more snug. Test switches: `-ShotEyesSet=<eye>:shield=<style>` and `jeeb.Test.Block 1/0`.

## The 2026-11-18 daytime batch (built by agents while the owner was at work, on free rein; owner to judge everything live)

- **Quests** (QuestComponent/QuestData/QuestPeople/KnightHUDQuests): data in `Content/Data/Quests.csv` (quest rows), `QuestsSteps.csv` (talk/bring/kill/visit steps, Mark = map target), `QuestsPeople.csv` (givers: spot in metres from the plaza, look, greetings); parse errors go to the log as "Jeebsland quests:". `UQuestComponent` on the knight: replicated owner-only `TArray<FQuestProgress>{Key, Step, Count, bDone}`; the client asks through `ServerQuest(Accept|Talk, key)`; the server checks distance (6.5 m to its own copy of the person), needs, items (removed on hand-in), room for the reward. Kills via `UQuestComponent::OnFoeBeaten` (FoeKnight yield; kill steps accept a foe Key or Group, `JeebFoes::IsFoeTarget`), visits checked every 0.5 s on the server. Saved in `UJeebSave` QuestKeys/QuestSteps/QuestCounts/QuestDone (old saves load with none; unknown keys dropped). Givers are local `ANpc` (`ENpcRole::QuestGiver`) spawned by `AQuestPeople` after `AWorldFill` on every machine. Escape screen tab 6 = QUESTS (shown second); J toggles it. Eight quests today (six starters, two bandit quests). The roadmap said the owner wanted to plan the first quests together: these are a first draft in data, to rewrite freely. Test: `-JeebQuestTest` (QuestTest.cpp, non-Shipping).
- **Bandits** (FoeData/FoeBandit/FoeCamps): `AFoeKnight` is general now: `SetupFoe` gives him a `Foes.csv` row and `RepKind` replicates once (255 = the pen knight, unchanged). The bandit brain (home, fight, return) walks on `DrawnHeight`, steers round obstacles with a capsule sweep, refuses steps into a town (`JeebFoes::InTown`: Oak Hill, the original square, every `Towns()` circle) or water over 50 cm. `AFoeCamps` places four camps the same way on every machine (moved to clear, dry, level ground 60 m from towns, 20 m from roads, 25 m from the river; hash in the log), spawns foes on the server, sleeps them beyond 90/105 m from every player, not relevant past 150 m. Cutpurse (tin, 45 hp), Bruiser (iron, round shield, 90 hp), Red Hal (gold, kite shield, 150 hp; there is no bronze tier). No archer: the bow code is tied to the player's knight. `jeeb.Foes.Bandits 0` leaves them out. Test: `-JeebFoeTest`.
- **Emotes** (JeebEmotes.h, KnightCharacterEmote/KnightAnimatorEmote/KnightHUDEmotes): 8 Mixamo clips on Y Bot, retargeted with knight_clip_retarget.py and imported as A_Emote* (Waving, Quick Formal Bow, Cheering, Clapping, Silly Dancing, Sitting Idle, Pointing Forward, Salute). Ids go over the network: add new emotes at the end, never reorder. Timing in the Emote_* rows of KnightMoves.csv. Animator keys are 100+id; standing emotes level the lower foot; sit uses floor sole 6. The server validates in `ServerEmote`; `RepEmote` is uint16 (id low byte, counter high byte) so late joiners see a dance in progress. Every emote except bow stows the gear. B opens the wheel. Credit "Animations: Mixamo".
- **Steele Creek** (`ASteeleCreekTown`, plan in SteeleCreek.h): the second real town, southwest forest at -95200,-2900; TownLab models per plot, streets as PathDefs Kind 2, pads in `JeebSteele::Apply` (HeightAt), a pack-tree ring (seed 51505; these trees can't be chopped), 12 quarry rocks (iron, silver, gold, emerald, diamond), 10 NPCs (troll smith, wizard banker, knight trader; `ANpc::SetFolk`), Meshy props in `/Game/SteeleCreek` (anvil, mine cart, ingot rack; 57 credits), fill rows Site "Steele" in FillOakHill.csv. Not replicated; the JEEBSTEELE hash compares machines. Frame 9.4 ms vs Oak Hill 8.7 (editor), GPU equal. Known: one house pad 28 cm off, the ore stall is quite orange, lore reuses the Blackrock speakers.
- **The stone bridge** (`ABridge`, Bridge.cpp, BridgeGround.h): a Meshy model (ids in `Art/Bridge/Sources.md`, 48 credits) at X -3550, Y 12110 on Oak Hill's west road; `Tools/BuildBridge.ps1`. Collision is hand-made: one procedural solid for deck and arches (profile tabled in Bridge.cpp; re-measure with `bridge_profile.py` if the model changes) plus 18 parapet boxes 80 cm over the deck (mantle works). `JeebBridge::ApplyGround` (last in `HeightAt`) raises embankments and pier footings; roads Kind 3 (Blackrock's east gate over the ridge) and Kind 4 (the span, no ribbon) are last in `PathDefs`. A server builds collision only. Changing BridgeGround.h or Terrain.h rebuilds nearly everything.
- **Oak Hill's war buildings** (`/Game/TownLab/War`, `Tools/BuildWarBuildings.ps1`, TownLabSwaps.h): one fort model used twice (plots 30, 31), the watchtower (32), the castle ruins (27, levelled). Unlevelled plots sit at Lo + 25% of the ground range under their walls (`JEEBOAK WAR` log line). The ruins skip townlab_bake (`war_ruins_lowmodel.py`). The tower and south fort are a few metres off the plan's spots; house 10 moved 0.9 m. Under 0.1 ms. 117 credits.
- **NPC bodies** (NpcBody.h/.cpp, `/Game/People`, `Tools/ImportPeople.ps1`): six Meshy characters (Townsman, Townswoman, Elder, Smith, Banker, Guard), each with its own skeleton and Idle/Walk/Talk clips from Meshy's rig and animation API (303 credits). `PeopleWho.csv` maps a name, quest key, folk or role to a model and tint (`shapes` keeps the old box body; trolls use it); `People.csv` holds walk speed, sink, garment brightness. `M_Person` dyes only the masked garment. `UNpcAnimInstance` is a code-driven three-clip blend with a head turn. On-screen-only ticking, URO, shadows within 30 m, draw distance 90 m, 4 LODs: at or below the old shapes' cost. `jeeb.Npc.Models` (default 1; 0 = old shapes, live). A dedicated server always builds the shapes. Meshy's default Idle turns the body: use Idle 3 (243) for men, Idle 4 (244) for women.
- **Fast travel** (FastTravel.h/.cpp, KnightCharacterTravel.cpp, KnightHUDTravel.cpp): the table is `JeebTravel::Towns()` (future towns listed with `bOpen=false`); `UTravelComponent` on the knight, `ServerTravel`; visited towns in the account's `.towns` file plus the character's `TownsVisited`. `[Travel]` in Tuning.ini: channel 3 s, combat 10 s, cooldown 30 s. `JeebTravel::PvpHook` returns false until PvP areas exist.
- **Staff and wand models** (`/Game/Props` SM_Staff, SM_Wand; `Tools/prop_upright.py`, `prop_clean.py` `girth` option; about 102 credits): the code-built bodies in `WeaponDesign::Build` are gone; icons are still code-drawn; the orbs no longer glow (M_Prop has no emission). Not checked: stowed on the back, walk and run, tiers other than tin and gold.
- **Lazy forest nodes and Oak Hill spawning**: `AOakForest::UpdateNodes` spawns an `AOakTreeNode` only within `jeeb.Forest.NodeReach` (35 m) of a player; the server removes only whole, idle nodes; `FelledSet` and `Fellable` hold the felled look without a node; `AKnightGameState::OnRep_Nodes` calls `SyncNodeCharges` so late joiners see felled trees. About 120 ms of forest build saved and 5,610 actors fewer. `AKnightGameMode::OakWakeSpot` (22 spots on the plaza arc, checked for other knights and solids) is used for new characters and `Die()`; new players and deaths already went to Oak Hill before this, the old "still Blackrock" note was stale.
- **Meshy spend today:** about 627 credits (staff and wand 102, Steele props 57, bridge 48, war buildings 117, people 303); balance about 990.
- **Stray folders** two agents made outside the project by a mistyped Blender path and could not remove: `C:\Saved\BridgeWork` (6 small pictures) and an empty `C:\tiles`. The owner can delete them.

### After the reviews (2026-11-18 afternoon): what the fix round changed

All five reviewers ran once on the combined build (package PASS, performance PASS, online FAIL with one blocker, visual FAIL, motion FAIL). The fails were fixed and merged; the fixes were not re-reviewed.
- **Online blocker, fixed:** clients crashed about 2 s after joining a world because NpcBody.cpp cached raw mesh and clip pointers in a static map and the collection between the menu world and the game world freed them. They are rooted now (`AddToRoot`). Confirmed afterwards with a server and two clients (4 joins each). Today's other caches were audited and are weak-held or UPROPERTY.
- **Online, small fixes:** a lazily made tree node beside a felled tree starts felled on a player's machine (`AOakForest::SpawnNode`); any blow or a fast travel ends an emote (`EmoteCancelled`, `TravelPlaced`); the fallen can't accept or hand in quests (`DoQuest`). Left: `RewardFits` checks reward items one by one (no quest gives several kinds yet). Unexplained: `-BridgeWalkNet` walking east from a standstill on the deck logged 3 corrections (the west leg none).
- **Casts:** the wand and staff use Mixamo clips (KnightMoves.csv Cast_Wand, Cast_Staff; KnightAnimatorCast.cpp). Legs stay his own, projectile timing unchanged (release at 0.3 = HitFrac). Tune with `jeeb.Knight.CastTiltStaff` / `CastTiltWand` / `CastAim`. A third clip (CastUp) is imported and unused.
- **Staff:** carried upright at his side (`StaffCarry`; `jeeb.Knight.StaffHand`, `StaffLean`; for about 0.2 s after equipping it shows the old flat hold); stowed, it is reversed and centred diagonally on his back (KnightCharacterBody.cpp `RebuildHeld`). Walk and run with the staff were not filmed.
- **Emotes:** bow stows the gear too. Gear stays stowed 0.45 s after an emote ends (0.7 s after sit, 0.3 s if moving) unless he attacks, blocks and so on (`EmoteStowLeft`). The salute's hand reaches the visor by IK (`jeeb.Knight.SaluteLift`, `SaluteAt`).
- **NPCs** turn on the spot at patrol corners (walk only within 30 degrees); head-look clamps at 55 degrees and fades out by 125. Test switches: `-PeopleLineup` with `-PeopleAtX` / `-PeopleAtY` / `-PeopleWalk` / `-PeopleWalkLen` / `-PeopleWalkNear`; `-PeopleLog`.
- **Bandits** turn before stepping on a stroll (40 degrees) and hold a sidestep 0.6 s. Test log: `-LogStroll` (JEEBSTROLL); start near a camp (`-LabAt=2800_47800`), because sleeping camps don't tick. Performance: awake foes cost 0.25 to 0.5 ms within about 90 m of a camp (full knight bodies; if it matters, give them the NPC models' LODs, 30 m shadows and URO).
- **Oak Hill forts** are 1.4x (OakHill.h row `OakHillFort`). The fort model's gate is a closed plank door, so nobody can walk inside at any scale: opening it is a model change, the owner's call. `-FortGateTest` / `-FortGateWalk` (non-Shipping) sweep a capsule through each wall and walk a client in (`JEEBFORTGATE`).
- **Steele Creek** has a real forest (round 3, replacing the 38 town trees and the 250 m ring): `JeebSteeleWoods::Plan()` in SteeleCreekTown.cpp plans about 1,000 trees on the forest's 6.5 m lattice (seed 51523; belt from 4 m off the walls thinning over 14-85 m, 0.30 share in the town's gaps, 200 m reach; clear of streets, yard, doors, quarry, walks, props, fill) and `FOakForestBuild::Steele()` in OakForest.cpp draws them as forest trees (colours, shadow twins and fade, grass thinning, solid trunks, `bNoChop`); `jeeb.Steele.Density` scales it (read at build; `jeeb.Steele.TreeShadows` is gone, use `jeeb.Forest.Shadows`);  (lobed belt edge, noise glades; keeps off water and steep ground; the hash is in JEEBFOREST and JEEBSTEELE. The stall uses `/Game/SteeleCreek/MI_SteeleStall` (`M_SteeleToned`), the mine carts `M_SteeleTonedProp`.
- **People:** `M_Person` has a skin dye driven by a SkinMask texture (`T_Smith_Skin`, `Tools/people_skin_mask.py`); PeopleWho.csv has Scale and Skin columns; trolls are the Smith body at 1.18x with green-grey skin. Pitfall: a grayscale texture parameter must not default to `/Engine/EngineResources/Black` (a colour texture): M_Person failed to compile and everyone rendered grey. Every villager wears their own garment colour.
- **Look assets:** `Tools/FixLookAssets.ps1` (`fixlook_assets.py`, editor closed) rebuilds the bridge tint (1.07, 1.02, 0.92), `M_PropGlow` (staff and wand glow; M_Prop untouched), `M_GlowOrb` (the bolt; Strength 4.0 and white-heart 0.3 are the knobs if it reads too white in daylight), the M_Person skin dye and the Steele toned materials. Rerun it after ImportPeople, prop or bridge re-imports. It crashes in Python teardown after printing "FIXLOOK done": harmless.
- **HUD:** tags and quest marks sit at the head top (`ANpc::HeadTopZ`, reads body scale); the E prompt sits beside the name; marks fade under screen titles (NoteTitle/UnderTitle); the emote wheel is 20 px bigger; the minimap has no town label.

## Townsfolk lives, counter keepers and the shop camera (2026-11-18/19, agent `lantern`)

The owner asked for four things. NPCs' feet were under the path. Villagers should walk round as if they owned a house, not stand in the square. Every shop and stall should be manned. Buying should zoom in, as in Hogwarts or Arc Raiders.
- **Feet:** people stand on `JeebTerrain::DrawnHeight`, the ground as drawn: Oak Hill's 1 m mesh, the square's 2 m mesh, else the world's 8 m mesh. They used to stand on `HeightAt`, which lies under the 8 m mesh in Steele Creek. Near a player they also take a short WorldStatic trace, accepted 0.5 m below to 0.7 m above (steps, bridges), and ease to it. No `WalkSink` or NpcBody change was needed.
- **Rounds (`NpcLife.h/.cpp`, `ATownLife`, spawned by KnightGameState after AQuestPeople):**
  - Each person's day is a fixed timeline of stand, turn and walk steps that repeats. Every machine builds it from `Content/Data/Townsfolk.csv` and the town data. Where they are comes from the server's clock (`GetServerWorldTimeSeconds`), so a dedicated server and its clients agree with nothing replicated. `JEEBLIFE built ... hash` must match on every machine.
  - Routes run on a 90 cm grid: buildings and water are blocked, a capsule test catches props, and streets are cheaper. The path is then pulled tight.
  - Each round: the doorstep, a potter round the yard, two errands (a neighbour named in the CSV meets them at the first one and they chat, taking turns), linger, home.
  - At night (day-loop phase 0.31 to 0.69) a round that starts then is spent on the doorstep. There is no lantern spot yet.
  - Quest givers wander 2-3 m round their post and sometimes meet the nearest giver for a chat. The server's quest reach adds their roam (`QuestComponent::NearPerson`).
  - The old patrol beats (gate knights, Blackrock villagers, Steele's people) became clock-driven beats too.
  - Each machine may run a person a little behind the clock while you talk to them or stand in their way. They catch up during their next stand.
- **Keepers:** Townsfolk.csv `Keeper` rows put a person at each counter, facing the customers' side:
  - Oak Hill: store, market stall, potion shop, potion stall, bank, bank stall, smithy.
  - Steele Creek: smithy, bank, store, and a new ore-stall troll, Grenna Oreheart.
  - Blackrock keepers are the existing ones.
  - Keepers step a little along the counter now and then. Oak Hill counters open with the nearest keeper of the matching role (`KnightCharacterInteract.cpp`).
- **NPC capsules** no longer block players (WorldDynamic, ignores Pawn), so no player's movement depends on an NPC's position.
- **Built half a second in:** `ATownLife` builds every round 0.5 s after it is spawned. Chaos only answers traces and overlaps for solids made the same frame a frame or more later. Built at once, the routes ignored every prop and the counter scan saw nothing. Rounds start from where each person was placed (`ANpc::PlacedAt/PlacedYaw`, set in Setup), never where they stand by then, since a client's people may already have turned to the knight.
- **Counters:** a stall keeper's spot comes from vertical traces walking in from the front: past the counter's top, the first clear spot (market stall 2.45 m in, Steele's ore stall the same). The bank stall (one deep solid box) and the potion stall (shelves right behind its low table) have no clear spot, so they use 70 cm in. Log `JEEBLIFE counter`.
- **Oak Hill places by plot Id** (merged with the 2026-11-18 layout): homes are house plot ids (`House_SE1`...), keepers' places are ids (`MarketStall`, `PotionStall`, `BankStall`, `Smithy`...), resolved with `JeebOakHill::PlotIndexById`; spots come from `JeebOakHill::DoorAt`. A keeper whose spot is within 1.5 m of someone already placed (Pell at the smithy counter) steps 1.6 m along the front, away from them (log `JEEBLIFE keeper ... clear of`). Trolls take the Troll model through the `troll` row of PeopleWho.csv (Townsfolk.csv Folk `troll`). At build, every walk is checked every 50 cm against the building walls: `JEEBLIFE walks inside buildings: N` (0 expected).
- **Cost:** far NPCs tick at 1 s (client) or 2 s (server), or 0.25 s on a server with a player within 60 m. Traces run only near a player. Building every round takes about 0.5 s at world build (about 20,000 walk tests).
- **Shop camera (`KnightShopCam.cpp`, from `CalcCamera`):** while a shop, bank or smithy is open with its keeper, the local view eases (0.5 s) to a close view of the keeper, off-centre, with your own knight hidden through `PlayerController::HiddenActors`. Looks only; trades are unchanged. Live `jeeb.ShopCam.*`.
- Test switches (not in Shipping): `-LifeLog`, `-LifeShots=Name@feet|@front+...`, `-ShopCamTest=Name+...`; add `-JeebOffline` to go straight into the world.

### 2026-11-18 night: movement-start corrections and the bandits' crab-walk (agent `night`; built and tested in phase 2)

- **Corrections when a client starts running (cause found by reading the code):** the knight's Tick set `MaxAcceleration` (WalkAccel 30000 below walking speed, SprintAccel 2048 above) and `MaxWalkSpeed` (strafe mode's 90% sideways / 75% back, read from the *last* move's push) once per frame. The server runs several of a client's moves between two of its frames, so the second move of a start-up still had WalkAccel on the server while the client had switched to SprintAccel: the engine's analog share (push / MaxAcceleration) came out 0.07, the server braked him and corrected the client. Not caused by the day's batch (none of it touched these lines) nor by the test scripts (`-BridgeWalkNet` pushes through the client's moves like a player). Now `UKnightMovement` works both out inside each move: `GetMaxAcceleration` from the move's starting velocity (frozen through the move, `ControlledCharacterMove` / `MoveAutonomous`), `GetMaxSpeed` from the move's own push, facing and Shift (`FLAG_Custom_1`, already sent for the fast swim), with the sprint's fade-in and the after-landing share advanced per move (`MoveStrafeSprint`, `MoveAirMul`, carried in corrections and put back on merged moves). The knight's Tick only publishes the slow parts (`UKnightMovement::FSpeedRule`: gear, potions, stances). Still per frame, outside moves: strafe mode's turn to the camera (`SetActorRotation`), the air speed clamp, the slide's glide, and crouch / guard / strafe mode (ServerState RPC).
- **Bandits face the way they walk round things:** each step `BanditMove` takes is noted (`NoteStep`): `WalkYaw` eases after the steps, and for 0.6 s after a step off the straight line (while still stepping) the fight beyond striking range (`FaceFoeWithin`, 2.8 m), the walk home and the stroll turn toward `WalkYaw` (360 deg/s; the stroll keeps its 180 and its turn-on-the-spot) instead of the foe or the goal. No new traces. `jeeb.Foes.FaceWalk 0` (server) brings back the old facing to compare; `-LogDetour` logs JEEBDETOUR twice a second while detouring (worst facing-vs-step angle and how many steps were more than 45 degrees off).
- **The mantle goes over, not through (owner: "stepping up slightly and moving through the object"):** the vault's capsule path (`PhysScripted`, MantleOver) now rises first to 12 cm over the top while still short of the edge (to 38% of the move), crosses at that height with a small arc to 28 cm past the far edge (to 62%), then drops to the landing; a climb onto rises to the top before moving on. The far edge comes from `PlanMantle` (`ActFar`, sent in corrections with `ActEdge`). In the pose (KnightAnimatorClimb.cpp) the clip's hips never sit lower over the capsule's feet than standing during a mantle, and `MantleFeetClear` lifts the whole body if any boot that is past the near edge and less than 30 cm under the top would be inside the top (eased, so nothing pops; `JEEBMANTLEFEET` in `-ShotLog` runs). MoveLab, `-MantleAt` logs: boots cross 11-27 cm over the 0.75 m post and the 1 m and 1.35 m fences with no lift needed; small lifts (3-31 cm, eased) only as the trailing boot leaves the far side; the climb onto the 1.2 m block lifts 1-9 cm. MoveLab has a new 75 cm stone post at (1000, 1380) for the low vault.
- **The stone bridge's four bollards** (Bridge.cpp) were never solid: he could walk and mantle through them. Each now has a hidden capsule (BlockAll, tagged JeebNoMantle; measured from Bridge.fbx in Blender: about 1.7 m over the deck, so not something to mantle).
- **The troll's own model (2026-11-18, phase 1 of the "troll model" fix; not yet imported):** `Art/People/Troll` holds `Troll_rigged.glb` (Meshy, 20.8k triangles, 2.2 m tall, 24-bone rig, 2048 textures), animation-only `Troll_idle/walk/talk.glb` (Meshy actions Idle_3 243, Slow_Orc_Walk 119, Talk_with_Hands_Open 313), the concept picture and thumbnail. Made with a 4-credit picture (nano-banana-2-1, A-pose), image-to-3d meshy-6 standard (30; it ignored target_polycount and gave 574k triangles, over the 380k rigging limit), remesh to 20k (5), rigging (5), three animations (9): 53 credits in all. Tasks: picture 01a1f177-9ccc-70dd-a3f4-41b16289610c, model 01a1f178-1bdc-76ca-982d-295a852b6c64, remesh 01a1f17b-38dd-700d-a107-92213885187e, rig 01a1f17c-6e12-7555-8521-fdc4810f38f1. `ImportPeople.ps1` has the Troll row; `people_export.py` gives the Troll an empty dye mask (no garment dye). `PeopleWho.csv`: `troll,Troll,,1,` (no tint, scale 1, no skin dye). `People.csv` Troll row WalkSpeed 60, WalkSink 3, GarmentLum 0.1 are now real (walk planted-toe speed 41.6 cm/s: slow, so NPC walkers play it at up to 2x; the import took the Base Color texture, the first version picked a black map). The green skin dye is off for everyone (M_Person SkinOn 0, no Skin rows); ImportPeople.ps1 passes forward slashes to Unreal (a copy path with 	 broke it).
- **Fort gates opened and tree clearing fixed (2026-11-18 evening, branch agent/townbuild3, asset step not yet run):** `Tools/fort_open_gate.py` (Blender, run first by `BuildWarBuildings.ps1`, writes `Saved/KnightBuild/FortOpen/OakHillFort_300k.glb`) cuts the closed plank door, its old low lintel and the log tips above it out of Meshy's fort and adds two tall gate posts, a double lintel at the palisade's top and two gate leaves swung back inside: opening 3.2 m wide and 3.6 m high at the 1.4x. No Meshy credits. The two trees inside the north fort were FillTrees.csv spots marked on the forts' old empty plots; `JeebOakHill::InWarFootprint(E, N, Margin)` (OakHill.h, from the plots, which take their size from the FModel row) now drops any spot within 2 m of a fort or the watchtower, in both `AOakForest` and `Fill.cpp`'s `TreeKeeps`. Hashes JEEBFOREST and JEEBFILL change (same on every machine).
## Menu look, wand and staff icons, class outlines (2026-11-18 evening, agent `clouds`)

- **Owner's words:** the liquid glass menu "looks outdated ... make it fit the game better"; the wand and staff icons "look too similar"; certain items only for certain classes, shown by an icon outline (wizard purple, troll green, knight grey; general tools none), nothing enforced yet.
- **The kit** (`KnightHUDStyle.cpp`): `jeeb.UI.Style` 1 walnut and brass (default), 2 midnight blue and gold, 0 the old glass. `Panel`, `Tile`, `Gauge`, `Btn`, `CloseBtn` in KnightHUDGlass.cpp branch to `KitPanel`, `KitTile`, `KitGauge`, `KitBtn`, `KitCloseBtn`; `CheckBox`, `SliderBar` and `MapFrame` are new shared helpers (settings tab, LOOK tab, the world map). Panels: a dark outer line, a brass band lit at the top, a dark hairline, a walnut body with a painted grain (`GrainTex`, 256x128, tiles) and a darker edge, brass corner fittings with a rivet (`FittingTex`, 64x64, flipped per corner) on anything 44 px or bigger. Buttons: raised wooden plates with a brass edge, brighter on hover, sinking a pixel when pressed, oxblood (teal in style 2) with a lit edge and two small diamonds when active (tabs), dull when disabled. Tiles keep the old meaning of their tint (cool = a plain cell, bright = hover, warm gold = picked, near-black = a label's backing) and draw as sunk wells; picked ones get a lit brass edge and a warm glow. Bars: dark wells in a thin brass rim, a matte fill with a lit top line. The map is framed like a chart (wooden border, brass line, corner pieces) and its fog is tinted like old paper. Under styles 1 and 2 `FillRR` corners are capped at 5 px and `Outline` is a square 2 px frame, so the overlays drawn by each screen (selected bar slot, dimmed cells) match. Both textures are made in code at first use (nothing to cook). Colours are written as sRGB 0-255 (`SC`) in `MakeWalnut` / `MakeMidnight`.
- **Fonts:** the engine has only Roboto (no serif); titles stay the spaced-capital ivory lettering (`TitleTextAt`). A serif display face would need a font the owner adds (an OFL one such as Cinzel), imported under `/Game`.
- **Class:** `Kinds.csv` column `Class` (wizard / troll / knight / all; `FKindRow::Class`, `JeebItems::KindClass`). Wizard: wand, staff. Troll: broadsword. Knight: sword, spear, katana, bow, longbow, crossbow, shield. All: axe, pickaxe, battle axe, fishing pole, armour, cape, rings, amulets, helm brush. `DrawIcon` draws a classed item three times: grown by about 4.5% of its size in the class colour, grown by a hair in near-black, then as usual (`Poly` grows each shape along its corner normals while `IconRim` > 0; see-through parts are skipped). The tooltip gets a coloured "Wizard only" (purple) / "Troll only" (green) / "Knight only" (steel blue, so it stands apart from the white lines) line (a `\x02<class>` marker, `TipLineColor`). `CanEquip` is unchanged.
- **Wand and staff icons** (`KnightHUDIcons.cpp`): the wand corner to corner (no tilt), pommel, wrapped grip, tapering shaft, collar and a faceted crystal; the staff tilted 12 degrees, a long shaft with grip and band, a big shaded orb with a soft glow, held by a collar and three claws. Orb and crystal colours by tier (`MagicColor`): tin copper, iron blue, silver pearl, gold yellow, emerald green, diamond cyan (the gem colours of tin and silver were both near white). The 3D models are unchanged.
- **Pictures:** `-JeebShot -ShotUI` (`KnightHUDShotUI.cpp`) opens every screen in turn and saves `ui_<screen>.png`; `-ShotUIMenu` the start screen in each look. The best ones are in `Docs/UI/`.
## Oak Hill's new layout (2026-11-18 evening, townlab agent)

Owner: "why is the entrance to the church tucked away behind a house ... move buildings around so that they make sense", review every item round the town (the town hall banners floated), make the world feel more natural. Plan, reasoning, the building table and the before/after plans: `Docs/TownLab/OakHillLayout_2026-11-18.md`.
- **Layout:** a market town along one street: West Street from the bridge (smithy, bank) through the market place round the oak, on as East Street to the church door, so the church tower closes the view from the bridge and the spawn. Town hall on the market's north side, door to the oak (Ring with 2.8 m more forecourt). Shops and stalls round the ring as before. Houses along North Lane, Church Lane, Fort Lane and the southwest and southeast roads, each 0.7 m off its street with its door to it, yards behind. Churchyard (graveyard plot) round the church's east end, allotments behind the southeast road, training ground at the south fork. Forts, watchtower, ruins, bridge unchanged.
- **OakHill.h:** `FPlot` has `DoorDir` (door bearing; hand-set for the new buildings, filled from `BuildingYaw` for the rest at the end of `Plots()`) and `Id` (Bank, Smithy, TownHall, Church, House_SE1, ...). `D(Name, Id, Model, E, N, Door)` places a building by hand; `BuildingYaw` follows `DoorDir` when set. New `PlotIndexById`, `DoorAt`. The roads are new paths (`Roads()`); OakForest's Birch Grove road starts at the southeast road's end (769.3, 699.3). The PLOT log line prints id and door. Plot order kept (FillPlots, the logs and FillOakHill use the indices). The layout was designed and checked offline (`Saved/Scratch_layout/new.py` in the townlab copy reproduces the spacing pass: no building moves).
- **Fill:** FillOakHill rows with `Site` `Plot` are in their plot's own frame (metres along the model's X, Y; Yaw relative), so doorsteps, banners, shop rows, the training ground and the graveyard move with their plot from now on. Hung wall sets ignore the grid's footprint cells (steps, porches) when they look for the wall: the town hall banners stopped at its steps a metre out (Fill.cpp `Snug`). Street lamps laid out again along the new streets (20), new junction signs, banner poles, benches. FillPlots: allotment and graveyard outlines moved, the churchyard yard row went, houses 20 and 21 have 5 m yards. FillTrees: 8 spots moved off new plots and streets.
- **Quests:** Pell stands by the smithy counter (`QuestsPeople.csv` anchor smith); Marta's text says the smithy is by the bridge.
- **Checked (one off-screen Field run, 2026-11-19 00:33):** every building's PLOT line matches the plan (no spacing-pass moves for the hand-set ones); the town hall banners hang on the wall (`Docs/TownLab/Pictures/OakHillLayout_*.jpg`: two aerials, church door, West Street, banners, Church Lane, shops, smithy). Hashes: JEEBOAK 618416b8, JEEBFILL total 010c8a38, JEEBFOREST eb58e60b. **Open:** the spacing pass now moves the North Fort about 2.9 m north (X -653 Y 13638, was -945 13656) and the watchtower 3.2 m west (956 14092, was 958 14408): the new Fort Lane and house 12 push them differently; their plot lines were left alone (forts agent's). The smithy's crossed-swords banner hangs from the porch eave over the forge, not on a wall. Not played, not tried online, the counters and Pell's spot not walked to.
## Building pads, the lake, the split, winding rivers, more hills (2026-11-18 evening, agent `water`)

The owner's pictures (Saved/Review/Owner 2, 5, 6, 8, 9): houses half buried on slopes, doors underground; a lake between Oak Hill and Steele Creek; the river splitting round a long island north of Oak Hill; more rolling hills; more natural water.
- **Building pads** (Terrain.h `BuildingPads`, `ApplyPads`, read inside `HeightAt` when `Hills == 2`): built at load time from the plot tables, never hard-coded. Oak Hill's and Blackrock's plots come from `JeebOakHill::Plots()` (model walls box, `FromPx`, `BuildingYaw`, model scale), Steele's from `JeebSteele` plots (`Level`). Each pad is the walls box plus 2 m, with a 4 m doorstep apron on the door side, blended out over max(4 m, min(20 m, 6x the height difference)). Pads are mixed by w/(1-w) weights like Oak Hill's TownM, so each flat stays exactly level, and fade out near water. Level: the no-pad height at the centre for level-placed buildings; for the war buildings (`bLevel` false) the median of the ground under the walls, at least 90 cm over the river. A new plot or model gets its pad automatically. Log: `JEEBWATER pad` per building, `JEEBWATER pads: ... hash=`.
- **Fine ground** (Terrain.h `FinePatches`, WorldTerrain.cpp `BuildFine`): 2 m ground (with collision) over Steele Creek, the lake and the split, where the 8 m world mesh was too coarse for pads and banks (Steele's houses were buried partly because of it). Patch edges follow the 8 m mesh's edges, so there are no cracks. `DrawnHeight` knows the patches.
- **Water model** (WorldMap.h): rivers are Catmull-Rom curves at 5 m with sideways winding (two sine waves each; none near the fork, the bridge reach, Steele Creek's river reach or where the river meets the lake) and a width that varies by about +-22%. `Halfs` and `Bends` per point. River 3 is the split (half width 4.7 m) round a long island north of Oak Hill (X 2000-31500, Y 5300-13300; part of it lies in the original square). The lake (`Lake()`: shore polygon with bays, island polygon, a 4 m signed-distance grid `LakeSD`) sits at (-50000, -3600), 63 x 74 m radii turned 20 degrees; the main river runs into its north end and out of its south. `NearWater` caches the last point and lists every body (4 rivers plus the lake); `RiverDistance` / `WaterEdge` / `FlowAt` / `IslandWoods` read it. Banks: deeper in wide water (`WideDepth`), sandbars on inside bends (`Shoal`), a sand beach round the lake (GroundMask.h). Water is one sheet of 4 m cells (`BuildWater`), no overlapping ribbons at the fork.
- **Hills** (Hills.cpp): a third, broader pass with a few bigger rises, and Steele Creek gets its own ring of hills; the grid is now rectangular, reaching Steele. Roads (and Steele's long road) are still levelled to about 5 degrees; the towns, the square and the water keep out.
- **Trees:** `TreeBlocked` keeps trees 6 m from the water's edge; the lake island and the split island are wooded (one line in OakForest.cpp's `Forest()` edge lambda: `IslandWoods`).
- **Kept:** the bridge reach (-3550, 12110) is exactly as before (log `JEEBWATER bridge reach`, half width 700). Every hash that depends on the ground or rivers changes (JEEBHILLS, JEEBFOREST, JEEBFILL heights, JEEBOAK and JEEBSTEELE levels, JEEBRUINS, JEEBFOE camps).
- **Hashes** (server and client equal, all 22): JEEBWATER shape 868503e5, pads e702fc4b, JEEBHILLS 2bf01d80 / finished ground 2656a15f, JEEBOAK a5e8027e, JEEBFOREST e6883ae7, JEEBRUINS 52a9e70f, JEEBSTEELE f17af50e, JEEBFILL total 6bb7b1d5, JEEBFOE camps 4fefb8a5.
- **Open:** pads at different levels close together on Oak Hill's west slope leave short steep bits between them (log: House -1230 16089 1.18, House -2633 15235 1.30, North Fort 1.18, Watchtower 1.37; 0.4-0.56 without the pads); the lake beach is still a pale sand colour.
- Layout was checked offline first with `Saved/Scratch_water/layout.py` (Blender's Python with numpy): the road keeps about 19 m from the water, the square's forest ring 22 m.
## The bush overhaul (2026-11-18 evening, agent grassline)

Owner, after playing: "I thought I asked to remove all these plants and delete them from the game" (the dark flat clump by a foundation, picture 3, and the small bright ones at the town hall wall, picture 4), then "too many bushes in the game, they aren't colored correctly, and they all look too cartoony and not fluffy ... get rid of all the bushes we have ... Let's do an overhaul here."
- **Removed everywhere:** the meadow's leaf-card domes (`AMeadow::PlaceBushesAndFlowers`, `MakeBushMesh`, `JeebMeadowDropCovered`: about 960 tries round the square; these were picture 3's dark flat clumps), the forest's undergrowth bushes (OakForest.cpp kinds BushLeafy/Round/Berries/Dome, ShrubLow, PlantClump, the two flowering ones: 2763 instances; the undergrowth branch now draws its number and skips, so logs and stumps keep their places), and every fill bush (FillKinds BushBall/Berries/Dome/Leafy/Round/Spiky/WhiteFlowers, PlantClump, ShrubLow, HedgeBox/Segment/End; picture 4's were ShrubLow/Bush rows of the plots' verges and FrontFlowers). Assets deleted: those 12 kinds' SM/MI/T in `/Game/TownDecor`, `/Game/M_Bush`, `/Game/T_BushLeaves`; `town_decor_copy.py` now BLOCKS them, `build_materials.py`/`BuildMaterials.ps1` no longer make M_Bush. Fill data: sets FlowerClump, HedgeBoxPair, BushRow, FrontFlowers deleted; bushes taken off lamps, signs, benches, banner poles, graves and the plaza bed; the plaza, Blackrock fountain yard and lawn-edge flower rows in FillOakHill.csv deleted; zone mixes keep their weights (FlowerClump folded into BushClump) so other props are unchanged.
- **New bushes** (`/Game/Bushes`, `Tools/BuildBushes.ps1`: `bush_atlas_export.py` writes the DZ leaf atlases, `bush_build.py` (Blender) builds each bush from leaf-spray cards cut from those atlases, normals pointing out from the bush's middle so it lights as one soft mass, vertex colour = wind weight; `bush_import.py` imports with a far LOD (40% of the cards, grown), no collision, no Nanite). Materials `MI_BushAspen`, `MI_BushAspenSmall`, `MI_BushOak` are children of the pack trees' one-colour twins in `/Game/TreeGroves`, so the bushes share the trees' leaves, wind and "Fall Color Tint" path. Kinds: BushFull (aspen, 2.0 x 1.7 m, 1800 tris), BushSmall (aspen, 1.2 x 0.75, 840), BushFine (cork oak, 1.7 x 1.35, 1320), BushLow (arching cork-oak clump, 1.4 x 0.7, 528), BushHedge (2.5 x 1.3 x 1.4, 2400). The flowering bush is a BushSmall with 3-5 of the grass pack's meadow flowers (flower_14_06, flower_17_05) through it. No Meshy credits spent; nothing from Fab or the Meshy library (the packs hold no shrubs; their leaf atlases do).
- **Placement:** `ABushes` (Bushes.h/.cpp, spawned after the bandit camps, clients only: visual, no collision) on a jittered 5 m lattice, each cell with its own seed: forest edges (some open ground and some canopy, JeebCanopy, within 7 m), a few under the trees, river banks and lake shores (JeebWorld::InWater within 4.5 m, so new lakes count). Never in water, on roads (1.5-1.8 m), on NoGroundCover, fill cover, slopes over 0.6, in a solid (a capsule overlap above the ground against the world's collision: trunks, logs, rocks, ruins, walls, camps), inside Oak Hill's box except its banks, on the original square or in Steele Creek's middle. Towns: the fill's plots (FillUses.csv: Bush1 at most one in a yard corner and one on a fence, Hedge1 along a garden fence; BushClump in Blackrock's cottage, hut, pig pen, pond and graveyard zones), so town bushes follow the buildings when the layout moves. Leaf colour live: `jeeb.Trees.DzGreen` x `jeeb.Bush.Shade` (0.85), applied to the wild bushes and the fill's (ApplyLook). `jeeb.Bush.Density` (rebuilds), `jeeb.Bush.Reach`, `jeeb.Bush.Shadows` (0). Draw distance 45-70 m (HISM cull distances).
- **Second pass (same night, after the coordinator's look check: the first bushes read as dark cabbages and agaves):** `bush_build.py` now packs hundreds of small twig cards (30-45 cm, each a dozen or more leaves a few cm long; the bare stalk cut off) over an uneven rounded shell that reaches the ground, with an inner layer so the middle is closed and a few longer stray twigs for a soft broken edge. One material per kind (`MI_BushAspenSmall`, the long narrow leaves, deleted). `jeeb.Bush.Shade` 1 (was 0.85): the trees' exact leaf colour. Sizes and triangles (LOD0 / LOD1 at 30% of the cards): Full 2.2 x 1.95 m 3600 / 540, Small 1.7 x 1.3 m 1680 / 252, Fine (oak) 2.1 x 1.8 m 3040 / 456, Low (a wide aspen mound, clear of the tall grass) 2.4 x 1.5 m 2880 / 432, Hedge 2.9 x 1.4 x 1.5 m 4000 / 600. Placement, counts and data unchanged (the flowers moved out to the rim). Test: `-BushLineupX=X -BushLineupY=Y` (non-Shipping) puts one of each kind in a row for pictures.
- **Counts (log JEEBBUSH, first run):** before about 2763 forest + up to 960 meadow + the fill's (several hundred) = about 3700-4000; after 1404 wild (184 banks, 1178 forest edges, 42 under trees: full 388, small 527, fine 238, low 251) + 657 meadow flowers in the flowering bushes + 51 in the fill = 1455 bushes. Placement takes about 370 ms on a client at load. JEEBFILL total hash changes (fa51a251); JEEBFOREST's undergrowth count drops from 3961 to 1198.
- Not verified: frame time (not measured; fewer instances and far fewer triangles than the old 12k-triangle TownDecor bushes), online (clients only, fixed seeds, nothing replicated), a package (the new meshes are in /Game/Bushes, cooked with /Game; nothing loads the deleted ones by name).

**Night look-review fixes (2026-11-19, agent/water):**
- Bridge lanterns: the four end posts carry the square black lantern (TownDecor LanternBlack, 70 cm, turned 45 degrees) standing on the post's measured top (443 cm over the pier bottoms; the old 140 over the deck sat the oil lantern 30 cm inside the post, a black nub), light at 40% of its height (`ABridge::BuildDecor`).
- Beaches: `Palette.ini` Sand 0.17, 0.135, 0.088 (was 0.40, 0.33, 0.21, cream; affects the sea beach too); M_Ground's sand blend softer (ramp 1.6, was 2.6) and sand no longer takes the low-sun SkyFill emissive that made it glow (`world_material.py`); the lake island's band about half as wide (`GroundMask.h`).
- Weeds: the 66 `SM_TL_Weeds` lime clumps at the TownLab buildings' feet (Oak Hill, Steele Creek, the lineup) are gone from `TownLabDecor.h` and `Tools/townlab_decor.py` (its random draws kept so nothing else moves). The asset stays, unused.
- Magic bolt: `/Game/Props/M_GlowOrb` is built by `Tools/bolt_glow.py` (fixlook_assets.py step 3 runs it): exposure-locked like the staff's orb, deeper cyan (0.02, 0.72, 1.0), heart only a little lighter, `jeeb.Magic.BoltGlow` 0.9 (was 4, a white blob at release), the ball 0.2 (was 0.28).

## The troll's club (2026-11-19, agent `club`; Docs/TrollPlayablePlan.md section 3)

- New gear kind `K_Club` (key `Club`, appended at the end of `EKind` after `K_Crossbow` so no saved id moves; gear ids 3300-3305). Kinds.csv row `Club`, "Troll Club", Class troll, two-handed, damage 14-20, cooldown 0.95 (between the sword and the broadsword). It `FightsLikeSword` (the sword's swings, jab combo and dash stab), trains Attack, sits in the hand slot, `BladeReach` 100 cm (the head ends 107 cm from the grip; 100 keeps the hit boxes on the banded head, not past the studs). The code-drawn icon (`KnightHUDIcons.cpp`) is a banded club with a studded cap; the dictionary lists it as "Troll Club". No `WeaponDesign::Build` case: it is a model (`JeebProps::PathFor` -> `/Game/Props/SM_Club`).
- Model: Meshy, from a picture. The library tag pages answered 403 to a plain fetch (the owner's Chrome was not used), so it was generated: picture task `01a1f427-17ff-7343-8614-d333a698b6f0` (nano-banana-2-1, background removed), model task `01a1f427-8ee0-7727-999f-c9e68f20c8eb` (meshy-t2.5, smart-topology, 4000 tris, meshy-7 texture, PBR). Links: https://www.meshy.ai/workspace (both tasks are in the owner's Meshy workspace history under those ids). About 19 credits (balance 920 after). Source kept in `Art/Props/Originals/Club_Meshy.glb` (not in git), `Art/Props/Club.glb` the build input.
- Picture prompt: "Stylised 3D fantasy game weapon, three-quarter view, whole object in frame on a plain white background. A big two-handed troll's war club: a long straight wooden haft that swells gradually into a heavy rounded club head, rich polished medium-dark brown wood with a subtle touch of painted grain and a little natural variation in tone, like stained walnut: brown, not yellow, not orange. Two dark steel bands around the club head and a dark steel cap on its tip with a few short blunt studs, a dark leather wrap on the grip and a small dark steel pommel cap. Slightly stylised, not quite realistic and a tiny bit cartoonish: sleek natural proportions, crisp bevels, hand-painted textures, rich clean colours, no rust, no dirt." (The model came out with three bands.)
- `Tools/prop_clean.py` PROPS "Club": 125 cm long (sized for the 2.2 m troll; the knight's sword is 92), grip share 0.86 (near the pommel end of the leather wrap), head along game -Z. The steel bands, studded cap and pommel cap take the tier (grey outside the wrap's -24..7 cm); the leather wrap is left out of the mask. Wood evened to the house brown.
- Not done: no held-pose or grip check on the troll (the troll pawn is agent `troll`'s work); in the knight's hand it would be oversized. Nothing restricts equipping by class yet, so a knight can hold it too.
## The playable troll (2026-11-19, agent `troll`; plan Docs/TrollPlayablePlan.md, sections 1, 2, 4-7; the club, section 3, is for another agent)
- **Class:** `ATrollCharacter` (KnightCharacter.h) is `AKnightCharacter` with `bIsTroll` set in its constructor, so every machine knows from the class; all the knight's move, combat, inventory, network and HUD code runs unchanged, with the troll's differences inside it (`IsTroll()`, `WalkNow()`, `SprintNow()`). Code: `KnightCharacterTroll.cpp`, `KnightAnimatorTroll.cpp`; numbers in Tuning.ini `[Troll]`.
- **Choosing him:** `-PlayAs=Troll` on the command line. Single player: read by `AKnightGameMode::GetDefaultPawnClassForController`. Online: the client sends `AKnightPlayerController::ServerChooseClass` just before its login (only taken before login). Test builds, single player: console `jeeb.PlayAs troll|knight` saves and swaps in place. No menu choice yet (the three-profile account system is separate).
- **Body (retarget choice):** neither an IK Retargeter nor a Mixamo re-rig. The troll's Meshy rig has the knight's 24 bones under other names, so `FClipLib::GetTroll` copies the knight's library (same bone frames, same 85 clips) with the troll's bone lengths and places from `Content/Data/TrollRig.csv` (`Tools/troll_rig_export.py`, Blender, from both .glb rigs); hips paths scale 1.25x. Every pose, hit box, grip and foot plant comes out troll-sized from the same code, server included (no engine animation at run time). The troll's own mesh (`/Game/People/SK_Troll`, attached under the hidden knight mesh) copies the pose: each bone turned in mesh space by as much as ours has turned from the bind pose (`TrollPose`). SK_Troll was imported mirrored front to back against the knight; the fit finds it (log `JEEBTROLL mesh ... 0.00 cm, mirrored 1`) and the component carries a -1 Y scale. Hurt boxes x `TrollHurtScale` 1.45; capsule 52 x 120; camera 640 back, 85 up. No armour or mail body shows on him yet.
- **Moves:** everything the knight does, except: the kick is a **left punch** (wheel up; `TrollPunch`: the sword guard under it, the fist driven ahead of the left shoulder with the chest turning in, on the kick's timing; four hit balls on the fist and forearm, `TrollFistRadius`; same damage and rules as the kick); the roll is a **quick hop** (wheel down + direction, 1 s cooldown; launch 620 across, 380 up: 243 cm, 0.4 s); **charged jump**: hold Space on the ground (he crouches, a gold bar fills under the climb bar), let go: under 0.18 s is the normal jump, else a launch from 250 cm (least) to 915 cm (full, 1 s held) across, peaks 90-200 cm, in the steered direction (else ahead); 8 s cooldown (grey bar), a held press during it gives a normal jump. The air speed clamp and air braking are off while a hop or leap flies (`bAirLaunch`, `FreeFlight`). Server: `ServerLeapCharge` / `ServerLeap` (charge capped to what the server saw held + 0.15 s, its own cooldown and ground check), `ServerHop`. Speeds 425 / 623.1 (0.85x of today's 500 / 733.1; the plan's 542 was 0.85x the old 637.5); slide floor 637.5.
- **Gear:** a troll may equip only Kinds.csv Class `troll` or `all` gear (`JeebItems::CanEquip`, `OwnerIsTroll`), plus TEMPORARILY the knight's sword until the club exists (its Kinds.csv row with Class troll and its WeaponDesign entry will pass by themselves; then drop the `K_Sword` exception in Inventory.h). The broadsword is already troll class. Knights are still not held to their class.
- **Measured (MoveLab, single player):** full charge 915-916 cm across in 0.93 s (target 914), half 583, least 251; cooldown: a full charge at 4 s became a tap jump, at 10 s it leapt again; hop 242-244 cm in all 8 directions.
- **Reviews:** online PASS (troll on server and both clients, troll model on the other client, no corrections or errors; the jump itself was not driven online then: `-TrollNetDo=` added after). Motion: punch hung at full stretch (eased since), charged jump goes crouch to tuck with no take-off stretch (open), hop and walk unreadable in the pictures.
- **Polish (2026-11-19, agent `troll`):** the charged jump now has a take-off stretch: legs out straight, arms swung up, chest open for about 0.16 s, then eased into a tuck over a quarter second (deeper with more charge). The code is `FKnightAnimator::TrollLeapAir` and `FInput::LeapFly`, set from `bAirLaunch`/`LeapShare`, so it shows only on the owner and the server, not on other clients. A troll's starting kit is the common club in the hand, the light tin suit and no bow or shield (`ResetToStartingKit`, also called in BeginPlay for a troll with no save). The `K_Sword` exception in `CanEquip` is gone. Unequipping never asks `CanEquip`, so an old save's sword can be taken off. Motion review PASS (jump, punch, club swing and dash). Visual review: the club is the right scale and the idle pose is fine. Open: the sword-swing follow-through brings the long club across his belly (cswingR 26-30), the dash reads as a sweep from the front, and the grip couldn't be judged in the dusk pictures.

- **The troll's own club swing (2026-11-21, agent `troll`):** in the sword's swing the club's follow-through crossed his belly. For a troll holding `K_Club`, jab 1 and jab 2 (animator keys 30/31) now call `FKnightAnimator::TrollClubSwing` (KnightAnimatorTroll.cpp) instead of the sword clips. Procedural from MoveU, so the Jab1/Jab2 From/To/Pause timing in Tuning.ini and every hit stay as they were (hit boxes ride the blade bone; server and other machines read the same replicated state). Swing 1: overhead from the right shoulder, ends low and out beside the right hip. Swing 2: flat sweep at arm's length from the right across the front, ends out on the left. Each is key poses (hand place in arm lengths, club direction, chest twist; the table is in the function) with the right hand reached, the club turned onto its line, the left hand reached up the haft and its fist closed (`TrollHaftW`). Charge/smash, dash and the punch are unchanged. Also touched: a dispatch line in the sword case and the left-fist weight in `SetFists` (KnightAnimator.cpp), two members in KnightAnimator.h. Frames: Saved/Review/TrollSwing. Not judged: the end pose of swing 2 reads as the club held up near his left shoulder; the left fist's turn on the haft; the lit-dusk frames were dark.

- **LOW's "old TV" static on ground and grass (2026-11-21, agent `lowlook`):** cause: the soft shadow filter (`r.Shadow.FilterMethod 1`, PCSS) scatters its samples per pixel and relies on the anti-aliasing history to smooth them. HIGH's TSR does; LOW's TAA, with its `r.Tonemapper.Sharpen 1.2`, left moving speckle in every shadow, worst in tree shade on dirt and grass when the camera moves. Found with `-ShotLow` eyes at 2190 20250 (low sun 0.15) toggling one suspect per eye with anti-aliasing off: only `FilterMethod 0` removed the speckle; the tree-shade dither (`jeeb.Forest.ShadeDensity` 1 / `ShadowFade` 0), contact shadows, sharpening, screen percentage and the grass pack did not. Fix: LOW uses `r.Shadow.FilterMethod 0` (PCF) in `ApplyGraphics`; HIGH unchanged. PCF is cheaper than PCSS, so no frame cost (not timed). Shadow edges on LOW are a little firmer. Pictures: `Saved/Review/LowStatic/` (`before_after_pan100.png` and `_pan60.png`: left before, right after, camera panning; `raw_noAA_suspects.png`: AA off, base / filter 0 top, contact off / sharpen 0 bottom).
- **Troll facing, arm and test swap (2026-11-21, agent `troll`):** (1) He faced backwards and an arm looked behind his back: Meshy rigged SK_Troll back to front (its toe bones and head-front marker point out of his back), so the bind-pose fit only matched mirrored (-1 Y scale), which showed his skin back to front. `MakeTrollMesh` now leaves toes, head-front and end bones out of the fit: plain turn R(0), no mirror, 13 cm mean miss (log `JEEBTROLL mesh ... mirrored 0`). Same on every machine (built from the asset). `-TrollOldMirror` (test builds) brings the old fit back for comparisons. Pictures: Saved/Review/TrollFix. (2) The knight/troll test swap froze input after knight -> troll -> knight: each pawn's input mapping context stayed applied after the pawn died (the subsystem holds it), and swapping from the open Tab screen left look ignored, cursor on, UI input mode. `SwapClassForTest` now closes screens, removes the old context, clears mappings, resets ignore-look/move, cursor and input mode; logs `JEEBSWAP now ...` and 4 s later `JEEBSWAP check ...`. `-SwapTest` with `-ShotCmds=jeeb.PlayAs~troll` chains four swaps (all passed: possessed, input component, context applied, nothing ignored). Single player only, as before. (3) **Smear still open:** troll-only (the knight's same swing over the same grass is crisp); not caused by the mirror (same ghosting with and without it); TSR at 60% screen percentage; `VisualizeReprojection` shows reprojection errors on his swinging forearm and club. Not yet tried: `r.AntiAliasingMethod 0`, the NPC models (same M_Person, same import) for comparison, a knight-style held material, skin cache off.
## The Dark Plate armour set (2026-11-21, agent `darkplate`)

Owner: "a new set of armor (split into pieces like the current knight armor) ... to fit over his chainmail base ... use these pictures as inspiration and don't worry about coloring them for the color levels". Inspiration: `Saved/Review/DarkPlate/37-40` (another game's black plate; ours is an original design in its mood): blackened steel, layered swept-back blade plates, finned pauldrons, a crested helm with two swept horns and a narrow slit, a ribbed breastplate with a centre ridge, pointed faulds, finned knee cops.
- **Made in Meshy** (API, no community model was close): each concept picture is `image-to-image` (nano-banana-pro, 9 credits) restyling the matching Classic piece's own render (`Saved/KnightBuild/PiecePictures/<piece>/before_front_tex.png`), so framing, size and layout stay those of the Classic piece; then `image-to-3d` (`latest`, 10k triangles, PBR, symmetry on, 30 credits). Concepts and models in `Art/Knight DarkPlate/` (`darkplate_<piece>.glb`, `_concept.png`). Five pieces as the suit's: chest (breastplate, belt and faulds), legs (greaves and sabatons), helmet, gauntlets (forearms and hands; no upper-arm tube: see the rerebraces below), shoulders.
- **Fitted** with the Classic suit's own tool and numbers: `Tools/BuildDarkPlate.ps1` runs `knight_piece_clean.py` with each Classic piece's fit; new options there: `pre=sx,sy,sz[,dx,dy,dz]` (lays a new piece onto the Classic piece's frame first; the legs use 0.92 across), `armpts=` (15 numbers: the arm piece's upper-tube top, elbow, wrist, hand line and elbow dome, on that piece), `ascale=`, `aroll=`, `ablunt=`; newer Meshy pictures (Image_0, Image_1, normal) are renamed by what they feed. Helmet without the Classic's chin-loop cut.
- **Into Unreal:** `Tools/ImportDarkPlate.ps1` (editor closed; `knight_darkplate_export.py` then `knight_darkplate_import.py`): `/Game/DarkPlate/SK_DarkPlate` on `/Game/NewSuit/SK_NewSuit_Skeleton`, slots named as the suit's, Meshy's normal maps used (the Classic suit uses a flat normal), FistR/FistL morphs on its gauntlets (`knight_fist.py`). Run it again after any `ImportNewSuit.ps1` (that wipes and remakes the skeleton).
- **In the game:** armour style 3 (`AS_Dark`, `NumArmourStyles` 4; ArmourStyles.csv fourth row "Dark Plate": StopMul 1.1, WeightMul 1.3, PriceMul 1.5). Item ids as every style (style x 10 + tier), so every tier exists and names read "Iron Dark Plate Helmet"; the tier sets only its numbers, the look is one colour. Stores, loot and random kits roll only the first three styles (`NumRolledArmourStyles`): the Test kit's "Dark Plate armour" page hands it out. `FKnightAnimator::Wear(Piece, Tier, Style)`: a Dark Plate piece hides the suit's section and shows its own on `DarkComp` (a follower of the suit, made when first worn, fists set like the bare mail's). Look: `ApplyDarkLook` (Iron's shine; `jeeb.Armour.DarkLift` 0.55 how far its dark paint is lifted, `jeeb.Armour.DarkTint` 0.82_0.86_0.98). His mail under the plate is the usual `SK_NewSuitUnder` (weights from the Classic plates). Foes and the armour wall still wear the Classic plates.
- **Review fixes (2026-11-21):** (1) the mail sleeve between pauldron and elbow read as black and white leopard print: `Tools/knight_rerebrace.py` (called by the export, before the fists) copies a band of each vambrace (the same triangles and texture) onto the upper arm, end for end, each point kept at its own height over the mail (rays to the mail arm, 0.6 to 3 cm), weighted to the upper arm bone. Numbers at its top (`SRC_T0/T1`, `DST_S0/S1`, `SPIN`). A little mail still shows in the armpit. (2) the gauntlet hand was 60% of the Classic fist and rolled a quarter turn, so the fist closed beside the grip: `knight_piece_clean.py` `ahand=len,width` (1.22, 1.15) and `aroll=-90` bring it to the Classic hand's size and facing, and the Classic fist numbers (`knight_fist.py` "suit") now close it round the grip; `atop=` (tube start, top) also exists, unused.
- Test pictures: `-ShotWear=<tier> -ShotStyle=3` (MoveLab), with `-ShotUnderRed` for mail coming through.
- **Troll body stipple (2026-11-21, agent `troll`):** the owner's "not crisp" troll. Measured with films at 120 fps (MoveLab walking at the camera, Field club swing, knight as control): `r.AntiAliasingMethod 0` showed the stipple already in the raw frame, a speckled patch on his shoulders and back that moved with him; `r.Shadow.FilterMethod 0` (PCF) removed it, contact shadows, `r.SkinCache.Mode 0`, `r.SkeletalMeshLODBias` and mip bias did not; `ShowFlag.VisualizeMotionBlur` showed he writes velocity like the knight. Cause: the soft-shadow filter's per-pixel scatter in his own head and arm shadow (a wide penumbra on a big matte body; the knight's metal hides it), which TSR cannot settle while he moves (same family as the 2026-11-15 shadow speckle). Fix: the troll casts an inset (per-object) shadow, much finer on him, so the same kernel covers a narrow penumbra (`jeeb.Troll.InsetShadow`, 1 default, 0 = before; set in `TrollPose`). Cost: one small per-object shadow depth pass per drawn troll, not frame-timed. Still open: during the fast club swing he reads a little softer than the knight's sword swing (fast large limbs under TSR at 60%), and a checker dither shows in raw frames on the club and his feet where the ground shadow falls (TSR hides it). Pictures: Saved/Review/TrollFix 5-8.
