The first version, for the record: House_CLS1 (behind the church) was the one open house: `SM_TL_HouseOpen` with the doorway cut (`Tools/interior_house_open.py`), a room by `AHouseInterior` from `Content/Data/InteriorHouse.csv` (tavern pack shell, free props), solid boxes on every machine, lights only near the camera, camera arm halved inside. Costs about +0.4 ms HIGH / +0.2 LOW at the door. `-NoOpenHouse`, `jeeb.Test.GoHouse 1`, `-StartAtOpenHouse`, `jeeb.Interior.*` sliders. Assets: `Tools/BuildInterior.ps1`.


## The open world: the northern range, snow, roads, trees, town sites (2026-11-22, agent `openworld`; Docs/OpenWorldPlan.md)

Owner: "I want the mountains into the north to be twice as tall, and I want them to have snow. Please replace the falling leave animation with a falling snow animation that's sometimes clear and sometimes constantly snowing ... more snow the higher up you go ... add roads and trees to the rest of the map. For the towns, just place a house ... as a placeholder ... some areas to be heavily wooded, but others to be more grass plains or intermittent wooded areas. I want less trees the higher you go on the mountains, and more pines the higher you go ... review the topography of the map outside oak hill and redo it ... something like the skyrim and hogwarts and breath of the wild topography."

- **The land** (`OpenWorld.h/.cpp`, namespace `JeebOpen`; log `JEEBOPEN`): one grid over the whole world on the 8 m mesh's own points, added in `JeebTerrain::HeightAt` after `JeebHills::Offset` (only for the full land, Hills == 2; `bOpen` false leaves it out, and Oak Hill's hills and forest roads are worked out without it). It is zero within 550 m of Oak Hill's plaza (whole from 700 m), round Steele Creek and between the two (JeebHills' land), in the original square, the sea and the four level town sites (there it only takes Oak Hill's hills off again: Birch Grove lies in their rim).
  - North of about X 50,000-60,000 the old range's share of `LandHeight` is taken off and a new one put on: foot about 610 m north of the origin, 300 m of climb to the divide, summits where a slow noise says plus a fixed second mass east of the river (90000, 64000), rounded ridge lines, spurs, gullies, a band of foothills, a valley where the river runs (none within 15 m of the water, whole about 150 m out), falling to the coast. Highest ground 100.1 m at (95200, 0); the old range reached 48 m.
  - Elsewhere: rolling country, a few metres on plains to about 20 m in hill country (a 520 m noise picks which).
  - Then, on the finished ground (sea and rivers applied): slopes cut to 35 degrees except rock bands on the range (terraced, to about 63 degrees: climbable, the world mesh is tagged `JeebClimb`), convex places eased down toward their 40 m average twice (rounded tops, as round Oak Hill), one 3x3 blur. 185 cells of 8 m are steeper than 45 degrees (rock bands).
  - Don't: read `JeebTerrain::PathDefs` or call `HeightAt` with `bOpen` true from inside `BuildGrid` (it would re-enter the grid's own static).
- **Roads** (`JeebOpen::RoadLines`, appended LAST in `PathDefs` as Kind 2 forest roads): an A* over every second grid point (16 m, sixteen headings, a step costs its length x (1 + 30 x grade squared), never within 15 m of water), control points every 48 m, smoothed. Each road's height is the ground softened over 20 m, limited to `OpenRoadGrade` (0.11, 6.3 degrees) and clamped inside that slope's cones on both ends so it arrives on the ground as it is; its corridor is mixed into the grid like the forest roads' (level 4 m either side, banks 12-45 m). Five roads: Oak Hill's north road on to Silver Point (324 m), its east road on to Anchorage (212 m), a fork off Steele Creek's long road to Birch Grove (432 m, over a 29 m hill in a cut up to 18 m deep), Birch Grove to Palmer Square (592 m) and Birch Grove north to the east branch opposite Oak Hill's south-east road (424 m). Steepest measured over 15 m: 3.5 to 6.7 degrees. No bridge or ford at the east branch. `Paths.cpp`: the forest roads' lift now wraps every 24 pieces (it grew 1.2 mm a piece and the new pieces would have floated 10 cm).
- **Snow on the ground**: `JeebOpen::SnowRaw(X, Y, Z, Up)` (0 at 45 m, 1 at 75 m, plus real noise at 90 m and 23 m, less on steep ground so rock shows, none south of X 35,000-55,000 so only the northern range has it). `AWorldTerrain::BuildLand` writes it + 1 into the world mesh's SECOND UV (U; the material takes the 1 off, so a ground with no second UV, which reads 0, gets none: without that the material's noise alone drew a faint lattice of snow specks over the original square); `M_Ground` (the snow lines of `Tools/world_material.py`) reads texture coordinate 1, adds a little of the stone patches' noise mix and the ragged 1.5 m noise, and lerps to the palette's Snow. Grounds that give no second UV (the square, the towns, the fine patches) read 0. For the record: the material's own tiled noise pictures thresholded by height showed a repeating weave along the snow's edge, near and far. `JeebGround::FMask::Snow` (the same function, clamped) thins the meadow's grass in snow.
- **The land beyond the edges** (`AWorldTerrain::BuildBeyond`, players only, no collision): from the new peaks you see over the map's north, west and south edges, where there was nothing. An apron of 64 m cells out to 1.3 km, then seven 640 m cells to about 5.8 km, starts on the edge line never above the real ground's own edge and eases to far rolling land (more snowy range to the north, seabed off the coast).
- **Snowfall** (`AOpenWorld`, `OpenWorldActor.h/.cpp`): 4,400 small cubes in one instanced set (M_Color, bright white), in a 30 m box that follows the camera (5 m ahead of it, 5 m under to 11 m over); each flake falls 0.9-1.7 m/s with a light drift and sway, wraps round the box, carries its previous transform (`SetHasPerInstancePrevTransforms`, as the leaves did) and shrinks away within 1.2-3.2 m of the camera and under town roofs. How many show: `JeebOpen::Snowing(server clock)` (whole slots of 210 s: slot 0 clear, slot 1 snow, then snow in 45% of slots by a hash of the slot number; eased over 25 s) x 0.7 x (0.6 in the low country up to 1 from 70 m) x `jeeb.Snow.Amount`. Checked on HIGH with a turning, sliding camera (`-ShotEyesMove=15_150_0 -ShotEyesFilm=2.2_6`): crisp dots, no streaks or ghost copies. `AAutumnDecor`'s drifting and gust leaves are off (`jeeb.Leaves.Fall 1`, read when the world is built, brings them back); the fallen leaves stay.
- **Town sites** (`JeebOpen::Sites`): Silver Point, Anchorage, Birch Grove, Palmer Square. Each: the town's spot (moved off the water if it lies on a bank: Anchorage's is 40 m north-east of its `JeebWorld::Towns` spot), radius Flat + 15 m, one `SM_TL_House` with its `_Col` model 16 m from the middle on the side furthest from the roads, door toward the middle (`AOpenWorld::BuildHouses`, every machine; hash in `JEEBOPEN houses`). `KnightHUD.cpp` shows the name as a title banner on walking in, for Oak Hill (75 m from the plaza) and Steele Creek (70 m) too. `JeebTravel::Towns` is unchanged: the four are still closed for fast travel.
- **Trees** (`FOakForestBuild::Wilds` in `OakForest.cpp`, after `Steele`, so older trees keep their places): the forest's 6.5 m lattice over the rest of the map; `JeebOpen::Woods` (a 360 m and a 95 m noise plus regions) gives deep forest (with the forest's glade noise), copses, or plain with lone trees 1.3x larger; with height (share of `PeakTop` 100 m) thinner from 28%, smaller from 40%, none above a ragged 64% treeline; spruce share rises to all by 50%, and to the north; mostly aspens within 300 m of Birch Grove; few spruces toward the south-east. About 9,700 trees (3,160 cork oaks, 1,960 aspens, 4,620 spruces; 350 lone), 1,480 undergrowth, 700 stones; all choppable through the lazy nodes. `-NoWilds` (with `-JeebShot`) leaves them out for timing.
- **Cost** (editor, off screen, 1280x720, one `solo` window, medians of about 800 frames, two runs of the default): frame 6.0-6.4 ms in wooded views, 5.2-5.6 on the open north-east and the peaks. Without the new trees (`-NoWilds`): 0.1 to 0.85 ms less (most in the river valley under the range, 6.05 against 5.20; about 0.4-0.5 at the forest north of Oak Hill and at Birch Grove). Snowing against clear: +0.2 to +0.5 ms a frame (the game thread +0.7 ms for the 3,000 flakes' transforms, mostly hidden behind the render thread). Not measured packaged.
- **Online**: a dedicated server and a client of the same build log the same JEEBOPEN grid 8a5a3fee, roads 32bcdd3d, finished ground 8fd7e7dd, houses fe2779bc, JEEBFOREST 516c8c27, JEEBHILLS e7301f7a, JEEBOAK e901fe68, JEEBSTEELE 04a37d07 (one machine; never on two).
- **Test commands** (non-Shipping): `jeeb.Test.GoTown N`, `jeeb.Test.GoPeak`; looks: `jeeb.Snow.Force`, `jeeb.Snow.Amount`, `jeeb.Snow.Low`, `jeeb.Snow.Size`.
- **Owner's fixes (2026-11-22, pt22 pictures 116-119):** snow falls only where snow lies round the camera (`AOpenWorld::Tick`, `JeebOpen::SnowOnGround` at five spots, eased; none in Oak Hill or the lowlands). The striped "strobing" triangle was shadow acne on 8 m triangles turned just away from a low sun: the ground's underside is now its own component with no shadow (`AWorldTerrain::AddUnderside`) and the sun's slope bias is at least 1 (`DayNight.cpp`; the moon copies it); the same view showed stripes before and none after. Straight ridge lines: the grid's last smoothing is three 5x5 passes (rock bands half, roads and towns none). The land beyond the edges starts on the world mesh's own 8 m edge points (rings 8/24/48 m, then 64 m), its edge smoothed more with distance and eased out over 1.2 km. Moon `LightSourceAngle` 1.5 -> 0.5 (night shadow grain). Feet in snow: `JeebOpen::SnowSink` (up to `SinkDepth` 13 cm by the ground's snow, open ground only, not mounted) lowers the knight's drawn body and hit boxes alike on every machine (`AKnightCharacter::SnowFeet`); footprint decals `/Game/M_SnowPrint` (`Tools/BuildSnowPrint.ps1`) every 70 cm on players' machines, fading after 18-30 s. Hashes: a dedicated server and a client of this build log the same 12 hash lines (JEEBOPEN houses 6de04e63). Frame (editor, off screen, solo, no same-build baseline): peak 7.3 ms, the view north 5.8, the foothills 7.2; the figures above were 5.2-6.4 in similar views, so up to about 1 ms may be new and wants a proper before/after.
- **Checks after (2026-11-22):** before/after in one solo window (main c389da9 vs this branch merged with it, five views, two runs each): frame within run-to-run noise (-0.6 to +0.3 ms), GPU +0.1 to +0.3 ms in some views, +34 draws at the peak; nothing trimmed. A 10-frame walk on snow (`-ShotMove=bowwalk`, HIGH): boots sunk, no smear; one soft round print shows behind him at a time (the next is off the bottom of the view), round rather than boot-shaped. The land past the north edge now has 16 m rings out to 320 m.
- **Open**: a straight crease still shows on a slope at the north edge (Saved/Review/pt22/edge_crease_still_there.png) and grazing sun on a crest draws a saw-tooth shadow from the 8 m triangles (ridge_sawtooth_shadow.png): two rounds done, for the owner (next would be a finer mesh on the range, not more blur); prints are round blobs; the dome in 116 unchanged; no crossing of the east branch; the Steele-Birch road's 18 m cut; the far plain beyond the west and south edges shows the ground picture's repeat from high up; flakes are lit cubes (dim at night); the weather changes nothing else (sky, light, sound); snow does not lie on trees or roads; `JeebTravel` rows for the four towns stay closed; the town-name banner was never seen in a test picture.

## The playable wizard (2026-11-21, agent `wizard`)

Owner: "the third playable character, the wizard ... work with meshy to create a base layer ... helmet to be the wizard hat, the chest plate to be the wizard robe, the boots to be the wizard boots, no gauntlets ... no shoulder armor ... the spell book ... the exact same dimensions as the knight ... a thick white beard ... his naked state like my fourth screenshot ... no gloves ... no scarf ... a transparent blue orb around his body ... for rolling, teleport a few feet ... the dash attack, keep the same animation like the knight".
- **Made in Meshy** (API through `Tools/meshy_call.js`, key from the environment; no community model had the knight's build): the base man is an `image-to-image` (nano-banana-pro) restyle of the Classic knight's own front render (`Saved/KnightBuild/knight_Classic_front.png`), so pose and proportions are the knight's; the robed man is a restyle of that picture, so both are the same man. Each: `image-to-3d` (`latest`, 20k triangles, PBR, symmetry on) then Meshy's rigging. Hat and spell book: a picture each, then `image-to-3d` (6k, 4k). Everything in `Art/Wizard/` (`wizard_<base|robed|hat|book>.glb`, `_rigged.glb`, `_concept.png`, `_task.json` with the task ids).
- **On the knight's skeleton:** `Tools/ImportWizard.ps1` (editor closed). `wizard_export.py` (Blender) moves every point by its bones' offsets (knight joint minus Meshy joint, eased along each bone, mixed by weight), which lays his joints exactly on the knight's 24, renames the weights, fits the hat to each head and the book behind each left hip, and writes one FBX plus preview pictures (`Saved/Wizard/Build/wizard_<look>_<side>.png`; numbers at the script's top: `ZCUT`, `HAT_*`, `BOOK_*`). `wizard_import.py` makes `/Game/Wizard/SK_Wizard` on `/Game/NewSuit/SK_NewSuit_Skeleton`, `MI_Wiz_Base/Robe/Hat/Book` (parent M_KnightSkin) and `M_WizardOrb`. Run it again after any `ImportNewSuit.ps1`.
- **Never shows through:** a worn piece replaces what is under it. Slots: `Wiz_BaseUpper` / `Wiz_RobeUpper` (the whole man above the boot line, head and beard included: the robe swaps the whole upper body), `Wiz_BaseFeet` / `Wiz_RobeFeet`, `Wiz_HatBase` / `Wiz_HatRobe`, `Wiz_BookBase` / `Wiz_BookRobe` (`FKnightAnimator::RefreshWizard`, KnightAnimatorWizard.cpp). The knight's own mesh still carries the pose with every section hidden.
- **Class:** `AWizardCharacter` = `AKnightCharacter` with `bIsWizard` (as the troll's `bIsTroll`). `-PlayAs=Wizard`; online `AKnightPlayerController::ServerChooseWizard` before the login (ClassChoice 2); single player test builds: console `jeeb.PlayWizard 1|0`. The knight's animator, clips, speeds, hit boxes: unchanged.
- **Gear:** armour style 4 `AS_Wizard` (ArmourStyles.csv fifth row; helmet = "Tin Wizard Hat", chest = "Wizard Robe", boots = "Wizard Boots"; no gauntlets or shoulders: `StyleHasPiece`). New kind `K_Book` (Kinds.csv "Book", `JeebNumWornKinds` 25), worn in `Worn[K_Book]`. `CanEquip`: only wizards wear wizard gear and the book; a wizard wears no other armour and no shield, and wields `wizard` and `all` class weapons. Starting kit: tin staff, hat, robe, boots, book. Test kit: "Wizard armour" page, the book under Other. Stores and loot don't roll them (`NumRolledArmourStyles`).
- **Tab screen:** for a wizard the gauntlet cell is the spell book's and the shoulder cell is not drawn (KnightHUDInventory.cpp); knight and troll unchanged.
- **Orb:** `ShieldActive()` is always true for a wizard; holding block shows a see-through blue ball (`WizardOrb`, drawn on every machine from the shared `bBlocking`), stops blows from every side (`bFront`, `GetShieldWedge` 180 degrees) with the shield's own health, drain, break and recovery; its strength is a kite shield of his book's tier (`WizardGuardItem`). No shield arm pose.
- **Teleport:** wheel down + direction, the roll's stamina and cooldown. Only the server moves him (`ServerBlink` -> `DoBlink` -> `BlinkSpot`: the farthest spot within `WizardBlinkDist` on walkable ground near his height, with room for his capsule and a clear line from where he stands), then `MultiBlink` tells every machine: his own moves there, all show the two flashes. Log `JEEBBLINK`.

- Numbers: Tuning.ini `[Wizard]`.

After main's troll-block merge the orb goes through `GuardActive()` (`bIsTroll || bIsWizard || ShieldActive()`); `ShieldActive()` is false for a wizard (no shield drawn, no shield arm). Orb material toned down (it bloomed white). Online test switch: `-WizardNetDo` on a client (teleport at 8 s, orb up at 10 s, down at 13 s; logs `JEEBBLINK`, `JEEBWIZARD`); `Saved/Wizard/net.sh` in the agent's copy ran a server and two clients on port 7795 (`-JeebWorldPort=`).

## Deer to hunt (2026-11-22, agent `deer`; plan and the Red Dead / Breath of the Wild comparison in Docs/DeerPlan.md)
- **What:** stags in herds of 3 to 5: five herds round Oak Hill (`AnimalHerds.csv` plaza rows) and fourteen wild ones spread over the open world by rule (19 herds, 71 animals with today's files). They graze, look up, amble; notice players by sight (30 m in front, a third of that behind, less of a crouched man) and hearing (sprint 24 m, walk 13 m, crouch-walk 6 m, still 4 m); first one stares and the herd looks up, then nearer than 15 m, or 3 m nearer than when noticed, or any hit, and the whole herd bolts at 8.6 m/s (sprint is 7.3), round trees, never toward a town or into deep water; calm 9 s after the last player leaves their senses (or once 66 m clear), then drift home at a walk. Under half health a stag runs a fifth slower.
- **Code:** `Deer.h/.cpp`. `ADeer` (server-owned actor, no Blueprint): replicates `RepMove` (place, yaw, speed; its own struct, the engine's movement replication is off), `RepState` (graze, idle, look, walk, alert, flee, dead), `RepKind` and `RepSize` once, and its `UHealthComponent`. Players' machines glide it toward `RepMove` plus speed x time since the update and stand it on their own ground (`JeebFoes::Ground`, the same on every machine); the clip comes from the state and the speed. Hurt boxes (`GatherHurt`: body, neck, head as `Z_Head` for the x1.5 headshot, two leg capsules) are worked out from where it stands, so the server needs no body; three plain joints (`JBody`, `JNeck`, `JHead`) give arrows something to stick to. `ADeerHerds` (spawned by `AKnightGameState` on every machine, does its work only on the server): places herds 1.5 s after the world build (overlap tests in the build's own frame miss the new trees), `GoodGround` (dry, level, 15 m off roads, 70 m from towns, 90 m from bandit camps, clear of solids), sleeps a herd with no player within 180 m of any of it (1 s tick, 1 update a second), and the players are never sent one past 160 m.
- **Data:** `Content/Data/Animals.csv` (one row per kind: health 24, speeds, senses, respawn 300 s, XP 25, clip speeds, loot) and `AnimalHerds.csv`, read in `FoeData.cpp` beside the foes (`JeebFoes::Animals()`, `Herds()`; the loot line is the foes' format). New materials at the END of `EItemType`: `Venison` (Raw Venison, sells 10) and `Antlers` (Stag Antlers, sells 28); the hide is the existing Beast Hide. Both have a model (ItemModels.h), an icon and a line in the store's sell list. Adding a material means: `EItemType`, the two `MaterialKeys` lists (JeebItemData.cpp, QuestData.cpp) and their asserts, `UJeebSave::ValidId`, the `SellAll` bound in KnightCharacterNet.cpp (it stopped at Bones, so the 2026-10-30 ores could not be sold with "sell all": fixed), `Sellable` in KnightHUDShops.cpp, ItemModels.h, KnightHUDIcons.cpp, Materials.csv.
- **Kill:** loot rolled on the server (1-2 venison; hide 70%; antlers 35%), each thing its own `ALootDrop` behind the body's back; 25 Archery XP if the killer stood further than 4.5 m, else Attack XP; `UQuestComponent::OnFoeBeaten` with `Stag` and `Deer`, and `JeebFoes::IsFoeTarget` accepts both, so a quest's kill step can ask for deer. The body lies 20 s, sinks, is hidden; a new stag stands at the herd's home 300 s after the fall, only while no player is within 60 m of it.
- **Model:** "Stag" by cyber_fox, Meshy community library, CC0 (Art/Animals/Sources.md). `Tools/deer_clean.py` 338k to 20k triangles with baked 2048 colour and 1024 normal pictures; Meshy rigging as a quadruped (5 credits) and seven library clips (21 credits): Deer idle x2, walk, run, Fawn sniff (graze), Cow alert, Cow death (a lying pose: the game blends into it and lowers the body 42 cm). `Tools/BuildDeer.ps1` (deer_export.py, deer_import.py) makes `/Game/Animals` (12 MB): `SK_Stag` with 4 LODs, `M_Animal` (its `Grey` 0.28 and `Tint` 0.80 0.78 0.76 calm the model's strong orange coat), 7 clips. The body is taken from the clips file. Shown at 1.3x (a red deer stag: as tall as the knight at the head, antlers over him).
- **Pitfall found (the stag was invisible at first):** written to FBX the usual way from metres, Blender puts a x100 scale and the axis turn on the armature object; Unreal folded that into the body's root bone but NOT into the clips' root track, so every clip played the stag 100 times too small, standing in the right place: an invisible speck with correct bone positions in the log. `deer_export.py` now bakes centimetres and Blender's own axes into the mesh, the bones and the location keys before writing (identity armature object, `axis_forward Y`, `axis_up Z`). The townspeople's files did not show it because their rigs come in already scaled.
- **Checks (2026-11-22):** nine stills from every side in game light, no shards or flecks (Docs/Deer/stag_views_1.jpg, _2.jpg). HIGH 120 fps film, camera turning and sliding, walker at 3.5 m and runner at 8 m: no ghost copies (Docs/Deer/stag_film_*.jpg). Online, server and two players (`Tools/deer_net.sh`): 13 of 13 steps passed twice (calm at 70 m, looks up at 24 m, herd bolts at 9 m and is 52 m off 6 s later, calms, a real arrow from 20 m takes 8 health through the hurt boxes, herd runs, the kill drops loot and gives XP, a new stag after the test's 25 s, all 71 asleep with everyone in town); both players' machines saw every state, the health and the hidden body. Frame cost (one solo window, two runs each, four stags in view within 15 m, HIGH 1280x720): frame 8.50 / 8.70 ms with, 8.63 / 8.68 without; game thread about +0.2 to +0.4 ms: nothing measurable on the frame.
- **Test switches (never in Shipping):** `jeeb.Test.GoDeer N`, `-DeerLab=X_Y_Yaw` (`Tools/deer_lab.sh stills|film|cost`), `-JeebDeerTest -DeerTestPlayers=2 -DeerRespawn=25` (`Tools/deer_net.sh`), `jeeb.Deer.MeshYaw`. `jeeb.Deer 0` (any build, server) leaves the animals out.
- **Open:** not compiled for Shipping or cooked (package-reviewer before the next package: `/Game/Animals` is loaded by name); no sound; no does or fawns; they do not see through trees any worse than in the open (no line-of-sight test); a stag standing still at 16-30 m stares for as long as you stand there; no collision with players (you can walk through one); the knight's melee reaches them only if he gets close, which their speed makes rare; nothing eats the venison yet (no cooking); the health bar hangs inside the antlers.

## The wizard's flying broom (2026-11-22, agent broom)
- Code: `KnightCharacterBroom.cpp` (ride, drain, bar size, spell-cost hook, orb drain, tests, `UKnightMovement::PhysFlying`), `Broom.cpp/.h` (sheets, Silver Point stall `ABroomStall`), `JeebBroomData.h`. Data: `Content/Data/Brooms.csv` (items 930 + row; never reorder), `Content/Data/MagicCosts.csv`, Tuning.ini `[Broom]`, `[Wizard] MagicPerLevel`. Quest `Broom` (Hazel Fernwick, Oak Hill plaza): 4 logs, 2 hides, reward Hazel's Old Broom.
- Server owns it: `ServerToggleBroom` (T for a wizard; knights' T still calls the horse), `bOnBroom`/`bBroomEmpty`/`BroomRow` replicate to everyone (`IsOnBroom()` for the later double-arrow rule). Flight is MOVE_Flying with our own PhysFlying inside saved moves (Space = bWantsRise, Ctrl = bWantsLetGo, both already in the compressed flags), ceiling by `JeebTerrain::HeightAt`. Empty: halts at BroomStop, drifts at BroomDrift, lands into walking, the server sees the mode change and ends the ride.
- Model: `Art/Props/Broom.glb` (Meshy meshy-6-lite image-to-3d from the CC0 community "Broomstick" cover, 15 credits; a 5-credit text preview was unusable), `/Game/Props/SM_Broom` via `Tools/BuildProps.ps1 Broom`. Placed at his Hips bone each frame; the seated (riding) pose is raised `jeeb.Broom.Raise` instead of the horse's saddle height.
- The orb guard: `GuardActive()` refuses a wizard with under BroomMinMagic (under 0.5 while already up); the server drains MagicCosts.csv GuardDrain while bBlocking.
- Tests: `Tools/broom_net.sh` (server + two wizard clients), `-BroomShot[=fly]` single player for pictures and films.
## PvP zones, fall damage, full camera pitch, mounted-target arrows (2026-11-23, agent `pvpfall`)
- **PvP zones** (`PvpZones.h/.cpp`, `Content/Data/PvpZones.csv`: Name, Title, X0, Y0, X1, Y1 in metres, SignEvery; X north, Y east): `bool IsInPvpZone(const FVector&)` (global; other systems call it) and `JeebPvp::MayHurt(From, To)` (both players inside, or MoveLab). The one server gate is `AKnightCharacter::Hurt` (any blow, arrow, bolt or burn from another `AKnightCharacter` is dropped unless allowed); the melee hit loop and `AProjectile` skip knights that may not be hurt, so no hitstun or knockdown either. First zone "Snowy North": X 840 m northward (the snow lies solid from about X 840 m; measured with the test's snow scan). Signs: `APvpSigns` (`PvpSigns.cpp`, players only, every 60 m 3 m outside the line, facing out, three instanced cubes: dark post, red board, white cross; skipped over water, off the map and inside other zones). HUD: `KnightHUDPvp.cpp` (red `TitleBanner` through `TitleTint`, "PVP ZONE" under it, the crossed-swords icon top middle while inside).
- **Fall damage** (`KnightCharacterFall.cpp`, `Tuning.ini [Pvp]`): server only, from where he last stood (`FallPeakZ` follows him on the ground) to where he lands walking; the first `FallSafeHeight` 450 cm is free, then `FallDamagePerMetre` 8, at most `FallMaxDamage`; water (swim mode, or `FallWaterDepth` 90 cm at the landing), a horse, a ladder or wall and teleports cost nothing. Measured online: 3 m 0, 8 m 28, 9 m into deep water 0.
- **Camera**: `ViewPitchMin/Max` -89/89 (`TickHousekeeping`); the grass clamp (`CamOverGrass`) keeps the camera 105 cm above the ground.
- **Arrow bonus**: `AKnightCharacter::IsFastMounted()` = `IsRushing() || bFastHorse || bOnBroom` (the horse and broom work set the last two on the server); `AProjectile` doubles arrow (not bolt, not rock) damage to such a target (`ArrowMountedMul`). Measured: 17 against 9.
- **Test**: `bash Tools/pvp_net.sh` (server + two players, `PvpTest.cpp`, takes all three slots through `uqueue.sh solo`): 15 PASS. Sign pictures without a net run: `-ShotEyes` on a sign's line (eye height is over the ground), e.g. `82900_<Y0+6000k>_170_0_-4` for zone X 840 m.
- **Open**: signs are plain cubes (no text, no skull); the zone is only the snowy range, PvP 2 and the Birch Grove carve-out are not in the CSV; no death-sack rules or immunity on entering yet (Roadmap "PvP").

Login with no class asked for uses `SavedClass`. `JeebItems::ItemClass` + `CanEquip` refuse other classes' gear (weapons/book from Kinds.csv `Class`, armour by style, shield/arrows knight, rocks troll, brooms wizard). `-TestOldSaveFormat` (non-Shipping) writes saves in the old layout.

## Troll holds, throw, club blows and armour fit (2026-11-24, agent `trollfable`)
Owner's list (pictures pt 6-10): hand too close to his face when aiming a rock, the rock not in his hand at the wind-up, the bag shattered, not in his hand and too small, the sword hold "makes no sense", the club swings, chest armour sinking into his back when he runs, boot tops too wide, helmet and shoulders cracked and sunk.
- **Where his fists are (`FKnightAnimator::TrollFistWorld`, KnightAnimatorTrollThrow.cpp):** the sack and the rock in hand used to sit a little past our wrist joint, but his drawn hand runs 40 to 50 cm on from there (the rock rode on his wrist, which the old wind-up held over his shoulder). They now sit in the hole of the drawn fist, read off his own mesh's hand bone (`TrollComp`), not our bones: his mesh fits our bind pose only to 13 cm and that miss is carried to the hands (placed from our bones the sack hung a hand's length off his fingers). The hole is `FClipLib::TrollFistR` (the left hand's is its mirror). The rock sits `jeeb.Troll.RockOut` (12 cm) out of the hole toward the thumb side so it shows between his fingers, and is drawn `RockHandScale` (2) times its 13 cm model; the sack `RockSackScale` (1.4) times its 42 cm. Log `JEEBROCK hands` (with `-JeebShot` or `-RockLog`, 1 s into a wind-up).
- **The wind-up and throw (`TrollThrow`):** the wind-up holds the upper arm out to his right at shoulder height with the forearm and hand lying back nearly level (`TrollWindHand`), so the rock is out to the right of and behind his head; the hand is turned to lie along the forearm (it stood up on end before). The coil is shared along three spine bones with a small side tip, and the head turns back so he keeps looking where he throws. The throw goes round the shoulder on an arc. The phase, its time and charge are latched (`TrollThrowMode/T/C`): before, the pose's clock went back to 0 when the throw ended while the pose was still fading, so every throw played a second time, faintly.
- **The sack arm (`TrollSackHand`, eased by `TrollSackW`):** he carries it: forearm bent forward, fist knuckles down, the wrist never lower than 1.26 m over his feet (running, he leans and it dragged on the floor).
- **The sack's model:** Meshy's sack is 6000 loose triangles in 465 bits, flat shaded, some facing in: it read as a net of black holes. `troll_rocks_export.py` welds it (3 mm), turns its faces outwards and smooth-shades it; `MI_RockSack` is two-sided. It reads as a whole burlap sack now; a cleaner model would still be better up close.
- **Club blows (`TrollSwingOneHand`, KnightAnimatorTroll.cpp):** the knight's sword cuts bent him double to the floor. His own one-handed blows now, standing up, on the same Jab1/Jab2 timing rows (hit boxes ride the blade bone as before): blow 1 an overhand chop from over the right shoulder in across his middle line to low front left, blow 2 a level backhand at his chest height (about 1.3 m) from his left out to his right; he steps his weight in (hips 36 cm forward and 10 down, both legs bent to keep the feet planted), the trunk coils, the head counter-turns, the left arm balances, `TrollClubClear` keeps the club off belly and floor. Measured on the still sparring knight (`-ShotMove=jab -LabKnight=<cm>`, logs `JEEBTROLLTIP` and `JEEBDEAL`): the head reaches 2.2 m and 2.1 m ahead of his middle and both blows land at 1.7, 2.0 and 2.4 m. The knight's cuts on him carried the whole club 2.3 to 3 m out: longer, but they passed clean over a knight 2 m away (no hit). `-TrollKnightSwing` brings back the knight's cuts, `-TrollOldSwing` the 2026-11-21 two-handed blows. The smash, dash and block are unchanged.
- **Broadsword in his hand:** a knight-sized weapon in his fist is drawn `TrollWeaponScale` (1.5) bigger about its grip (`RebuildHeld`; looks only, reach and hits unchanged), he holds it in one hand (no left hand hunting for the grip: `GreatGripLine` skips the troll) and swings it with the club's blows.
- **Armour fit (`Tools/troll_armour_fit.py`, then `Tools/ImportTrollKit.ps1`):** new steps `repair()` (welds each piece: Meshy's pieces are loose triangles, the chest 16000 in 595 bits, so every per-point push tore them apart; smooth shading below 50 degrees, the helmet left flat), `taper_top()` (boots: from 42% of the way up the shin the boot is drawn in to the calf, x0.71 at the top), `pose_clear()` (the chest's back half is checked in seven test poses, running both ways, hunched, leaning back, arms up, wind-up, twist, against the torso's skin only, and pushed out where it would go under, the amount spread through space so cracks don't open; back points under the skin in the run poses 449 -> 174). Shoulders sit 4 to 8 cm higher and further out. Boots follow the shin and foot only, not the toe bone. Every kit material is two-sided (`troll_kit_import.py`), so a plate facing the wrong way is not a hole.
- **Kit clean-up (2026-11-26, agent trollkit; `Tools/troll_kit_clean.py`):** the helmet was remeshed by Meshy (2026-11-25); the other pieces' Meshy tasks had expired, so chest, shoulder, gauntlet, boot and sack were rebuilt in Blender with no credits. Each old `lo_<piece>.glb` was 6000 to 16000 loose triangles in 450 to 1100 bits with 90% of the edges open. Steps: weld; every triangle given 1.4 cm thickness (Solidify) because Blender's voxel remesh turned open loose triangles into a lattice; voxel remesh at 0.008 (one closed surface, no open edges, one bit); six smooth/unsmooth passes; stray blobs under 3000 faces dropped; decimated to a sensible count (chest 14000, shoulder 10000, boot 8000, gauntlet 8000, sack 6000, one closed manifold each); UVs made on a heavily smoothed copy of the same mesh and laid on the real one (a smart project straight on the lumpy surface made 1000s of tiny islands and a speckled normal map); base colour and metal/roughness baked from the old model, the tangent normal map baked from the fine voxel surface (not from the old shards, whose faces baked in as noise) and eased to 60% toward flat. The result is written over `Art/TrollKit/Lo/lo_<piece>.glb` in the layout the fit reads (the old files kept in `Saved/TrollKit/Old`, not in git); `troll_armour_fit.py` then gives the skin weights from his body as before, so nothing about weights changed. Run: `blender -b -P Tools/troll_kit_clean.py -- Art/TrollKit/Lo chest,shoulder,boot,gauntlet,sack`, then `Tools/ImportTrollKit.ps1` (it now passes `-Multiprocess -LiveCoding=false -abslog`: without them the import hung at startup while another build ran). Also fixed: a comma lost inside a comment in the helmet's `PIECES` line broke the fit script. `troll_rocks_export.py` skips the sack's turn-faces-outward step when the sack is already closed. Checked in game (MoveLab, `-PlayAs=Troll`, his starting Tin kit): stills from the front, back, both sides, head, shoulders, chest, gauntlets and boots, plus 120 fps films of a run and a sprint and the club blow from three sides: whole, no cracks, flecks, see-through faces or ghost copies; chest does not sink in the run (same fit numbers as before). Boards: `Docs/Troll/KitClean_models_before_after.jpg`, `KitClean_game_before_after.jpg`. Open: the sack was not framed in game; the fur and plate are a little softer than the old crisp shards; the kit's overall fit (big gauntlet cuffs, boot raggedness at the sole) is the old fit, not changed.
- **Test films in one run:** console variable `jeeb.Test.TrollDo <name>` runs a test move on your own troll when its value changes (jab, jab1, bigjab, rockhold, rockthrow<hundredths>, rockon, rockoff, block, run<s>, sprint<s>, clubsprint, kind<n>, any `-SuitDo` name; `!x` suffix to repeat a value), so `-ShotEyesSet=0:jeeb.Test.TrollDo=jab;1:...` films a different move from each `-ShotEyes` eye; `-ShotEyesFilmEvery=N` keeps every Nth film frame. `JEEBDEAL` logs every blow that lands in `-JeebShot` runs. MoveLab: the gear wall stands at X -700 and the weapon board at about X 400, so film from (-600,-1500) looking +X.

## Mountain snowstorms (2026-11-26, agent `storm`; Docs/ThursdayList.md 14)
- One weather per game day (midnight to midnight on the server clock, 1200 s; the sun's loop is 2 x 600 s, noon at clock 0): `JeebOpen::Snowing(serverSeconds)` (OpenWorld.cpp) returns 1 (storm) or 0 (blue skies) from a hash of the day number against `[Weather] StormChance` (0.5), eased over `BlendSeconds` (120) centred on midnight. It replaces the 210 s slots. The LOOK tab's sun-speed slider is local only and doesn't move it. `JeebOpen::StormAlt(Z)` gates by the camera's world height (`AltFromM` 60 to `AltToM` 80 m; the range reaches 100 m).
- `AOpenWorld::StormNow` (SnowNow x height gate, per machine's camera) drives: the flakes (`AOpenWorld::Tick`: also still needs snow lying round the camera; flake size 1.8 now), and `ADayNight::Storm` (eased, at most 2 s): grey haze (`FogGrey`), fog start 0, height falloff `FogFalloff` 0.02, directional glow off, density `FogDensity` (1.0) in `AKnightGameState`'s fog line, cloud cover to 2.6 (solid), saturation -35%, exposure -0.3. Log `JEEBSTORM` (non-Shipping, every 4 s in a storm). Test: `jeeb.Weather.Force` (replaced `jeeb.Snow.Force`; `jeeb.Snow.Low` removed). Lab: `Tools/storm_lab.sh`.
- The sky is not fogged (FogCutoffDistance 400000 keeps it clear, deliberately since 2026-10-30), so the storm sky is the cloud layer at 2.6 cover.

## House loot and lockpicking (2026-11-26, agent loot; HouseLoot.h, Docs/LootPlan.md)
- The chests, cupboards and crates already in the ten walk-in Oak Hill rooms (`Interior_<PlotId>.csv`: SM_Int_Chest, SM_Chest_001, SM_Closet_001, SM_Crate*, SM_Box_00x, SM_Int_PotionCrate; 32 in all) are containers. `AHouseInterior::Build` registers them on the server and every client from the same rows; lock level and tier come from a CRC of "<PlotId>#<n>", so nothing replicates. Requests name a container by its world spot.
- Lockpicking was already a skill (number 9, used by the cottages' doors), so no new skill was added; containers train it.
- E: unlocked containers are searched at once (EInvOp::BoxSearch); locked ones start a 1.6 s pick (EInvOp::BoxPick, the server's timer, `AKnightCharacter::FinishBoxPick`, the bar from `ClientLockPick`). Chance as the cottages: clamp(0.35 + 0.06 x (level - lock), 0.35, 0.95). A slip may be heard by the nearest townsperson within 12 m (halved crouched, down to a quarter at Stealth 99): the container is barred to that player for 90 s and stealth-sensing NPCs in range are alerted.
- Loot from `Content/Data/HouseLoot.csv` (tiers 0-3 by lock: 0 open, 1-14, 15-39, 40+); refill per container after `[Loot] LootRefillSeconds` (600). Server checks: container exists, reach (LootReach + 60), climbing lock-out, level, empty, barred, one request a second, a free pack slot (what does not fit is left behind).
- Tests: `Tools/loot_net.sh` (server + two players, JEEBLOOTTEST / JEEBLOOT / JEEBLOOTDO), `-LootShot` pictures.
## Distinct arrows (2026-11-26, agent `arrows`; Docs/ThursdayList.md item 13)
- `Content/Data/Arrows.csv`, seven rows found by position (id 900 + row; `JeebItems::FArrowRow`, `ArrowRow`, `ArrowRowOf`, `EArrow`): 0 Wooden (was tin), 1 Broadhead (iron), 2 Bodkin (silver), 3 Heavy (gold), 4 retired (emerald: `ReplaceRetired` turns a save's into Eagle when it loads), 5 Eagle (diamond), 6 Fire. Damage: the row's `Steps` + half the bow tier's (`WeaponDamage`); the Archery level still adds level / 5 and sets the draw time, and now a small random stray (`[Archery] SpreadAt1` 1.2 degrees at level 1, none from `SpreadGoneAt` 60, half on a full draw; server, `FireArrow`).
- Jobs, all on the server in `AProjectile` (hit block): `AnimalMul` against `ADeer`/`AEagle`; `Pierce` lerps the armour's ZoneTake toward 1 (`AKnightCharacter::Hurt`'s new `Pierce` argument; `UHealthComponent::PierceNext` for foes); `Knock` = `TakeReact` (0.35 s stun, push) on knights in a PvP zone, `LaunchCharacter` on other characters; `Burn` total over 3 ticks (`Burn(Target, Total)`); `SpeedMul` on launch.
- Making: `AKnightCharacter::SmithArrowsNow(Row)` (the Smith op with A -1, B the row): the row's Smithing `Level` and up to three materials; the smithy's Arrows column has one card per arrow (`ArrowKindId` order: wooden, broadhead, bodkin, heavy, fire, eagle) with `ArrowJob`. Anyone fires any arrow. Store: wooden or broadhead always, sometimes bodkin, heavy or fire (`Shop`). Starting kit (new characters and the wake-up kit after dying): 99 wooden arrows only (2026-11-26, checks2); `UnlimitedArrows` tops up wooden only; saves are untouched.
- Models: no Meshy credits spent (13 left; a head each would not have fitted). `Tools/BuildArrows.ps1`: `arrow_heads.py` (Blender) cuts the Meshy arrow's head off the shared shaft and builds each head in the item style (whittled point, slim square bodkin needle, blunt ogive with a collar, small blade with a pitch bundle, long leaf blade with 40% larger swept fletching for the eagle), red vertex mask on metal so `MI_Arrow` tints it; the broadhead keeps the Meshy head (`SM_Arrow`). `arrow_import.py` makes `/Game/Props/SM_Arrow_<Cheap|Bodkin|Heavy|Fire|Eagle>`. `JeebProps::ArrowMesh(Id)` picks by `Model`; flying, nocked; the nocked arrow (drawn bow, `UpdateBowBits`, follows the replicated `LookArrows` for other players) and, since 2026-11-26 (agent/nock), the five arrows in the quiver (`UpdateQuiver`) use the picked kind's own model, X-scaled 0.8 so the head stays inside the tube (`SM_ArrowBack` is now only the fallback). Icons and dropped bundles draw each head's outline.
- Tests: `bash Tools/arrows_net.sh` (`ArrowTest.cpp`, `-JeebArrowTest`; rides on `APvpSigns`' tick). Pictures: `-ArrowLineup=X_Y` (a stack of every arrow, a post with one of each stuck in it, one of each flying across 8 m on every 0.6 s).
## Arrow smear film, invisible-player cost, first-drink hitch (2026-11-26, agent `checks2`)
- Arrow film (`Tools/arrow_lab.sh film`, `-ArrowFilmCam`): all six arrows are single crisp copies at HIGH 120 fps with a moving camera; no motion-vector change needed. The white line is the designed streak (`AProjectile::TickTrail`).
- Invisible players' frame cost (HIGH, 1080p, meadow view at -3000,8000, solo window; `jeeb.Test.InvisCrowd`): 0 knights 7.08 ms frame / 6.11 GPU; 1 knight 7.34 / 6.32; 4 knights 7.76 / 6.67; back to 0 7.13 / 6.21. About +0.15 ms per invisible character (draws +45 / +170, triangles +0.25 M / +1.0 M for 4 knights), so no cheaper path is needed.
- First-drink hitch: the 200-350 ms first measured was the test's own camera move (grass regrowing at the first eye), not the drink. Properly measured (same-view second eye): 37 ms without precache (ShowInvisible took 28 ms: the sync LoadObject of M_Invisible), 18-23 ms with it. Fix kept: `AKnightCharacter::TickInvisible` loads and holds the material and shows it for one frame 3 s after a local knight ticks (the swap-in/out costs one 25-30 ms frame then). Editor numbers only; a cooked build's PSO behaviour was not measured.

## Fish sizes in the pack (2026-11-26, agent fishsize; FishSizeTest.cpp, Docs/FishingPlan.md)

`FInvSlot::Size` (tenths of an inch, 0 = none) rides with a trout, salmon or bass through `AddInvItem(Id, Count, Size)`, `CanAdd`, `PutInto` (bank), `TakeEverything`, `ALootDrop` (drops, sacks, `TakeOne`/`TakeAll`) and `UJeebSave` (`SlotSizes`, `BankSizes`, `FJeebClassPack::SlotSizes`; no version bump, old saves read as zero). A sized slot never stacks (Count 1) and never merges; `RemoveItems` takes plain stacks first. `AResourceNode::YieldSizeTenths` (fishing overrides) hands the size to `FinishGathering`. Tooltip: `ItemTip(Id, Size)`; helpers `JeebProps::FishSpecies/FishSizeText/IsBoardFish`. Server decides all; the client only reads the replicated slot. Test: `Tools/fishsize_net.sh` (`-FishSizeNetTest` server, `-FishSizeDo` client; the client's bite clicks the reel for itself).
